# Changelog

## Unreleased

- **Changed (SOAK-05): the server launchers set the recommended native memory settings.** `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`, `-XX:MaxDirectMemorySize=256m` and `-XX:TrimNativeHeapInterval=60000` (resident memory about `-Xmx` plus 0.4 GB instead of plus 0.9 to 1 GB in the soak) are now set by `drishti-stack` for every server it starts, the ECS server image and entrypoint, `deploy/docker/server.Dockerfile` and `compose.yaml`, and the IntelliJ run configurations; `tools/windows/start-server.ps1` sets the two JVM flags (`MALLOC_ARENA_MAX` is glibc's). Java 21 accepts both flags. `tools/test_launcher_memory.py`; PERFORMANCE (*Resident memory*), OPERATIONS sections 4 and 7.2.
- **Fixed (QA3B-07): after a burst of data-load signals the BI layers settle.** 2,976 signals in rounds (8 clients, 60 dates) rebuilt each BI table 24 to 25 times per date, four rebuilds failed on a race (`DRS-9027 … part.parquet.….tmp is gone`, `cannot write the layer's metadata`), and 20 minutes later the server was at 1,000 to 1,600 % CPU with 588 full GCs. Each signal cleared the date's partitions (deleting their folders, under builds writing into them) and queued a rebuild, so every round rebuilt every date again; the latest's build and a date's build of one partition took different locks. Now a signal for a date whose stored partition was built from the data the connector holds now (its data version matches) changes nothing; a waiting rebuild runs only when the data changed since the one before it; two tables' background rebuilds run at once in the whole server; an invalidation removes a partition's metadata only (the Parquet file stays until a build replaces it, so a page reading it finishes and a build keeps its temporary file); and every build of a partition (on demand, background, a table's latest) checks, writes and records it under the partition's one lock, into a file of its own (`part-<id>.parquet`, named by the metadata; the replaced one goes ten minutes later), so a query reading the previous file finishes on it; a signal rebuilds in the background only the dates a page has read (a backfill's other dates are built when asked). Measured on a 3 GB server over the 66-table, 60-day demo lakes, 3,000 signals in 25 rounds (8 clients) with three model-query readers: 6,621 queries, none failed, slowest 1.7 s; no DRS-9027; CPU 500 to 950 % during the burst, 28 % 12 s after the last signal and 1 to 2 % from then on (7 minutes watched), no full GC, RSS 1.7 GB. With the coalescing alone (before the last two changes) the same burst left the server at about 1,000 % CPU and 587 full GCs seven minutes after the last signal, still reading every signalled date of twelve tables from the documents. An `after-load` dataset refresh has one running and one waiting with every signalled date, each once, skipping a date whose layers hold the data held now. `LoadBurstTest` (3,000 signals with three readers: none rebuilt for unchanged data, one more read for a date changed mid-burst, no failure without data versions), `DatasetRefresherTest`; DATA_LOADS (*On ready*).
- **Fixed (QA3B-12): `load-iceberg.sh` and `load-iceberg.ps1` end a failed load with one line, not a Java stack trace.** A REST catalog whose bucket was missing ended in `Exception in thread "main" java.io.UncheckedIOException … NoSuchBucketException` and eight frames. The loader now prints one line and exits 1: a missing bucket names the bucket (from `--warehouse` or the root), the endpoint and what to check; an unreachable catalog or object store names it; anything else is `iceberg: the load failed: <why>` (`-Ddrishti.loader.trace=true` adds the trace). The scripts add one line of their own, remove their input file, and the PowerShell twin no longer throws an error record on top. `IcebergLoaderFailureTest`, `tools/test_load_scripts.py`; ICEBERG_CONNECTOR section 6.
- **Fixed (QA3B-05): a Cassandra connector that cannot connect no longer leaks a driver session at every retry.** The session still connecting was left to close "when it connects", which a refusing cluster never does: each restart by the retry board added one more session with its own threads and reconnect WARNs (`too many session instances: 9 active`), and they went on after the connector file was deleted. A close (a retry, a stop, a deleted file) now cancels the session being built, so the driver stops reconnecting and closes it, and the connector's refresh task is stopped too. `CassandraConnectFailureTest` counts the driver's sessions and threads over five restarts against a closed port; CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR section on diagnosing.
- **Fixed (QA3B-11): a Cassandra TLS refusal is named in Health.** `DOWN: connecting to Cassandra at …` read the same for a closed port and a TLS refusal; the cause was only in the driver's WARN. While the driver retries, the connector opens one connection of its own to the first contact point (at most every 30 s) and says what it found: no connection, the node asks for a client certificate, the client certificate refused, the node's certificate not trusted or not naming the host, `tls.enabled` against a plain port, or a node that requires TLS from a connector without it, each with the `tls.*` settings to set. `CassandraConnectFailureTest` (local TLS servers), `CassandraTlsTest` (Cassandra 5 with mutual TLS); CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR.
- **Fixed (QA3B-03, QA3B-04): a moved Iceberg table is "degraded" in Admin → Connectors with the `relocate` to copy, and `relocate` reads plain folders.** A domain written by `load-iceberg.sh --engine native`, moved and served through a connector file was DEGRADED in Health, but Admin → Connectors showed it as *running* (its badge read only DOWN and warnings), so the page said UP and hid the fix; each read answered `502 DRS-1003` with a full stack trace. `IcebergMovedTableTest` passed because it checked the plugin's own health, not the page. Now the badge is **degraded** with the health beside it; tables moved together are one sentence with one pair, given in the plain form to copy (`relocate: '/data/ice1 -> /data/ice2'`); the 502 says the table names files that are not there (no folder in it) and the log has one line. `relocate` accepts plain folders, Windows folders and shares, `file:` URIs in any spelling and `s3a://` for `s3://` (both sides are read into the metadata's `file:` form; whole folders only), says when its old folder holds nothing or its new folder lacks the files, and a pair that cannot be read fails the start naming it. `RelocationTest`, `IcebergMovedLoaderTableTest`, `IcebergMovedConnectorFileTest`, `ConnectorBadgeTest`; ICEBERG_CONNECTOR sections 14 and 15, CONNECTOR_FILES.
- **Design: fewer duplicate BI runs** (BI_RUNS.md 8.3a, product owner 2026-10-20): normalise questions before hashing, offer an existing result on older data before a heavy run, suggest a rollup to the model owner for heavy questions asked again and again (in 2.6.7), with reuse and join rates in Admin → Runs.

- **Design: BI runs (release 2.6.7).** New [BI_RUNS.md](docs/architecture/BI_RUNS.md), the product owner's decisions of 2026-10-20: super scale is the architecture at every data size; every BI query goes through one bounded asynchronous pipeline (submit, plan with a metadata-only estimate, admission, run, store, notify), drawn inline within about 2 s or shown as a visible job in a new Runs tab of the BI Workspace with a count on the bell; three tiers (interactive, known heavy from products and rollups, ad-hoc heavy as a background run); results stored as Parquet under the data folder, keyed by fingerprint, data version and security signature, re-masked or recomputed per recipient when shared; background class caps in admission, cancellation, restart and cluster rules; settings in `config/runs/runs.yaml`; codes DRS-9080 to DRS-9089; build steps R1 to R9 for two builders. The release order in IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN and BI_DESIGN 12.2 (2.6.6, 2.6.7, 2.7.0, 2.8.0, the real-life QA, 2.9.0, 3.0.0), SUPER_SCALE and the docs index follow.
- **Design: one Drishti database and the write-back decisions of 2026-10-20.** New [DATABASE.md](docs/architecture/DATABASE.md) (2.7.0 item 0): all Drishti-owned relational state in one database (SQLite by default, PostgreSQL long term), no migrations (two complete schema files, a parity test, a version stamp, `drishti.py db copy`), configured in `config/db/database.yaml`. [WRITE_BACK.md](docs/architecture/WRITE_BACK.md): a durable shared edit log (`drishti_edit_lane`, `drishti_edit_log`) fenced by epoch, with SQLite, PostgreSQL, S3 and edit-primary fallbacks (`edit.log: auto`); the lane owner does every write, the lake is never written per edit, source commands go through the outbox; connector `writes:`; off by default at three levels and controlled by roles; W0 and cluster acceptance tests. IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN, BI_DESIGN 12.2 wave W and the docs index follow.
- **Fixed (QA3A-05, 07, 08): `build_demo_lakes.py` tells the server you name, once, says what it is waiting for, and ends with lines that fit the run.** `--server URL` (and `--token-file`, else `DRISHTI_TOKEN`) tells that server once at the end, one data-load signal per table for its newest date (`lake demo --signal-once`), never one per table and day; by default no server is told and the closing lines say to restart it or wait for its refresh. The heartbeat says `writing <table>` only while files land, else `nothing written for N s`; `lake demo` counts signals the server **refused** apart from those it **did not answer** in time (the token hint only for 401/403) and reports the build and the signals' time apart. After `--files-only --system calypso` the closing lines name a CALYPSO trade from your newest file and say no restart is needed. `test_build_demo_lakes.py`, `tools/lake/test_demo.py`; TOOLS 4.9, QA_ON_WINDOWS_WITH_CUSTOM_DATA 3.0.
- **Fixed (QA3A-02, 03, 04, 06): a change made through one console worker shows on every worker at once, in every browser of the user.** With `server.workers=4`, a pack choice came back as the old one on 17 of the next 20 pages, a saved layout flipped back and forth for a minute, a disabled user kept working for 10 s on the console the administrator used, and a second browser showed the old theme for 30 s: each worker kept its own copy and only the worker that made the change forgot its own. Now the change writes the user's stamp (one small file per user under `<console data>/user-stamps`; one for everyone when an administrator switches a pack), and every worker checks it (one `stat`) before reusing a copy of the user's settings, packs, view layouts, saved pivots, BI powers or session check. The browser cookie stamp stays as the fast path. The copies now live 10 s (`ui.user_cache_seconds`; settings were 30 s, packs, layouts and pivots 60 s), which bounds other consoles behind one address that share no data folder. `core/user_stamps.py`; `test_user_stamps_workers.py`; CONFIGURATION (`server.workers`, `ui.user_cache_seconds`, `auth.recheck_seconds`), OPERATIONS, USER_MANAGEMENT.
- **Fixed (QA3A-01, P1): a storm of data-load signals no longer drives the server out of memory.** A 60-day `--only` load sent 60 signals; each dropped the trade connectors' caches, cleared the BI layers and started a full read of every trade document beside the ones running, and reads whose caller had timed out stayed queued for a document-read slot (880,000 parked threads, then `OutOfMemoryError`). Now: signals for a connector refreshed less than `drishti.loads.refresh-window` (5 s) ago are merged into one more refresh at the end of the window (at most one running and one waiting per connector; the merged signal still looks for the newest table version); a purge of grouped pages keeps the build that runs and builds once more after it (`rebuild-delay`, 5 s, on disk), never two full reads at once; BI layer rebuilds run one date at a time per table, each date once; and the Delta, Iceberg, JDBC, DuckDB and MongoDB read slots are bounded (`max-queued-reads`, 4096 for documents) and drop a read whose caller has stopped waiting (`ReadSlots`, `ReadDeadline`). `AbandonedReadsTest`, `DerivedSourcePluginTest`, `QueryEngineTest`, `LoadsApiTest`; DATA_LOADS, DELTA_CONNECTOR, ICEBERG_CONNECTOR, CONFIGURATION.
- **Fixed: a BI table rebuilt on Windows failed with DRS-9027** (`part.parquet.tmp -> part.parquet`): Windows cannot replace a file anything holds open (a query reading the previous version, a virus scanner, the indexer). The new file is written under a name of its own (two builds never share one) and moved over the old one, tried again for about 5 s while it is held; past that the message says what held it. `FileSwapTest`. Found on the product owner's Windows machine.
- **Fixed: the server makes the folder `java.io.tmpdir` names when it is missing** (an IDE run configuration passes `-Djava.io.tmpdir=<data>/tmp/server` but cannot make it; on a fresh clone DuckDB, RocksDB and CatBoost then failed to unpack). The console never falls back to the system temp folder: without a setting its base is `<data dir>/tmp/console`, however it is started. `JvmTempDirTest`, `test_tmpdir.py`.
- **Fixed: `build_demo_lakes.py` no longer signals a running server for every table and day** (3,960 signals for 66 tables over 60 days, each making the server read the data again; QA3A-01/05). Restart the server after a build, or let it pick the tables up at its next refresh.
- **Fixed (SOAK-10): the soak configures only what its lakes hold.** Its servers retried `collateral-store`, `legal-store`,
  `market-store` and `risk-store` (about 8 WARN attempts each) and warned `deprecated: connector 'trade-…' is defined in the server's own
  configuration` because of the soak's own `--drishti.sources.connectors.*.enabled=false` flags. No server loads market-risk any more,
  and before each start the soak writes a connector file (the pack's template with `enabled: "false"`) for every Delta connector whose
  tables the node's lake lacks, the documented way; the flags are gone. `net_soak.connector_files`; test_net_soak; DRISHTI_NET
  section 17.1, TOOLS.
- **Fixed (SOAK-09): the soak's mask check covers something.** It checked 35 distinct histories, and no viewer trade history had a
  masked field (the default `drishti.security.redact` names `trader`, which the synthetic trades do not hold). The soak's servers now
  mask `PRICE`, `TRADER_ID` and `NAME`; a viewer's trade history must show `PRICE` and a counterparty's `NAME` as a masked column, the
  mask on every row and never a value in a diff, or the answer is wrong. The checked set holds `--history-sample` (40) trades per
  system (MUREX ones peer-only from a and b) and 20 counterparties, each asked as administrator and viewer with two of ten ranges (about
  140 distinct histories, 280 requests); the verdict's `masksExercised` fails unless the reference masks them in every viewer history.
  `soak_check.mask_missing`; test_net_soak; DRISHTI_NET section 17.1, TOOLS.
- **Fixed (SOAK-08): the soak keeps every wrong answer.** It kept five per kind, with no time for the others and the body cut at 600
  characters before `partial` and `problems`, which were what made them wrong. `soak.json`'s `wrong` now has the count per kind of
  request and per rule that failed (`byKind`, also in `soak-report.txt`), every wrong answer's time, node, request and rule (`all`), and
  the first 200 with the status, `partial` and `problems` whole, the rest of the body cut and a compact diff against the reference
  (`samples`). `soak_check.judge_history_why`, `WrongLog`; test_net_soak; DRISHTI_NET section 17.1, TOOLS.
- **Fixed (SOAK-07): the soak measures the console's memory.** It sampled the uvicorn parent alone (one thread, 50.7 MB on all 120
  samples of the 2026-10-20 soak); `tools/bench/net_soak.py` now samples the console's whole process tree from `/proc`: resident memory,
  threads and open files summed over the parent and its workers, `processes`, and each worker's own figures (`workers`), so the console's
  memory slope means something. test_net_soak; DRISHTI_NET section 17.1, TOOLS.
- **Fixed (SOAK-04): the soak's memory check tells a leak from a node taking over the load.** `tools/bench/net_soak.py` judges each
  process by its steady slope: only the samples outside the kill windows and `--settle` seconds (600) after each, fitted with a level per
  steady span, so a step when the console moves sessions to a node at a failover is not growth. The whole-run slope is reported beside
  it (`wholeRunSlopeMbPerHour`, `steadySpans`). On the 2026-10-20 soak's b (128 to 367 MB at c's kill, flat after) the whole-run slope
  is +166 MB/h and the steady one 0. `soak_check.memory_trend`; test_net_soak; DRISHTI_NET section 17.1, TOOLS.
- **Fixed (QA-OPEN-01): `tools/testdata/make_testdata.py` writes the same bytes on every Python.** 16 counterparty and netting-set
  change files differed in the sixth decimal of walked values between environments (`NET_MTM` 484329001.595759 vs .595758): a netting
  set's `NET_MTM` is the sum of its trades' MTM, and the built-in `sum()` of floats gives a different last bit on Python 3.13 and 3.14
  (597445765.99 vs 597445765.9899999), which the walk of earlier days carries into the sixth decimal. The banking generator's sums
  (`data_results.py`: `amount()`, whole numbers exactly, others by `math.fsum` to the cent; every other float sum by `math.fsum`) and the
  walk's drift (`samplegen/lake.py`) no longer use it; the pack samples are unchanged, data/testdata regenerated (63 files).
  `make_testdata.py --check` passes on 3.14 and 3.13. `tools/testdata/test_deterministic.py`; TOOLS, DEMO_DATA, DEVELOPER_GUIDE.
- **Investigated (SOAK-05): resident memory that creeps while the live heap is flat is the committed heap and glibc's malloc arenas,
  not a native leak.** Two runs under 20 users with Native Memory Tracking: metaspace, code, threads and direct buffers flat; RSS grows
  with G1's committed heap (to `-Xmx`) and, with the default allocator, 149 malloc arenas holding about 700 MB (about 200 MB with
  `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`). PERFORMANCE.md *Resident memory* gives the numbers, the budget (`-Xmx` plus about 1 GB, or 0.4 GB with two
  arenas) and the settings `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2`, `-XX:MaxDirectMemorySize=256m`, `-XX:TrimNativeHeapInterval=60000`.
- **Fixed (SOAK-01): a complete history is no longer marked partial while a node is down.** A node that is down counts only for the
  business dates its last digest said it holds: each source of a digest now carries `earliestDate` beside `latestDate`
  (`SourcePlugin.earliestDate`, given by the Delta and Cassandra connectors), and `NetRouting.downHolders(kind, date)` names a down
  node only for a date inside one of its sources' ranges. A `range=3M` history of a CALYPSO trade or a counterparty asked while the
  MUREX holder is down is complete again, not `partial` with `DRS-5052 … drishti-c (dead): 54 days of the window may be held there`
  naming days before any lake's first day. DRISHTI_NET sections 6 and 7, HISTORY_QUERIES section 12, NET.md, API_GUIDE;
  HistoryPeersTest, NetPeerDownTest.
- **Fixed (SOAK-02): a history through a peer that just died answers `DRS-5052` naming it, never `502 DRS-1003 … ConnectException`.**
  A connection failure to a peer (refused, reset, no route) is that node being down for the read: the next holder is asked, and the dates
  no one answered are `DRS-5052` naming it (`… cannot be reached now: drishti-c (not answering: the connection failed)`), only for the
  dates it may hold; any other failure of a peer is `DRS-5052` too, as views say. A date one peer failed and another answered is no
  longer partial. `DRS-1014` stays for a time-out. The console never sets aside the server that answered for a problem about another
  node: `DRS-505x`, and a `DRS-1003` naming a peer from a 2.6.5 server, are its own answer (`peer_problem`), and `504 DRS-1014` never
  counts; the soak had 43 such set-asides of healthy servers. HISTORY_QUERIES sections 12 and 16, DRISHTI_NET 11.1, NET.md;
  HistoryPeersTest, NetPeerDownTest, test_cluster_failover.
- **Fixed (SOAK-03): a starting node joins Drishti\*net only once it is ready.** The node gossips itself alive on `ApplicationReadyEvent`
  (web server up, connectors started), no longer as soon as its net service is made, 12 to 19 s before its port opened, so peers no
  longer route to a closed port after a restart (`ConnectException`); a peer that refuses the connection passes the read to the next
  holder. The log says `this node joins once the server is ready`, then `joined Drishti*net` after `Tomcat started`. DRISHTI_NET
  sections 5 and 12, NET.md; NetJoinWhenReadyTest (a node held 4 s before its port opens: routed to while closed in 94 polls before,
  0 after).
- **Fixed (SOAK-06): expected refusals about another node are one short log line, not a stack trace each.** `DRS-5052`, `DRS-5053` and
  `DRS-1014` are logged once per change of what they say about a node (`drishti-c (suspect)`, then `(dead)`), then as a count once a
  minute (`request refused 6000 times in the last 60 s naming drishti-c (dead)`), never with a stack trace; unexpected errors keep
  theirs. While a node was down each server wrote about 11 MB of log a minute (68 MB in the soak's six-minute window). NET.md
  troubleshooting, HISTORY_QUERIES section 16; RefusalLogTest (10,000 refusals: 10,000 WARN lines with stack traces before, 2 short
  lines after).
- **Fixed: a theme (or any personal setting) changed on one page could come back as the old one on the next page** when the console runs several worker processes (four on Windows by default): each worker kept the user's settings for 30 s, and a change cleared only the cache of the worker that saved it. A change now stamps the browser (`drishti_settings_at` cookie), and a worker re-reads the settings when its copy is older than the stamp; with four workers, 5 of 24 pages came back in the old theme before the fix and 0 after. `test_settings_workers.py`. Found on the product owner's Windows machine (Green → netting set → SIMM came up Blue).
- **Fixed (QA2-205): the docs say `lake demo` builds 66 tables**, as its summary prints (one per dataset of
  data/testdata/testdata.yaml: 49 generated, 15 from the world-economy and news-and-markets samples, the captured quotes and headlines),
  not 64: GETTING_STARTED (twice), PACKS, DEVELOPER_GUIDE, DEMO_DATA, tutorial 1 (twice), the Windows QA guide, data/testdata/README.
  test_testdata checks every one against the manifest.
- **Fixed (QA2-204): `drishti.py bi statement preview` and `file` say when a table was cut.** Each table that stops at
  `drishti.distribution.statements.max-rows` is printed on a `cut:` line (`cut: Positions: The first 10,000 rows only: …`), from the
  preview's `cut` and from the file's new `X-Statement-Cut` headers (one per cut table, URL-encoded), as the PDF's note says it.
  CLI_GUIDE `bi statement`, STATEMENTS, API_GUIDE; test_cli_bistatement, StatementRowsTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-203): `iceberg-external` names what it looked for when a table folder has no metadata, and says DEGRADED.** Health
  and Admin → Connectors read `qa-ghost: no table qa.ghost: looked in <root>/qa/ghost/metadata for version-hint.text,
  v<N>.metadata.json and a catalog's NNNNN-<uuid>.metadata.json (the folder is empty)` (or what stray files it holds), and a
  connector with a configured table it cannot serve (missing, no id) is `DEGRADED`, no longer `UP (…)` with level ok.
  ICEBERG_EXTERNAL_CONNECTOR section 2; IcebergExternalPluginTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-202): a read during a connector file swap waits for the new connector.** While a connector of the kind is being
  restarted (its file changed, Delta to Cassandra and back, a retry), an entity read (`/entities/…`, views) waits for the new instance,
  up to 30 s as a BI query already did, instead of answering `404 DRS-1002 connector trade-endur is running` for its drain and start
  (3 to 5 s). A start that takes longer answers `503 DRS-1015 connector … is restarting`. CONNECTOR_FILES section 13, API_GUIDE,
  TROUBLESHOOTING; SourceRouterRestartTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-201): the demo's market data is one picture each day.** A day's FX `BID` and `ASK` keep their spread around the moved
  `MID` (it lay outside them on 59 of 60 days); a bond's `YIELD` moves against its `PRICE` by the modified duration, its `Z_SPREAD` with
  the credit share of that move and its `MOD_DURATION` with the yield and the bond's age (all three never changed). Audited and fixed
  the same way: an equity's `PRICE` and a commodity's `FRONT` are the close of their own `OHLC` bar of the day, an index `LEVEL` its
  `HISTORY` close; embedded series end on their day (no future points); an equity's `MARKET_CAP` follows its price; fixings end on the
  day with `LATEST` the newest and `RATE_PCT` = `RATE` × 100; the IR and repo curves of a currency move together (zero rates, quotes,
  discount factors, forwards, `TEN_Y`, `SLOPE_2S_10S`, `OVERNIGHT`; `BUILT_AT` the day's, and `lake demo` moves it with the dates); a
  commodity curve follows its commodity's `FRONT`, an FX forward curve its pair's `MID`. Credit curves and vol surfaces stay as on the
  last day (internally consistent). `tools/testdata/marketdays.py`; data/testdata regenerated (115 MB, no file over 5 MB);
  `tools/testdata/test_market_quotes.py` checks every committed day; data/testdata/README.
- **Added: `lake demo` says how far a table has got**: every ten committed days a line `trading/trade_endur: committed 20/60 days, 90,023 rows so far (through 2026-08-19)`, besides the table's closing line and `build_demo_lakes.py`'s heartbeat.

- **Faster: `lake build` and `lake demo` with the deltalake writer make one commit per business date** (every partition of the date in it; one per partition only with `overwrite-partitions`) and a checkpoint every ten commits. Sixty days of `trade_endur` (five product types) were 300 commits, each replaying the whole log (98 s on Linux, far longer on Windows, where each log file is opened and scanned); now 60 commits with a short log, the same rows.
- **Added: the Drishti\*net soak exercises history queries and runs for hours** (`tools/bench/net_soak.py`, `users.py`, new
  `soak_check.py`): `--history-share` (0.15) of the load asks histories (30 and 60 days, 3M, DIFF, *known at*; trades held only by a peer,
  counterparties, `--equities`), `history` its own line of the latency and error table; the checker compares history answers with the
  reference server row by row (dates, values, changed flags, masked columns, as administrator and as viewer) and, while a node is killed,
  accepts only complete answers or partial/refused ones naming it (`DRS-5052`, or `DRS-1014`), never a silent short answer or `DRS-1013`.
  Every minute each process's memory, heap, open files and threads are sampled, and the report ends with a verdict (no wrong answers,
  errors only in kill windows, p95/p99 per action, memory flat after warm-up) that sets the exit code; `--days`, `--kill-grace`,
  `--sample-every`, `--warmup`, `--max-growth`, `--max-p99`. Unit tests without servers: `tools/bench/test_net_soak.py`. DRISHTI_NET.md
  section 17.1, TOOLS.md.
- **Fixed (QA2-21, round 2): `lake demo` and `make_data.py` write the feeds under the server's data folder.** `lake demo` takes
  `--data-dir`; without it, `DRISHTI_DATA_DIR`, else the folder holding `--lake` when that is a `delta/` folder, else `data/`;
  `make_data.py --data-dir DIR` too. A server on another data folder no longer finds its `file` connector DOWN for want of `feeds/`.
  TOOLS section 4.9; tools/lake/test_demo.py.
- **Fixed (QA2-15): the statement designer's Paper tab is readable at 1400 px.** The tab sits between the Studio's side panels, so its
  two columns now follow the tab's own width (a container query), not the window's: when it is narrow the settings go above the preview,
  and the sheet keeps the tab's width (it was about 130 px) while the four margin boxes share their row instead of running under the
  preview. Page Down and Page Up page the preview wherever the focus is (but in a field), not only after a click on the sheet.
  STATEMENTS section 5; test_bi_wave_d_browser.
- **Fixed (QA2-16): Admin → Health fits a phone.** The external libraries' folder and jar list (and any long path in the summary lines)
  wrap inside the page at 375 px instead of adding 154 px of horizontal scroll. test_admin_health_phone_browser.
- **Fixed (QA1-14, QA1-15, QA1-16): the market history page.** The chart's hover label stays inside the chart and is put back at the
  selected row after a resize or zoom, so narrowing the window no longer leaves it where the wider chart had it (647 px of horizontal
  scroll). The table's numbers read in the chart field's format when the column has none (`21.66`, not `21.657685`); the CLI's headline
  too (`chg +1.09 (+5.25%)  avg 21.29`); a row from the embedded series reads `—` (*not read on this day*) under each part, not `0`; the
  crosshair's date is no longer drawn on the axis over its labels. A day opened from the history (`Enter`, *Open the day*) says *as it
  was, from its history* (not *as it was shared*), with *Back to the history*, and the view's back link is that history, not an earlier
  page's trail. USER_GUIDE, tutorial 21, HISTORY_QUERIES; HistoryServiceTest, test_history_routes, test_history_browser,
  tools/test_historycmd.py.
- **Fixed (QA2-12, QA2-14): a statement for a date with no data says so; a card with no value reads `–`.** A preview or file for a date no
  data covers (`9999-12-31`, `1999-01-01`) has `hint` *No data is held for December 9999 (…): every band is empty. Choose a date the data
  covers.* (also first in the file's notes, and beside the page count in the designer's preview) instead of an empty statement in silence.
  A card whose value is empty (a sum of no rows) reads `–`, as a masked card and a card with no rows do, never blank; a count reads its number.
- **Fixed (QA2-17, QA2-18, QA2-19): bursting says who is skipped and why, and draws what a recipient may see.** A disabled user named in a
  subscription is listed as skipped (*the account is disabled*) instead of missing from the run; a recipient without access is skipped
  with *… may not open the report: it is not shared with them, or their roles do not reach it* instead of `DRS-9001 no item 'bi-…'`; a
  statement table sorted by a measure masked for the recipient is drawn in the query's own order with the note *Not sorted: 'MTM' is masked
  for you …* (never *No rows.*), and the delivery's `notes` say so. STATEMENTS, DISTRIBUTION; StatementApiTest, SubscriptionServiceTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-20): a field parameter in the wrong place is one problem naming it.** A field parameter in a well that cannot take its
  choices is one `DRS-9037` per parameter and well, naming every choice that does not fit (*… can be MTM, NOTIONAL, measures: put it in a
  measure well*), instead of one line per choice. Through `/bi/mq`, `param.metric` among `select.fields` answers `DRS-9164 'param.metric'
  chooses among MTM, NOTIONAL: measures, so it goes in select.measures` instead of `DRS-9152` about its default; a value that is not a
  choice reads *metric is one of its choices [MTM, NOTIONAL]* (not *the fields*). REPORTS, tutorial 20, API_GUIDE, TROUBLESHOOTING;
  FieldParamsApiTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-22): an Iceberg table moved after loading is one clear problem.** Its reads failed inside the connector's caches, and
  each failure was logged by the cache with a full stack trace at every refresh while Health said `UP`. Health now says `DEGRADED:
  cannot read trade: its metadata names files under <old folder> that are not there (…): the table was moved or copied after it was
  written`, with the `relocate: <old> -> <new>` that reads it with `engine: native`; the log has one short line when the problem appears
  or changes and one when it is over. `relocate` given to `engine: library` (which does not read it) is said in the log and in Health
  instead of being ignored silently. The connector's refresh is cancelled when it stops. ICEBERG_CONNECTOR sections 14 and 15;
  IcebergMovedTableTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-23): a disabled Delta connector is quiet.** Its periodic re-listing of ids was never cancelled when the connector stopped,
  and ran against the closed connector: one `trade log cannot be read: RejectedExecutionException` per stopped connector about 50 s
  later (and a stale problem on its listing). The re-listing is cancelled at close, and a run already under way when it closes reports
  nothing. CONNECTOR_FILES; DeltaSourcePluginTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-10): a missing id is `DRS-1001` while the kind's routed connector is off and another serves the kind.** With
  `trade-endur` disabled and a Cassandra `trading-store` serving `trade`, an id nobody holds answered `DRS-1002 connector trade-endur is
  disabled` for every missing id; it now answers `DRS-1001 no source holds trade/… (asked trading-store; trade-endur, which kind 'trade'
  is routed to, is disabled)`. `DRS-1002` stays the answer when nothing else serves the kind. CONNECTOR_FILES, CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR,
  TROUBLESHOOTING; SourceRegistryLiveTest.
- **Fixed (QA2-21, the rest): every loader's heap is `DRISHTI_LOADER_HEAP` (default `4g`) and its input under the data folder.**
  `load-redis.sh` no longer has a fixed `-Xmx6g`, and the DuckDB, files, MongoDB, PostgreSQL and Aerospike loaders no longer run with
  no `-Xmx` (a quarter of the machine); all of them write `banking.jsonl` under `DRISHTI_DATA_DIR` and remove it after the load.
  `make_data.py` writes the feed folder under `DRISHTI_DATA_DIR`, not the repository's `data/feeds`. ICEBERG_CONNECTOR, CLI_GUIDE;
  `tools/test_load_scripts.py`.
- **Added: `build_demo_lakes.py` says it is alive**: every 30 s (`DRISHTI_HEARTBEAT_SECONDS`) a line with the step, the time so far, the lake's size and the table being written, so a long `lake demo` or `lake build` never looks stuck.
- **Fixed (QA1-205): a `knownAt` before the data names the right thing.** `DRS-1013` now says nothing of the entity in that window was
  known at that instant, and names its days with data *as known now*, which may have been loaded later (*ask without knownAt, or with a
  later one*), instead of sending the reader to days that give the same 404. HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 7, tutorial 21.
- **Fixed (QA1-208, the rest of QA1-18): a history kind in another case, or no kind at all, is named.** `history/Trade/…` and
  `history/TRADE/…` answer `DRS-4004 unknown kind Trade: kinds are written in lower case, did you mean trade?` (kinds are exact everywhere
  in the API); `history/nokind/…` answers `DRS-4004 unknown kind nokind: the kinds here are …`, unless a source that serves any kind holds
  that entity; the CLI's `drishti.py history Trade …` says the same. Never `DRS-1001 no source holds`. HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 7.

- **Fixed (QA1-204): on a node that does not hold the trade, the type-ahead keeps the exact id first.** `TRD 1000002` on a node holding
  `10000020…` lists the peer's `1000002` right after the ranges, before this node's longer ids, instead of leaving it out.
  HISTORY_QUERIES.md 4.4.

- **Fixed (QA1-203): the range forms that still escaped answer `DRS-4022` with what to type**: `TRD 1000001 30.` (it opened today's
  view), `1e3`, `FROM 2026-10-01 TO` (*TO needs a date*), `TO 2026-10-01` (*TO follows FROM*) and `DIFF 30` (*DIFF follows a range*)
  instead of parser text; full-width digits are read as digits (`３0` is `30`, `０` is *0 days is empty*) instead of a raw `DRS-5001`;
  a bare id with a bad range (`1000015 -5`, `1000015 since yesterday`) answers `DRS-4022` from plain words and the console shows it on
  the terminal page. HISTORY_QUERIES.md 4.2 and 15.5, USER_GUIDE.
- **Fixed (QA1-207): plain words no longer drop half a time phrase.** `since A until B` (and `from A until B`) is `FROM A TO B`, the
  dates in order; a kind word and one id with no range open it (`fx audusd` is `FX FX-AUDUSD`); a time phrase in a search (`trades in
  BOOK-RATES-3 last week`) is listed as left out with how to ask for one entity over time, so the search no longer runs as if it was
  read. HISTORY_QUERIES.md 15.5, USER_GUIDE *Asking in plain words*.

- **Fixed (QA1-202, QA1-206): an advertise-url or seed url with port 0 or a port past 65535 is refused**, by the schema check
  (`POST /net/file/check`, `PUT`, the folder at start), by the gossip intake (such a member is never kept, so it no longer shows *alive*
  while every read through it fails) and by `drishti.py net`. The CLI and the server now take and refuse the same addresses: the CLI no
  longer writes `%`-escaped or trailing-dot hosts the server refuses, and takes a global IPv6 address (`http://[2001:db8::5]:18611`) as
  the server does; one table of cases (`advertise-urls.tsv`) is tested on both sides. NET.md section 2 says the rules.

- **Fixed (QA1-201): `tools/bench/trades.py` (and so `drishti-stack`'s lakes) opened the seven-day test data's `snapshot-2026-09-22`**, gone since the test data holds sixty days: it now takes the system's snapshot whatever its date. The help examples and docs name `snapshot-2026-07-08`.

- **Added: one part of the demo lakes at a time with `tools/build_demo_lakes.py`**: `--files-only` loads only your files into their system's table (the rest of the lakes kept), `--source-dir` with `--system calypso|murex` for another system's files, `--only trades/endur,…` rebuilds only those datasets. Windows QA guide step 3.0; `tools/test_build_demo_lakes.py`.

- **Fixed (QA1-201): `tools/bench/trades.py` (and so `drishti-stack`'s lakes) opened the seven-day test data's `snapshot-2026-09-22`**, gone since the test data holds sixty days: it now takes the system's snapshot whatever its date. The help examples and docs name `snapshot-2026-07-08`.
- **Fixed (QA1-17): history exports agree with the page.** With *changes only* on, the JSON holds only the changed days too
  (`onlyChanged: true`), as the CSV and Excel files do; Excel's dates are date cells (`yyyy-mm-dd`) that sort and filter as dates.
- **Fixed (QA1-11, QA1-12, console): the history page links to an entity's days with data and never swaps a range.** A `DRS-1013` page
  offers *From its first day with data* (the span the server names); `FROM b TO a` typed in the page's range field says `DRS-4022`
  and stays, as the server does. `test_history_routes.py`, `test_history_browser.py`.
- **Fixed (QA1-10): the demo lakes' market history has no phantom missing days and a real daily change.** `lake demo` moves the `DATE`
  of every point of a document's embedded series (`HISTORY`, `OHLC`, `FIXINGS`, `PNL_SERIES`) by the same business-day offset as the
  documents (holidays and coupon schedules stay); the test data's `CHANGE_1D` is now each day's move of the document's own price, not the
  last day's figure on all sixty (`data/testdata` regenerated). `tools/lake/test_demo.py`, `tools/testdata/test_testdata.py`.
- **Fixed (QA2-11, QA2-13): statement formats and paper.** Any format but pdf or xlsx gets the one answer *a statement is made as pdf
  or xlsx* (`docx` once got the export's list); `orientation` is read whatever its case, as `paper` was.
- **Fixed (QA1-09, console): the console's discovered servers follow the server's address rule.** `core/cluster.py` refused only
  localhost, 127.x and the wildcard; it now refuses every spelling the server's `HostAddress` refuses (`0`, `2130706433`, `0x7f000001`,
  `localhost.`, `ip6-localhost`, link-local and bridge addresses, and host names that resolve to them), in `core/hostrule.py`.
- **Fixed: `lake maintain` lays out the per-source trade tables.** It looked a table's layout up by its folder name as if it were a kind,
  so `trade_endur`, `trade_calypso` and `trade_murex` got a plain compaction out of id order; it now finds the layout of the connector whose
  table it is, and `relayout` keeps a table's own partitions (`business_date` and `PRODUCT_TYPE`) and checks each of them. `test_maintain.py`.
- **Fixed (QA1-07): History *Link* and `Alt+S` copy on a plain-http LAN address**: the link is shown, selected, in a field under the
  toolbar and copied with the Clipboard API where the page may use it, else from that field (`execCommand('copy')`), with a visible
  *Copied*; the link keeps `&only=1` when *changes only* is on. `test_history_browser.py`.
- **Fixed (QA2-08): Admin → Tokens no longer says tokens "never change anything"**: it says a read token only reads and a token with write
  scopes may change what they name; a new **Scopes** column marks each token *read only* or *writes* with its scopes; *Last used* is in the
  console's time zone with its name (`2026-10-20 08:54 New York`), as on the other Admin pages.
- **Fixed (QA2-09): `iceberg-external` (and the native Iceberg engine) find a path-based table a catalog wrote** (pyiceberg's
  `00003-<uuid>.metadata.json`, no `version-hint.text`) at the folder the docs name: the newest metadata file by sequence number is the
  current version, and a newer one is picked up at the next refresh. `CatalogNamedMetadataTest`.
- **Fixed (QA2-06): a bad `history:` block in a pack on disk shows on Admin → Packs (`problem`, `historyProblems`) and in Health (the pack
  DEGRADED)** from the start and after every reload, and is logged then, not only at the first history request; the pack keeps running
  with the default history. `HistoryBlockProblemsTest`.
- **Fixed (QA2-04): the Iceberg and Cassandra example connector files start as copied.** Their credentials default to empty
  (`${DRISHTI_ICEBERG_CREDENTIAL:}`, `${DRISHTI_CASSANDRA_PASSWORD:}`, a path-based lake or a node with no sign-in) instead of refusing
  the file; the trades' examples are `trade-endur.yaml` (the connector kind `trade` routes to), not a fourth `trading-store`, and promote
  the UPPER_SNAKE columns. ICEBERG_CONNECTOR's and CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR's complete files match. A credential may be an environment reference with an
  empty default (`${VAR:}`), never a value. `ConnectorExamplesTest` resolves every example with no environment set and checks its promoted columns.
- **Fixed (QA2-05): `tools/load-iceberg.sh --trades N --days D` no longer deletes the sample trades of those days**: the samples and the
  book are one input to one load, as `load-cassandra.sh` does; the same for `tools/windows/load-iceberg.ps1`. Both shell loaders take
  `DRISHTI_LOADER_HEAP` and keep their input file under the data folder, removed after the load (QA2-21). `tools/test_load_scripts.py`.
- **Fixed (QA2-03): Crimson Terminal's accent buttons** (the statement designer's Save and Preview pages, and every `.btn-accent`) were a pale
  pink gradient of the text accent under white labels (about 2:1); they now run from `--d-fill` to `--d-fill-strong` with `--d-on-fill`. A new guard in
  `test_contrast.py` fails any button rule that fills from `--d-accent`, or writes `--d-on-fill` on another ground.
- **Fixed (QA2-01): a statement's table no longer stops silently at 5,000 rows.** A table or matrix band is read whole, up to
  `drishti.distribution.statements.max-rows` (10,000), past the 5,000 rows one JSON answer to the screen holds; a longer one says so in a note above it,
  in the preview's new `cut` list and in the Excel file. Excel and CSV exports read their tables whole the same way (up to `export.max-rows`), and any
  other visual the engine cut says *The first N of M rows*.
- **Fixed (QA2-02): statement tokens work where STATEMENTS.md says.** `{page}`, `{pages}` and `{as-of}` are filled in a text band and a visual's title
  (a *(continued)* title carries its own page), and the check refuses `{param.<name>}` naming no parameter of the model and an unknown token in a title
  (`DRS-9038`).
- **Fixed (QA1-11): `DRS-1013` says when the entity has data** (*… has no data from 2026-09-01 to 2026-09-30; its days with data run
  from 2026-10-09 to 2026-10-20*), and for a number of days names the business days walked, not the whole `max-days` span as dates.
- **Fixed (QA1-18): a mnemonic, a plural or an unknown field in a history request is named.** `history TRD 1000001` answers
  *TRD is a mnemonic: use the kind trade*, `history trades …` *unknown kind trades: did you mean trade?* (`DRS-4004`), and a field in
  `fields` that no day has is `DRS-4004` instead of being dropped; a top-level field that is no column (`TRADER_ID`) is shown.
- **Fixed (QA1-08): the documented plain-words history phrases work for market kinds and bare ids.** `equity EQ-CSCA last 3 months`
  (an id that is also a field's value), `fx audusd since 2026-10-01` (an id named by its end) and `1000015 30` (a bare id with a range)
  open the history instead of becoming searches; `BOOK-RATES-3 last week` is the book's history; `trades in BOOK-RATES-3 since …` stays a
  search. USER_GUIDE and HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 15.5 say the rule.
- **Fixed (QA1-05, QA1-06): the type-ahead after an id offers every range, labelled, in a cluster and with masks.** Merged across
  Drishti*net it kept one row per entity, so only `30` was offered; now each range is its own row and the ranges come first. For a
  user with field masks the range rows were retitled as the entity (`TRD 1000002 · Commodity …`, or the masked title) and a partial
  range (`TRD 1000002 3`) offered nothing; range rows now pass through the masks unchanged (they hold nothing of the document).
- **Fixed (QA1-02, QA1-19): a Drishti*net peer that does not answer a history in time is a time-out** (`504 DRS-1014 the node qa1-b
  did not answer the history of … within 10 s`, or a `DRS-1014` problem of a partial answer), as HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 16 says,
  not `502 DRS-1003`. RELEASE_NOTES 2.6.5 *Across a cluster* notes the QA1-01 and QA1-02 fixes.
- **Fixed (QA1-03, QA1-12): a bad range typed after an id says so.** `TRD 1000001 0`, `-5`, `1.5M`, `3X`, `FROM yesterday`,
  `FROM 2026-02-30`, `DIFF DIFF`, a bare `DIFF` and two ranges (`30 30`) answer `DRS-4022` with what to type, a number of days past
  the limit (`99999`) `DRS-4021`, and `TO` before `FROM` is `DRS-4022` as at the endpoint (was `DRS-4001`); the console shows the
  message on the terminal page instead of today's view or plain words that dropped the id (`HistoryRange.malformed`).
- **Fixed (QA1-09): Drishti*net's "real address only" check can no longer be bypassed.** `advertise-url`, a seed's `url` and an
  address learned by gossip are refused in every spelling of loopback and wildcard (`localhost.`, `ip6-localhost`, `0`, `127.1`,
  `2130706433`, `0x7f000001`), when link-local, when they are an address of this machine's container or VM bridges (`172.17.0.1`), and
  when they are a host name that resolves here to any of those (the machine's own name is `127.0.1.1` on Debian and Ubuntu; the
  message says *oryxpro is 127.0.1.1 here*). The same in the server (`HostAddress`, the schema and the gossip intake) and in
  `drishti.py net` (`tools/hostaddr.py`).
- **Fixed (QA1-04): a history "as known at" an instant works on Delta Lake and Iceberg.** It was always refused with `DRS-1007`
  naming the shipped `file` connector, which serves every kind but holds none of the dates; a store without time travel is now
  refused only when it may hold one of the dates, as single reads do.
- **Fixed (QA1-13): a bad `knownAt` names itself** (`DRS-4003 knownAt 'garbage' is not an instant …`), not the business date.
- **Fixed (QA1-01): a history read through a Drishti*net peer shows the peer's new days.** The history cache was keyed by this node's
  data version only, so a load on the peer never showed. The key now carries the data versions of the peers the read asks, as their
  digests carry them; a connector that holds nothing of the kind counts as version `none` (so a node with an empty lake caches too).
- **Fixed (QA1-02): a peer that cannot be reached is named, never "no data".** When a node that holds the kind is down or suspect and
  no node that answered holds the dates, a history answers `502 DRS-5052` naming it (or, with some days read, a partial answer whose
  `problems` name it), not `DRS-1013`; an empty, failed or partial answer is never cached, so the rows come back with the peer.
- **Added: the AWS Delta lake deck** (`docs/publications/Drishti-AWS-Delta-Lake.pptx`, 72 slides, and its PDF): AWS_DELTA_LAKE.md section by section, from the lake in S3 and the folder layout and partition keys to setup, loading, maintenance, caches, scale, cost, runbooks and the decisions to take.

- **Docs: the 2.6.5 QA's documentation verification** ([DOCS_VERIFICATION.md](docs/qa/2026-10-20/DOCS_VERIFICATION.md)). The demo
  lakes' outputs say sixty business days (`64 tables, 60 business days 2026-07-08 to 2026-09-30, 943 s`; about 15 minutes and 2.2 GB),
  and figures captured on the seven-day lakes of 2.5.0 say so; `MX-20000001` is noted as cancelled part-way through the sixty days,
  and the ten-amendment ids (`1000279`, `1001049`, `CLY-4000509`, `MX-30001745`) are named for history. Tutorial 20 step 6 no longer
  saves a subscription with a token (`/me/**` writes never take one: the console saves it, a token reads it back; QA2-07). API_GUIDE
  says which workspaces a token never touches (QA2-25); HISTORY_QUERIES.md is marked built (QA1-19); tutorial 21 says its trader
  column is the reference answer's; GETTING_STARTED step 5 says the lakes end on today's business date (QA2-24);
  IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN names 2.6.5 as the current release; nineteen plugins and twenty-one tutorials where the counts were old.
- **Changed: the committed test data holds sixty business days** (`data/testdata`, was seven; 115 MB gzipped in 2,942 files, the
  largest 5 MB): a day-1 snapshot and fifty-nine days of changes, so a trade's history (`TRD <id> 60`) shows real amendments.
  `make_testdata.py --days` defaults to 60. The docs that said seven days say sixty; captured outputs (dates, counts, timings) are
  re-checked in the 2.6.5 QA's documentation verification.
- **Added: `tools/build_demo_lakes.py`**, every demo lake from start to finish in one command: `lake demo` over all sixty days, then
  your ENDUR files (`--endur-dir`, one `YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` per date) in place of the synthetic ones, `--dry-run` to look first. The
  Windows QA guide's new step 3.0.
- **Changed: a history's change log that has nothing to show says so and offers the way on** (`TRD <id> 30 DIFF` on an unchanged
  trade): which days were read, *Show the table* (`D`) and *Look back a year*, instead of one grey line that read as a failure.
- **Added: a Delta lake in Amazon S3 for Drishti past 200 TB, with real scripts** ([AWS_DELTA_LAKE.md](docs/architecture/AWS_DELTA_LAKE.md)).
  The lake (data files and `_delta_log`) is in S3; EFS keeps each node's state and warm caches. `deploy/aws/delta-lake/`: buckets
  (versioning, SSE-KMS, Block Public Access, VPC endpoint only, access logs, request metrics), IAM (read-only servers, a loader that
  never deletes, maintenance), a conditional-write probe, lifecycle tiers by **business date** (Standard, Standard-IA, Glacier
  Instant Retrieval; never an expiry of table files, never Flexible or Deep Archive), the daily load, backfill and restatement,
  nightly maintenance (`lake maintain` on `s3://`), a health and size report and CloudWatch alarms; `deploy/aws/ecs/`: EFS access
  points one per node per path, Cloud Map seeds, task and service definitions for the nodes, a console service, the loader,
  maintenance and report tasks, and EventBridge schedules. Every script is bash with the AWS CLI v2, takes its settings from
  `deploy/aws/env` (git-ignored; `env.example` has placeholders only) and has `--help` and `--dry-run`; `tools/test_deploy_aws.py`
  runs them all that way. Not yet run against a real AWS account.
- **New: `drishti.py lake publish`** commits the files of a table `lake build` wrote into a Delta table in S3 as one commit that
  replaces the dates (or partitions) it carries, with S3 conditional writes (deltalake 1.x has no DynamoDB lock), checking every file
  is in place first and refusing a change of columns ([TOOLS.md section 4.11](docs/guides/TOOLS.md#411-lake-publish-a-built-day-into-a-delta-table-in-s3);
  `tools/lake/test_publish.py`).
- **Changed: `deploy/` reorganised.** The Dockerfiles and compose files moved to `deploy/docker/` (`docker compose -f
  deploy/docker/compose.yaml up`, `docker build -f deploy/docker/server.Dockerfile .`; the build context stays the repository
  root); the ECS images and entry points that were written out in AWS_DEPLOYMENT.md are files in `deploy/docker/ecs/` (plus a
  loader image), and AWS_DEPLOYMENT.md points to them (status: scripts in `deploy/`). The lake bucket's versioning is now on, with
  noncurrent versions expired after 14 days. `deploy/lake-maintenance.yaml` (the local example) stays where it was.

## 2.6.5 — History queries: any entity over time from the command line (`TRD <id> 30`, `EQ <id> 3M`), on every connector and across Drishti*net (2026-10-20)

- **Fixed: an API token can preview a statement and fetch its PDF or Excel** (`POST /api/v1/bi/reports/statement/preview` and `/file`
  read and render as the caller and store nothing, so they are read-only posts; in 2.6.0 they had no token scope).
- **Docs: QA on Windows** gains section 6c, history with the demo lakes' ids that changed most, the CLI form and plain words; the
  trading pack's history shows status, action, version, quantity and price first; `drishti-stack`'s peers hold 60 business days of
  synthetic trades for history demos.

- **Added: history queries in the console (H7, H8, H9).** A range after the id shows the entity over time: `TRD 1000015 30`, `EQ EQ-CSCA 3M`,
  `FX FX-AUDUSD YTD`, `CRV CRV-USD-SOFR FROM 2026-09-01 TO 2026-09-30`, each optionally ending in `DIFF`. The server reads the range off the
  end of a command after the alias is expanded (`HistoryRange`); a lone id after the mnemonic, a search and an id that contains the words stay
  as they were, and a range that cannot hold says why (`DRS-4001`). `POST /api/v1/command` answers `history` (range, mode, query) and the
  type-ahead offers `30`, `3M`, `YTD`, `30 DIFF` after one entity's id. The console's `/go` opens `/v/{kind}/{id}/history?days=30`
  (`/v/{kind}/{id}?days=30` redirects there; a pick list carries the range to its rows). The page: a header with the dates, the counts and
  the days with no data; one row per business day, newest first, changed cells tinted with what they were, `⟲2` for a restated day,
  changes only (`C`), the keyboard (`↑↓ ←→ Enter Esc R ?`), every theme; a market kind adds its headline numbers (last, change, high, low,
  range, average) and a chart that follows the selected row. DIFF lists each changed day's changes with links to Compare and to the day.
  Exports `/export/history/{kind}/{id}.csv|.xlsx|.json` and a link that pins the last date
  ([USER_GUIDE › History](docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md#history-an-entity-over-time)).
- **Added: `drishti.py history KIND ID` and the Python client's `history()` (H11).** `--days`, `--range`, `--from`/`--to`, `--fields`,
  `--known-at`, `--diff`, `--changes-only`; a text table newest first with changed cells marked `*`, `--json` (the answer), `--csv`
  ([CLI_GUIDE › history](docs/guides/CLI_GUIDE.md#history-an-entity-over-time)). `Drishti.history(kind, id, days=…)` and `history_rows()`
  in `clients/python/drishti_client.py` ([CLIENTS](docs/guides/CLIENTS.md)).
- **Added: plain words for history (H11).** A kind, an id and a time phrase make a history command: `trade 1000015 last month` is
  `TRD 1000015 FROM 2026-09-01 TO 2026-09-30`, `equity EQ-CSCA last 3 months` is `EQ EQ-CSCA 3M`, `what changed on trade 1000015 this week`
  ends in `DIFF` (`HistoryPhrase`, `PhraseParser.Parsed.command`). Read fully, it opens at once; otherwise the search page says *Read as …*
  ([USER_GUIDE › Asking in plain words](docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md#asking-in-plain-words)).
- **Added: tutorial 21, An entity over time**, and the help centre's `history` entries (F1 on the history page opens the user guide's
  History section) ([docs/tutorials/21-an-entity-over-time.md](docs/tutorials/21-an-entity-over-time.md)). `test_tutorials.py` now reads
  every numbered tutorial (its glob stopped at 19). Tutorial 20 gains its *You should see* lines, a *Next steps* section and the
  statement's second page (`statement-page-2.jpg`).
- **Added: history queries, more fast paths (H5, H6).** DuckDB (one range query), MongoDB (one range read on the `_id` index), Aerospike
  (the entity's index record, then one batch get) and Iceberg (the entity's dates from the layout and id maps); each passes
  `DatedSourceContract`'s history cases. The file, S3, REST and stream connectors keep the default per-day read.
- **Changed: a market history longer than the store's days is filled from the document's own series.** `EQ EQ-CSCA 3M` on a lake that
  keeps a week took the week only and listed 55 days as missing: the days before the oldest stored one now come from the newest
  document's `series:` list (`basis: "documents+series"`, those rows `source: "series"`), so the chart and the high, low and change cover
  the three months; and the days before an entity's first day with data are not missing (`window.loadedFrom`).
- **Added: history queries, the Cassandra fast path (H6).** An entity's days from `entity_days` in one range read, then one point read per
  day, eight at once ([CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR.md](docs/connectors/CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR.md)); `DatedSourceContract`'s history cases pass.
- **Added: history queries, the Redis and PostgreSQL fast paths (H5).** Redis reads an entity's days and all their documents in two or three
  round trips (`ZRANGEBYSCORE`, one `MGET`) ([REDIS_CONNECTOR.md](docs/connectors/REDIS_CONNECTOR.md)); PostgreSQL in table mode in one
  range query ([POSTGRES_CONNECTOR.md](docs/connectors/POSTGRES_CONNECTOR.md)). Both pass `DatedSourceContract`'s history cases.
- **Added: `HistoryBench` (H14, `drishti-benchmarks`).** 10K synthetic trades × 30 business days on Delta, 200 trades: the 30-day history at
  the service in 13.2 ms cold (p95 19.1), 0.83 ms warm, 0.29 ms cached ([HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 14](docs/architecture/HISTORY_QUERIES.md#14-performance-targets),
  [SCALE_STRATEGY.md](docs/architecture/SCALE_STRATEGY.md)).
- **Added: history queries, the shipped packs' `history:` blocks (H13).** trading (trade; the derived kinds off), market-data (equity,
  equity index, FX spot, IR and credit curves, commodity, bond, inflation index as charts with their headline numbers), counterparty-risk
  and market-risk (their measures as columns) ([PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#history-an-entity-over-time),
  [PACK_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/PACK_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md#history-a-kind-over-time)). `sutra lint <pack-dir>` (and so the deploy
  check) reports a wrong block as `DRS-2061`. `ShippedHistoryBlocksTest` holds every block clean and naming real sample fields.
- **Added: history queries, the Delta fast path (H5).** The Delta connector lists an entity's dates from the snapshot's partitions and
  the id maps it already keeps (`days()`), so a history reads only the days that hold the entity ([DELTA_CONNECTOR.md section
  8](docs/connectors/DELTA_CONNECTOR.md#8-seven-years-of-history)).
- **Added: history queries across Drishti*net (H4).** A node with the pack but not the data reads an entity's history from the peer that
  holds it in one call for all the dates (`GET /api/v1/net/history/{kind}/~`, node-only, masked there and again here); a node without the
  pack forwards the whole request, as views are ([NET.md](docs/admin/NET.md#7-routing-where-a-request-goes)). `NetHistoryTest`. A history of
  data days now ends at the entity's newest day with data, and a span that ends after the newest loaded date says so (`window.loadedTo`)
  instead of listing those days as missing.
- **Added: history queries, the server (H2, H3).** `GET /api/v1/history/{kind}/{id}?days=30 | range=3M | from=&to=` (and `/~?id=`)
  answers the entity over the business days it has data: one row per day with its top-level fields, the fields that changed from the day
  before (a baseline day is read for the first row), the counts of its lists, versions and generation, and for market kinds the headline
  numbers (last, change, high and low with their dates, range, average); `diff=true` adds each day's changes, `knownAt` reads the history
  as the store stood then. Every day is masked for the caller before anything is computed from it. The kind's dated connectors are read in
  order, each for the dates no earlier one covers (`SourceRouter.history`); answers are cached by the kind's data version. New settings
  `drishti.history.*` ([CONFIGURATION](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md)), new codes `DRS-1012`, `DRS-1013`, `DRS-1014`, `DRS-2061`,
  `DRS-4021`, `DRS-4022` ([API_GUIDE](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md#history-queries-an-entity-over-time),
  [TROUBLESHOOTING](docs/guides/TROUBLESHOOTING.md#error-codes)); the field dialog's series now goes back `drishti.history.max-days`
  instead of 260 days; Admin → Cache lists and purges `history`. `HistoryServiceTest`, `SourceRouterHistoryTest`, `HistoryApiTest`.
- **Added: history queries, the connector contract (H1).** `SourcePlugin.history(HistoryRequest)` returns `HistoryRead(days, covered,
  failed)`, one `HistoryDay(date, document, versions)` per business date the source holds the entity on; `SourcePlugin.days(ref, from, to)`
  lists those dates without reading a document. The default (`HistoryReads.perDay`) reads date by date through `fetch(ref, asOf)`, eight at
  once, within a budget, so every connector has history; `DatedSourceContract` gains eight history cases that every dated plugin passes.
  The JSON contract of `GET /api/v1/history/{kind}/{id}` is fixed as `drishti-testkit/src/main/resources/history/trade-1000015-30.json`
  and `equity-EQ-CSCA-3M.json` ([CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE](docs/connectors/CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md),
  [HISTORY_QUERIES.md section 8](docs/architecture/HISTORY_QUERIES.md#8-the-json-contract)).

## 2.6.0 — Statements and field parameters, the Cassandra connector, drishti-iceberg (Iceberg without Hadoop), three Terminal themes, the known-bug pass (2026-10-20)

- **Fixed: a BI item's history, review, impact and lineage could show an older state.** Drawn again after a save, the item asked for them again,
  and a slower answer to the earlier request, arriving last, overwrote the newer one: the history could miss the save just made. Only the
  answer to the newest request is drawn now (found by `test_bi_gov_browser` under load).
- **Fixed: an embedded report's visuals, and Analyse's model queries and SQL, gave up after the console's usual few seconds.** 2.5.0 let a
  report's query wait as long as the server lets it run (35 s) on the console's own pages only; an embedded report (`/embed/v1`) and the
  Analyse page still timed out at 5 s with DRS-1004 on a loaded or cold server while the server was still answering. They wait as long now
  (found by `test_embed_report_browser` under load: every visual said DRS-1004).
- **Fixed: a BI query could fail with "connector 'trade-pages' is not running (running)" while a connector restarted.** A connector being
  restarted (a retry, a changed file) is out of routing for its drain and its start, and a query in that window was refused although the
  connector was listed as running. A query now waits (up to 30 s) for the restart. And a derived connector (`trade-pages`) asked while the
  server was still starting said it was DOWN, which made the registry restart it as a lost connection a few seconds later, opening that
  window: it now waits for the server instead (found by `test_bi_wizard_and_controls_browser` under load: the dataset wizard's first step
  never appeared).
- **Fixed: the report designer could save a draft without its last change.** The YAML is written by the server a moment after each change;
  when two changes' texts crossed (the earlier one arriving last), the earlier text was kept and the YAML tab marked up to date, so the
  YAML tab, and a Save, missed the last change (a tablet layout's Wide, say). An older text never replaces a newer one now, and Save waits for
  the text of the last change (found by `test_bi_wave_a_browser` under load).
- **Fixed: a report visual's Show the rows and Drill through used the selection of when its buttons were drawn.** A mark chosen just before
  the click (the buttons not yet redrawn) was ignored: the rows of the earlier selection, or of none, were shown. The selection at the click
  is used now (found by `test_bi_reports_browser` under load).
- **New: the Apache Cassandra connector** (plugin `cassandra`, `plugins/drishti-plugin-cassandra`), built as designed in
  [CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR_DESIGN.md](docs/architecture/CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR_DESIGN.md) over the Apache Cassandra Java driver 4.19 (pure Java):
  Apache Cassandra 4 and 5, ScyllaDB and DataStax Astra (secure connect bundle and token); Amazon Keyspaces' SigV4 log-in is wired but not
  yet checked by hand. A data domain is a keyspace, each kind a table with a partition per business day, generation and bucket keyed by id,
  beside `entity_days` and `kind_days`; no secondary index, and no statement without a whole partition key. Every read path: one entity
  (snapshot and effective kinds), type-ahead, a day's promoted columns for searches, pick lists, derived kinds and impact, reverse lookups,
  a day's documents, inventory, the latest day and data versions, the pool and health. `CassandraLoader` (`tools/load-cassandra.sh`,
  `tools/windows/load-cassandra.ps1`, `drishti.py data load --store cassandra`) writes each day under a new generation and names it in
  `kind_days` last, so **a day appears whole or not at all**, then drops the old generation's partitions; `--create` makes the tables (else
  it prints the CQL), `--ttl-days` (one-day time windows) or `--keep-days` with `--force-drop`, bounded writes in flight, unlogged batches
  only within a partition. TLS through the shared `tls.*`; `proxy.*` refused (TCP). Tests: the dated-source contract against Cassandra 5 and
  ScyllaDB 6.2 in Testcontainers, every read path equal to the Delta connector over the same data, a reader polling through a reload, every
  prepared statement checked against the live partition keys, mutual TLS against Cassandra 5, the loader's schema, retention and TTL.
  [CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR.md](docs/connectors/CASSANDRA_CONNECTOR.md) (with numbers measured at 10,000 trades a day); CONNECTORS, CONFIGURATION,
  TLS, DEMO_DATA, TOOLS, GETTING_STARTED, DEVELOPER_GUIDE, the help centre and `config/connectors.examples/cassandra/`.
- **New: drishti-iceberg, Drishti's own Apache Iceberg engine with no Hadoop; Iceberg on Windows without winutils.exe.** The Iceberg
  connector reads and writes through it with `engine: native` (or `DRISHTI_ICEBERG_ENGINE=native`; `--engine` for `IcebergLoader`
  and `IcebergMaintenance`): table metadata (fields it does not know are kept when it writes the next version), path-based tables,
  a metadata file named directly (`metadata-location.<kind>`) and REST catalogs over the JDK's HTTP client (config, OAuth2 client
  credentials, `loadTable` with the ETag, vended S3 credentials, commits, SigV4 for Glue), Avro manifests, planning with
  partition-summary and column-bound pruning over every transform, Parquet read by field id (renames, added columns, widened types,
  files without ids through the name mapping), position and equality deletes by the sequence-number rules, time travel, and the
  writer: Parquet with field ids and Iceberg metrics through parquet-java on `PlainParquetConfiguration` with Drishti's own codecs,
  commits made safe by a hard-link create on local disk, a conditional PUT on S3 (a store without them is found by a probe and its
  tables are single-writer) and the REST catalog's requirements. Each Parquet footer, manifest and delete file is read once and kept,
  so the first single-entity read no longer re-reads the footer. Format version 3, encrypted tables and ORC or Avro data files are
  refused by name. `engine: library` is today's Apache Iceberg Java code, kept for tables in Azure, Google Cloud Storage or HDFS;
  **`native` is the default** (every Iceberg test passes on it), and left to the default a root it cannot reach is read by the
  library; `engine: library` (or `DRISHTI_ICEBERG_ENGINE=library`) brings back the old behaviour, `auto` keeps the library on Linux. On 10,000 trades a day the native engine started the connector in 0.6 s (library 1.8 s) and read a trade
  in 54 ms median (75 ms) ([ICEBERG_CONNECTOR.md section 17](docs/connectors/ICEBERG_CONNECTOR.md#17-engines-native-and-library)).
  `tools/windows/load-iceberg.ps1` loads Iceberg tables on Windows, `tools/windows/start-server.ps1 -Iceberg` serves them;
  GETTING_STARTED's Windows table loses its Iceberg row. Proven by a pyiceberg interop suite (fixtures written by pyiceberg, read
  back equal; tables written by drishti-iceberg read back by pyiceberg), every plugin test on both engines, a class-loader test that
  refuses Hadoop's file systems, `Shell` and `Configuration` for the connector, the loader and maintenance, writers committing at once
  (local disk, a REST catalog), and refusal and Windows path tests ([DRISHTI_ICEBERG.md](docs/architecture/DRISHTI_ICEBERG.md)).
- **New: the `iceberg-external` connector, for Iceberg tables Drishti does not write** (Spark, Flink, Trino, Snowflake, Glue, dbt):
  read-only through drishti-iceberg, the row is the document (structs, lists, maps; `decimals: string`, `binary: omit`), the id comes
  from the identifier fields or named columns, the business date from a column, a timestamp's day in a zone or the partition spec
  (else current only), every primitive column is searchable, `links` make reverse lookups, an unsorted table gets an id index,
  `tables: auto` serves a namespace and names tables without an id, and `StarterSchemas` drafts a pack's schemas from the tables
  ([ICEBERG_EXTERNAL_CONNECTOR.md](docs/connectors/ICEBERG_EXTERNAL_CONNECTOR.md)).
- **Changed: the native Delta engine's file access is a module of its own, `drishti-lakeio`**, shared with the coming Iceberg engine
  ([DRISHTI_ICEBERG.md](docs/architecture/DRISHTI_ICEBERG.md) step I1): local disk and S3 storage, byte ranges, the Parquet page codecs
  (which now also compress, for writers) and the per-file Parquet footer cache moved out of `drishti-deltalake` unchanged; local disk gains
  an exclusive create (a hard link of a finished file) and S3 a conditional PUT (`If-None-Match: *`) with a start-up probe, for safe commits.
  Every Delta test passes as before; the tests of the moved classes moved with them.
- **Fixed: Drishti*net: the cluster never ends with no enabled administrator.** The last-administrator rule was checked only where a change was
  made, so two nodes disabling (or demoting, or deleting) the last two administrators at the same moment could leave none enabled. A peer's
  change that would leave a node with none is now repaired as it is applied: the disabled administrator enabled most recently is enabled again
  (every node picks the same one), else the one the change took away is kept; the repair travels to every node, is audited
  (`last-admin-kept`) and shown on Admin → Health ([NET.md section 7](docs/admin/NET.md#7-routing-where-a-request-goes)). `NetLastAdminTest`.
- **Changed: Drishti*net members advertise only a real address.** `advertise-url` in node.yaml and a seed's `url` in `peers/*.yaml` that is
  `localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `::1`, `0.0.0.0` or `::` are refused by the server at load, Admin → Net → Files and `drishti.py net
  check|set|add-peer`, with a message naming this machine's address to use; a member learned by gossip with such an address is never kept nor
  passed on, and the console never discovers one (a member that moved address replaces its old one in `servers.json`). Without
  `advertise-url` a node now advertises this machine's primary IPv4 address (the default route's interface, never a container bridge) with
  its port, shown on Admin → Net as *detected*. The in-process cluster tests, `net_stack.py`, the soak, tutorial 19 and `drishti-stack/`
  (`DRISHTI_STACK_HOST`, or `host:` in stack.yaml) use the machine's real address ([NET.md section 2](docs/admin/NET.md#2-a-cluster-of-three)).
- **Field parameters in a report's wells.** A well of a report on a model may name `param.<name>`, a field parameter of the model, and the reader's
  parameter slicer switches which field or measure the visual draws (a field parameter's choices may now be measures as well as fields, in the model
  designer's Calculations tab too). The server puts the choice in place of the parameter in the wells and the sort, asks that as the reader (masks hold)
  and answers the visual's `wells` as drawn; the report state carries the reader's parameter values (`params`), which every model query of the report
  now takes as MQ v2 `params`. The report schema refuses a parameter that does not fit its well, a what-if parameter in a well, one on a report over a
  dataset (`DRS-9037`). BI Studio's Build offers the model's field parameters in each well that can take them. A model query takes a field parameter of
  measures in `select.measures` and in `sort`. `FieldParamsApiTest`, `drishti-console/tests/test_bi_wave_d_browser.py` (a slicer switches a column chart
  from DESK to PORTFOLIO and from MTM to NOTIONAL). REPORTS.md section 21, BI_GUIDE.md section 27, tutorial 20.
- **Paginated reports and statements.** A report page with `layout: statement` is paper, as boards became a report layout: its bands (text, cards,
  tables, matrices) are laid out page by page by the server on the paper the report's `statement:` sets (A4, A3, A5, Letter or Legal, portrait or
  landscape, margins, a header and footer with tokens such as `{page}`, `{pages}`, `{run-date}`, `{period}`, `{reader}`, `{param.<name>}`), with
  automatic and explicit page breaks (`pageBreak`), a table's column titles repeated on every page it runs onto (`repeatHeader`), bands and groups of rows
  kept together (`keepTogether`, `groupBy`), a period worked out from the run's date (`period: previous-month`) and the model's parameters. It is made as a
  page-exact PDF (written by the server, no library) or as Excel set to print on the same paper (print titles, header and footer), and a subscription
  bursts it: one statement per recipient, each made as that recipient, the file named after them. `POST /api/v1/bi/reports/statement/preview` and
  `/file`, `drishti.py bi statement preview|file`, `drishti.distribution.statements.*`, the report schema's checks (`DRS-9038`). The statement designer in
  BI Studio: Format → Layout → statement, the **Paper** tab (paper, header and footer, period) with a page-by-page preview, and each band's Format →
  Statement. Accepted: a monthly statement bursts to ten recipients, each masked, page-exact in PDF (page count and header text checked on every page,
  two recipients' statements differing only by their masks and row rules; `StatementApiTest`, `StatementLayoutTest`). STATEMENTS.md (new),
  DISTRIBUTION.md, REPORTS.md section 22, API_GUIDE.md, CLI_GUIDE.md, CONFIGURATION.md, tutorial 20.
- **New: three themes, the Terminal look in one dominant hue.** **Green Terminal** (`terminal-green`: phosphor green with light lime on deep
  forest), **Crimson Terminal** (`terminal-crimson`: crimson with blush on wine-black; text in the accent dusty rose, links peach, crimson kept
  for buttons, highlights and the chrome's gradient) and **Blue Terminal** (`terminal-blue`: azure with periwinkle on midnight): ten themes in
  the theme menu, the account page, `PATCH /api/v1/me/settings` (`DRS-5001` lists them), `ui.default_theme` and the elements' `theme`.
  Buttons and pressed controls now fill with `--d-fill` / `--d-fill-strong` and label with `--d-on-fill` (the accent and `--d-on-accent` unless a
  theme sets its own, as Crimson Terminal does: a deep crimson under white labels). `test_contrast.py`: every text colour at 4.5:1 on every
  ground in all ten themes, and labels on button fills. USER_GUIDE (Themes: how to choose, the contrast rule), GETTING_STARTED, ARCHITECTURE,
  CONFIGURATION, API_GUIDE, ELEMENTS.
- **Plan (product owner, 2026-10-20): 2.6.0 is the known-bug pass, statements, field parameters, the Cassandra connector and drishti-iceberg**
  (Iceberg without Hadoop). Send and push (Outlook, Teams, SharePoint, connector dump) and integration (the PostgreSQL-wire endpoint, the
  Excel feed, embed tokens bound to envelopes, SDK events, and the feeds and embedding UI) move after the 3.0.0 release; the Sajha MCP
  connector moves to 2.8.0 with the AI work ([BI_DESIGN.md §12.2](docs/architecture/BI_DESIGN.md#122-the-three-waves),
  [IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](docs/architecture/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md#roadmap)).
- **Design: Drishti on AWS** ([AWS_DEPLOYMENT.md](docs/architecture/AWS_DEPLOYMENT.md)), design only, with no AWS or Docker code in the
  repository (`deploy/aws/` removed; the Dockerfiles and entry points are in the document): ECS with the server and the console as separate
  containers, every written path on EFS at the same path, lakes in S3, Drishti*net through Cloud Map.
- **Design: super scale** (`docs/architecture/SUPER_SCALE.md`, not built, release not decided): 500 GB of JSON a day kept seven years on
  one Delta lake in S3 shared by many servers and consoles: tables by year, a loader fleet with a day register, products computed when loading,
  range maps instead of id maps, (table, day) slices owned on the Drishti*net ring; build plan S1-S8.
- **Docs reviewed for 2.5.0 and 2.6.0.** The release order the same everywhere (BI_DESIGN, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN, BI_MODEL_ARCHITECTURE,
  WRITE_BACK, GENUI, SQL.md); Drishti*net as built (CLI_GUIDE's `net` walk-through on real output, NET.md and TROUBLESHOOTING for the
  real-address refusal and *Last administrator kept*, OPERATIONS, ARCHITECTURE, HOW_IT_FITS); the README and the documentation index
  (twenty tutorials, statements, themes, broken rows repaired, ADR-021); help cards; the deck current to 2.6.0.

- **Design: history queries** ([HISTORY_QUERIES.md](docs/architecture/HISTORY_QUERIES.md)), for 2.6.5: a range after the id
  (`TRD 1000015 30`, `EQ EQ-CSCA 3M`, `YTD`, `FROM … TO …`, `DIFF`) shows the entity over the business days it has data, as a table of
  its top-level fields with the changes marked or, for market data, a chart with the high, low and change; a pack's `history:` block per
  kind, `GET /api/v1/history/{kind}/{id}`, an optional `SourcePlugin.history` with fast paths per store, the same masks on every day.

- **Design: write-back** ([WRITE_BACK.md](docs/architecture/WRITE_BACK.md)), editing and saving fields on a Sutra page through ordered
  lanes (one change at a time per record), for 2.7.0; AI moves to 2.8.0 and wave E to 2.9.0
  ([BI_DESIGN.md §12.2](docs/architecture/BI_DESIGN.md#122-the-three-waves)).

## 2.5.0 — Drishti*net: many servers, one Drishti; no JVM litters /tmp; the documents, help and deck reviewed (2026-10-19)

- **Fixed: Drishti*net: a user known to one node stays signed in after the console falls over.** The other nodes refused a user they did not
  hold (`registered-users-only`), so a console moving from the node where the user was made signed them out, unless every node shared one
  identity database. The nodes now copy their identity to each other over the signed user-state batches: users (roles, packs, enabled, the
  password hash, never a password), the roles of Admin → Roles and personal API tokens (the secret's SHA-256), the newest change winning. Disabling
