  and deleting a user travel too: a deletion leaves a tombstone (`<data dir>/net/identity-deleted.json`, 90 days), so a node that was down deletes
  the user (with their tokens, settings and sessions) when it comes back and its stale copy never brings them back; a user made again after a
  deletion is a new user; of two users of one name made apart the older stands (a new node's seeded administrator never replaces the cluster's).
  Every change applied is audited on the receiving node with `net:<node>`. `user-state: off` (one shared database) copies nothing, as before.
  `NetIdentityTest` (three servers in one JVM, security on). NET.md section 7, DRISHTI_NET.md sections 9, 11 and 19, tutorial 19 step 8.
- **Fixed: a Delta connector's on-disk document cache keeps what was read before a restart.** A document read by the start-up warm-up went
  into the memory tier before the disk tier, so a page answered from memory at that moment never wrote it to disk, and a close dropped the
  warm-up's late write; a background read still inside the cache also kept it locked after the close. The disk tier is now written first, and
  closing waits up to 5 s for background reads to leave (found as a flaky `DeltaDocDiskCacheTest` under the drill's load).
- **Fixed: a live view's tick no longer closes an open panel help popover.** A tick that replaced a panel with tabs selected the reader's tab
  again by clicking it, and that click reached the page's own listeners: the panel `?` popover (and a pinned tip, a menu) closed about once a
  second on a ticking view, in the console and in `<drishti-view>`. The tab is now selected by a click that does not bubble (live.js,
  controls.js and Drishti Elements **1.4.3**). Found as a flaky `test_trusted_types_enforced_host_still_works`.
- **Fixed: a BI visual a busy server answers in more than 5 s is drawn, not refused.** The console gave every BI call its usual 5 s wait,
  while the server lets a query run 30 s (`drishti.bi.query.timeout`): a tile or visual on a loaded server showed `DRS-1004 the server did not
  answer in time` while the server was still working on it. A query (`.../query`, `mq`, `sql`, the Arrow rows) now waits 35 s. Found as the
  flaky board designer test of `test_wave_b_browser` (0 of 3 under load before, 5 of 5 after).
- **Fixed: the report designer's Query pane shows the selected visual's query when its answer comes after the selection.** It redrew on a
  selection and 0.6 s after a change only, so a visual selected before its query was answered said *Choose a visual*; it now redraws when the
  answer arrives. And the Fields pane is no longer rebuilt when nothing in it changed: a rebuild just after the page was ready took the field
  being dragged from under the pointer and the drop was lost. Found as the flaky `test_w1_model_designer_browser`.
- **Fixed: Pause on a live BI page stops the redraws at once.** A redraw asked by a tick just before Pause (waiting out the 250 ms coalescing)
  still ran on the paused page; it is now dropped. Admin → Health waits 30 s for the server (it measures connectors and temp folders, and is the
  page an administrator opens when the server is struggling) instead of answering 504 after the console's usual 5 s.
- **Fixed: `pytest` from the repo root collects every test.** `tools/bench/test_trades.py` and `tools/packgen/trades/test_trades.py`
  shared a module name, so collection stopped with an import-file-mismatch error; the benchmark's test is now `tools/bench/test_bench_trades.py`.
- **Fixed: derived pages kept on disk are never removed while another process has them open.** A server's start-up clean-up of
  `<data dir>/derived/<connector>` deleted every folder it did not keep, including a store another process was writing (two servers
  sharing a data folder; found by the drill's parallel test forks); it now leaves a store whose lock is held (`SortedStore.inUse`).

- **Drishti*net membership (N1).** Several servers act as one cluster, **off by default**: with no `config/net/` folder (or `enabled: false`
  in its `node.yaml`) a server is a single server exactly as before. Switched on, each node gossips over HTTP (SWIM-style push-pull every
  second with three random members, direct and indirect probes, `alive`, `suspect` after 5 s, `dead` after 30 s more, `left` when it stops
  cleanly), saves `<data dir>/net/peers.json` every 10 minutes, on every join or leave and at shutdown, and rejoins from it after a restart with
  no seed. Every call between nodes is signed with the cluster key (`secret: ${DRISHTI_NET_SECRET}` in `node.yaml`, an environment reference
  only; HMAC over method, path, time, node and body; a 60 s window); a refused call is `401 DRS-5051` and is listed. The folder is managed like
  connector files: `node.yaml`, `peers/<name>.yaml`, `gossip.yaml`, `routing.yaml`, `access.yaml`, watched, each checked against its schema (a
  refused file keeps the previous settings, its problems named by line), written atomically with a history under `<data dir>/net/history/`;
  ready-made files in `config/net.examples/`. **Admin → Net** (Members: every node's state, version, tags, latency, incarnation; add a seed,
  remove a gone member, drain, undrain, leave, rejoin; Files: check, save with `If-Match`, delete, history with a diff); `/api/v1/net/gossip`,
  `/ping`, `/members`, `/seeds`, `/members/{id}`, `/drain`, `/undrain`, `/leave`, `/rejoin`, `/files`, `/file`; `drishti.py net list | check |
  add-peer | set | members`. New module `drishti-net`; error codes `DRS-5050` to `DRS-5056`. Measured: five nodes from one seed list each other
  within 5 s and a killed one is dead everywhere within 40 s (fake clock); three real servers in one JVM converge 52 ms after the third starts.
  [NET.md](docs/admin/NET.md), [DRISHTI_NET.md](docs/architecture/DRISHTI_NET.md) section 19, CONFIGURATION, OPERATIONS, API_GUIDE, CLI_GUIDE,
  TROUBLESHOOTING, ARCHITECTURE.
- **Drishti*net capability digests (N2).** Each node publishes what it serves (`GET /api/v1/net/digest`: packs with version and a hash of their
  files, peer-only packs, kinds with mnemonic and sources, each source's table identity, newest business date and data version, the load, the
  business calendar, masked fields, nodes per role) and gossips its hash; peers pull a changed digest within a quarter of a second of hearing
  the hash. From all digests every node builds the capability table: kind to the nodes that render it and the nodes that hold its data, the newest
  pack version marked, **replicas** (the same table read by several nodes: a search asks one) and **shards** (different tables: a search asks
  all). A pack switched on at one of three servers is in the others' tables 1.0 s later. New source SPI method `SourcePlugin.tableIdentity(kind)`
  (the Delta connector: the table's id from its log, else its location). **Admin → Net → Capability map** with a filter and warnings (one version
  of a pack with different contents, different calendars, different masks); `GET /api/v1/net/capabilities`. NET.md section 5, API_GUIDE,
  CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net security (N3).** The cluster key rotates with no downtime in three rolling steps (accept the new key, sign with it, drop the old):
  13,542 signed calls among three servers through the rollover, none refused; Admin → Net → Security shows the key's state (never the key), which
  peers still sign with the previous key, mutual TLS and its certificates' expiry, and every refused call. Forwarded requests carry a 30 s
  on-behalf-of token for one node; a node refuses one whose roles may not reach it (`403 DRS-5054`, nodes per role in `access.yaml`) and records
  the request as `net:from <node> request <id>` in its access log. **Admin → Net → Access**: nodes per role (ids or tags) with the members each
  reaches now, this node's tags and peer-only packs, written to access.yaml and node.yaml, applied live; `GET/PUT /api/v1/net/access`,
  `GET /api/v1/net/security`. NET.md section 6 (the key, its rotation, mutual TLS, on-behalf-of, masks twice, nodes per role, audit), API_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net routing and the console's failover (N4).** A request for a kind whose pack is not on a node, is older there or is served to peers only
  there is **forwarded whole** to a node that renders it (signed, one hop, an on-behalf-of token; the answer passed back unchanged and streamed, a live
  view too): the console cannot tell. A view whose pack is here but whose data is not is built here from the document a peer reads for the caller
  (`GET /api/v1/net/fetch/{kind}/{id}`), masked there and again here: a field this node masks stays masked when the peer masks less. The **newest pack
  version is served**; **peer-only packs** are hidden from a node's own users and served to its peers. A node that is down makes a clear
  `502 DRS-5052` naming it, never a wrong page. Admin → Net → **Routing** (explain a request as a role; forwarded traffic per peer a minute, slowest
  peers, latest calls), Admin → Health's Drishti*net line, metrics `drishti_net_members`, `drishti_net_forwarded_total`, `drishti_net_gossip_rounds_total`,
  `drishti_net_peer_latency_seconds`; `GET /api/v1/net/explain`, `/traffic`; the access log of both nodes names the other and the request. **The console
  takes several servers** (`backend.urls`; `discover`, `discover_every` 60 s, `check_every` 5 s, `fail_after` 2): it learns the cluster from the server it
  uses, keeps the list in `<console data dir>/net/servers.json`, keeps each session on one server, falls over when it fails (a read retried once on
  another server, a write never), uses a recovered server again for new sessions after a minute, and reports each failover (audited
  `net-console-failover`). Source router: a `Fallback` to the peers after the local sources. NET.md sections 7 and 8, CONFIGURATION, API_GUIDE,
  OPERATIONS, TROUBLESHOOTING, USER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net scatter-gather (N5).** A search is asked of every node that holds other data of the kind (shards; a replica of a node already asked is
  not), each on its own data (`GET /api/v1/net/search`, one hop, `scatter-budget` 3 s), and merged as one answer: the union with an entity once, ordered
  and cut as one node would, `scanned` and `matched` summed, a node that failed, was late or is down named in `failed` with `partial: true`, and `nodes`
  (who answered what) for administrators. A search of a kind whose pack is not on the node is scattered and merged there too. The type-ahead asks every
  reachable node within `suggest-budget` (150 ms). On trades split three ways (one on two nodes) every node answers the same union (grouped pages:
  below). NET.md section 7, API_GUIDE, TROUBLESHOOTING, USER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net live relay and BI (N6).** A live view of an entity held on a peer ticks on the node that shows it: one stream from the peer
  (`GET /api/v1/net/subscribe/{kind}/{id}`, masked there for the user) feeds the viewers of the same roles, opened again when it breaks; the topic hub
  keeps an upstream per entity and role set while the cluster runs. A BI report's requests go to the node that holds the kinds its datasets read (the
  report listed in that node's digest): the same report on a node with no data answers what the node with the lake answers. Source SPI unchanged;
  `TopicHub.partition`. NET.md section 7, API_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net user state (N7).** A user's saved searches, layouts, pivots, workspaces and settings written on one node are copied to every other
  node within a second (`POST /api/v1/net/state`, the newest write winning), so they follow the user after a failover; a node that joins or comes
  back is given what it lacks. Alerts, monitors and scheduled reports stay where they were made (they act). `user-state: off` in routing.yaml for a
  cluster with one shared identity database. Admin → Net → Members shows what was sent, applied and waits. Pushed gossip now tells the node of joins
  and departures as its own exchanges do. NET.md section 7, API_GUIDE, USER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net grouped pages across nodes.** A page a derived connector builds (a counterparty's trades, a book's P&L) is gathered from every
  node that holds the kind it groups, with the scatter-gather of a search: only nodes the caller's roles reach, no replica asked twice, each
  node's share masked there and the page masked again where it is shown (`GET /api/v1/net/group/{kind}/{id}`). Exact, with a member two nodes
  hold counted once, for groups up to `max-members`; larger groups combine each node's totals. A node that did not answer makes the page
  partial (`partial`, `missing`). NET.md section 7, API_GUIDE, USER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net: the node that answered, for administrators.** *How this view was built* has a line **Node** for an administrator naming the
  node that built the view and how (local, data from a peer, forwarded from a node, scatter-gather from several), on the view and every frame
  of its live stream; nobody else sees it, so a user cannot tell which server answered. The finance pack's book P&L Sutra gained the panel.
  NET.md, USER_GUIDE (How this view was built).
- **Drishti*net docs.** Tutorial 19, a cluster of three (UI, CLI and YAML; failover); the Windows QA guide's optional second server; README and
  Help → About. NET.md's command outputs are the CLI's real ones.
- **No JVM leaves native libraries in `/tmp`.** Since JDK 17 `Files.createTempFile` uses the `java.io.tmpdir` the JVM started with, so the server setting the property at start never moved DuckDB (60 MB a copy), RocksDB, CatBoost or Maven's jansi: every JVM killed without a clean exit left its copy in `/tmp` (about 590 files, 22 GB, once; the per-user quota filled and Chromium crashed). Every JVM Drishti or its build starts now gets `-Djava.io.tmpdir=` on its command line: the test forks `<module>/target/tmp` (the parent pom's surefire `argLine`; `mvn clean` removes it), Maven itself `<repository>/data/tmp/maven` (`./mvnw`; `DRISHTI_MAVEN_TMP`), the console test stacks a folder under their own `tmp_path`, and the tools, run configurations, `tools/windows/start-server.ps1`, the scripts `tools/packmake.py` writes, the loaders, the benchmarks and `deploy/server.Dockerfile` (in `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`) the server's temp base `<data dir>/tmp/server`. The Python launchers share one helper, `tools/javacmd.py` (`java_cmd`); a guard test fails on a test or tool that starts java without it. At start the server warns, in its log and on Admin, Health, when the flag was not passed (with the exact flag to add), and removes stale native-library copies from its own temp base (never `/tmp`; skipped quietly on Windows when held). The documented start commands carry the flag (README, GETTING_STARTED, OPERATIONS, CONFIGURATION, TROUBLESHOOTING, the Windows QA guide, tutorial 1); CONFIGURATION, *Pass `-Djava.io.tmpdir=` to Java*, says why.
- **Drishti*net: an open page keeps ticking through a failover.** With several servers (`backend.urls`) a live view whose stream broke, or ended
  because its server stopped, is opened again on the next server (up to 12 times in a row, a second apart and growing) instead of turning the view
  *Static*; the browser is sent the view afresh and keeps ticking, with no reload. Measured on three real servers: the console moved off a stopped
  server in 0.8 s and the open page kept ticking. The net browser suites' fixes: the stand-in suite's wait no longer evaluates a bare string (the
  console's CSP refuses it), the three-server stack starts its JVMs through `java_cmd` (its native libraries in its own folder, not `/tmp`) and
  times the failover from the stop, not from the process's exit (a server with a live stream open waits up to 30 s in its graceful shutdown).
  NET.md sections 8 and 10.
- **Docs reviewed for 2.4.0 and 2.5.0.** The changed flows re-checked deep (README, docs/README, BI_GUIDE, REPORTS, DATASETS, PYTHON_BI, EXPRESSIONS, USER_GUIDE, ARCHITECTURE, IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN, API_GUIDE, TROUBLESHOOTING, GETTING_STARTED, PERFORMANCE, OPERATIONS, tutorials 10, 12, 14 and 17), the rest by name: UPPER_SNAKE fields in the hand-written examples, the seven packs and the retired ones marked, boards as report pages (no board designer, no board kind), Drishti*net built, the release order 2.6.0 to 3.0, every `drishti.py` command in the docs checked against the CLI's own help; a troubleshooting section for BI Studio, themes, Transforms and the Python console; IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN lists 2.2.0 to 2.5.0. The help centre's cards say what 2.4.0 and 2.5.0 added, with short addresses (`/help/bi-studio`, `transforms`, `python-console`, `themes`, `drishti-net`) and F1 on the BI designers, Analyse, Transforms and the Python console opening their own section; Help → About gains BI Studio and Python and loses Perspective. The Design-and-Evidence deck (docs/publications) is current to 2.5.0, with slides for BI, BI Studio, Python data prep, Drishti*net and the 35.7-million-trade run.
- **Drishti*net: a stopped node gives named answers, never "not found" or a page that looks whole** (found by the three-server soak). For about half
  a second after a node stops, while it is suspect, a trade only it holds was answered `404 DRS-1001 no source holds` when another node that does not
  hold it was asked: a read that every asked peer answers "not here" now names the holder that is down (`502 DRS-5052 ... cannot be reached now:
  drishti-c (suspect)`), and a node asked by a peer for data it does not hold says "not here" (404) instead of a 502 that named itself. A grouped page
  (a counterparty's trades) gathered with a node down was partial in its document only; the view now carries `provenance.partial` and
  `provenance.missing` and the page shows a **Partial** banner naming the server, for every reader. The console no longer moves off a server
  that answers 502/503 about another node (`DRS-5050` to `DRS-5059`): with one node down, every view of its data made the console flap between
  the healthy servers. Tests: `SourceRouterFallbackTest`, `NetForwardTest`, `NetGroupedPagesTest`, `test_cluster_failover.py`,
  `test_view_macros.py`. NET.md sections 7, 8 and 12, USER_GUIDE.
- **Drishti*net: a console that moves to another server keeps everyone signed in** (found by the soak). Console sign-in sessions lived only in
  the identity database of the node that opened them, so after a failover (or a session that a console worker pinned to another node) the next
  server did not know the session and the person was sent to sign in again, and pages outside the sign-in gate (query by form) answered
  `401 DRS-5010 missing bearer token`. Sessions are now copied with the user's state (`POST /api/v1/net/state`, namespace `sessions`: the SHA-256 of
  the id, the user and the expiry, never the id); a sign-out or an administrator's ending a user's sessions on one node ends them on all; a node that
  joins is given the sessions that stand (`SessionStore.Listener`, `adopt`). The console's sign-in gate now covers `/form` and `/forms` (it sent
  an anonymous request there). `tools/bench/users.py` counts a page that sent the user to sign in as a failure. `NetUserStateTest`. NET.md
  sections 7 and 8, CONFIGURATION.
- **Drishti*net soak on real processes** (`tools/bench/net_soak.py`): three servers from one seed (two reading one lake, one its own), a console
  given the first, `tools/bench/users.py` load, a shard holder killed and restarted, then the console's own server, every answer compared with a
  single server holding all the data. Results in NET.md section 13.
- **Admin → Net → Members: the hash ring.** Beside the member table (above it on a phone) an SVG donut with one arc per member, coloured
  by state from the theme's tokens, sized by the kinds whose data each node holds (the capability digest; equal when none reports any, as the
  caption says), this node outlined, the console's server banded; labels with the node id and packs, the kinds held on hover; the centre counts
  alive / total. Clicking an arc selects its row (and a row its arc); it redraws with the page's 5 s refresh, a member joining grows in and one
  removed fades out. `/admin/net/api/members` carries `holds`. `test_admin_net.py`, four browser tests in `test_admin_net_browser.py`; NET.md
  section 9, tutorial 19 picture 1 (taken on the drishti-stack cluster), the deck's Drishti*net slide (drishti-b left).
- **A ready-made local cluster: `drishti-stack/`.** Three servers and a console on one machine from one file, `stack.yaml` (each node's
  port, heap, packs, lake and the connectors it switches off; adding a node is one entry): `start.sh` starts what is not running (and makes
  what is missing: the secrets, a node's net folder, the synthetic ENDUR, CALYPSO and MUREX lakes with `tools/bench/trades.py`, and
  `run/server.jar`, a copy of the newest build, so a rebuild never replaces the jar under a running server), `stop.sh` stops by the process
  ids it recorded (checking each is still the stack's own), `status.sh` shows the processes, ports, members and the console's servers.
  Each node's `nodes/<node>/net/` is checked in with `secret: ${DRISHTI_NET_SECRET}` only; the token, session and cluster keys are in
  `drishti-stack/secrets.env`, made with `openssl rand -hex 32` (mode 600) and git-ignored with `run/` and any `net-key`, and
  `tools/test_no_hidden_names.py` fails if one of them or a jar is tracked. Node data and lakes go under `data/stack/`.
  [drishti-stack/README.md](drishti-stack/README.md), NET.md section 2, tutorial 19, GETTING_STARTED, README.
- **News falls back to the committed snapshot with offline mode off.** A connector of an internet plugin (`drishti.offline.plugins`:
  `news`, `yfinance`, `feed`) that its file switches off (as shipped: `DRISHTI_NEWS_ENABLED` unset) has its kinds read from the other
  connectors that serve them, exactly as in offline mode (`SourceRegistry.standIns`); Admin → Connectors shows it as *off (served from
  the snapshot)* with `servedBy`, and an entity they do not hold is `DRS-1001` naming them (and saying when they have no table for the
  kind yet: a demo lake built before the snapshot was committed, rebuilt with `drishti.py lake demo --only news-and-markets`), no longer
  `DRS-1002 connector news is disabled`. `OfflineModeTest`, `ConnectorBadgeTest`, `test_news_browser.py` (the topic page's headlines
  with the live connectors off and offline mode off); the pack guide, CONFIGURATION (offline mode), TROUBLESHOOTING (`DRS-1002`).
- **Tutorial 19 with real captures.** Ten pictures of Admin → Net on three real servers made by the picture script (`tools/docs/shots/t19.py`,
  the console tests' own cluster: members, the console's servers, Files, a trade only c holds opened through a, Explain, the capability map,
  Access, Security, the cluster after a stopped, the page at 390 px), the real output of `drishti.py net members`, the servers' and the
  console's log lines, the explain answer, and a *You should see* in every step; NET.md section 9 shows the members after a failover; the
  Windows QA guide's Drishti*net section says what the tester should see. The tutorials test counts nineteen.
- **The Design-and-Evidence deck's Drishti*net slide** shows Admin → Net after a failover (a node left, the console on another server), its four
  measures in a 2×2 beside it.
- **Tutorial 14 pictures 25 to 29 remade** on a demo lake with `NOTIONAL` (`drishti.py lake demo --as-of 2026-10-16`, outside the repository,
  then removed): the Transform input, profile and steps, the Python console and its promoted run (12,751 live trades of 15,000) match the text.
- **Tutorial 16 picture 11 remade** (the React sample, after `fetch-vendor.sh --only react`, whose builds stay git-ignored): the search types
  `1000001`, an ENDUR trade id of the production-shaped data (it typed `END-1000`, an id of the data before 2.3.0, and found nothing).
- **The full browser rehearsal (2.5.0)**, every console browser file one at a time: a long folder path on Admin → Lakes (the lake roots, the
  settings and history files) widened the page at phone width; it now breaks anywhere. The About tests ran on the genomics pack, which moved to
  `examples/packs`, and had been skipping since (the server did not start): they name the folder and read the gloss term `VAR_99`. Three tests made
  steady: the model designer's report drop waits for the designer to be ready (it failed one run in two), the governance save-twice test counts the
  history once it has loaded and waits for each save's own row, and `test_tree_rows.py` starts Chromium with a short `TMPDIR` (with a long one its
  process socket path passes the system's limit and Chromium aborts printing nothing, the failure seen in a full run). In a second full pass,
  the About editor's keyboard test failed once in six runs: it now waits for the starter text before tabbing on. Two full passes: 635 and 651
  tests passed in about an hour each.
- **Drishti*net: a view's linked entities held on a peer show as themselves, not *pending*.** The 40 ms `drishti.graph.link-budget` is
  the wait for this node's own sources; a linked entity none of them holds is asked of the peers and waits routing.yaml's new
  `link-budget` (1 s, from the read's start), so its badge and a panel that reads it (`source:`) are there on the first view, exactly as on
  the node that holds it. A slow local source still keeps 40 ms, a forwarded request asks no peer, and with Drishti*net off nothing changes
  (`SourceRouter.Fallback.budget`). The page still does not say which server answered, except to an administrator. `NetLinksTest` (three
  servers in one JVM; with the old budget every link of the trade was *pending* on the nodes without the data), `SourceRouterFallbackTest`.
- **Drishti*net: a search counts every entity once, from any node.** The `file` connector names its table (`file:<folder>#<fingerprint>`, the
  fingerprint a SHA-256 of the kind's files with their sizes and times, taken at each rescan), as Delta does, so two servers reading one folder
  are replicas and a search asks one of them; a source that holds nothing of a kind (a Delta connector with no table for it, a folder with no
  file of it: `SourcePlugin.holdsNone`) is left out of the capability digest, so it no longer makes a node a holder. And whatever the
  connectors, the merge counts an entity two nodes matched once: each node's share lists its matches' ids (up to 20,000; above that the
  overlap is taken from the rows both returned), so `matched` and `scanned` are no longer summed twice; the ids never reach the client.
  `NetSharedLakeTest` (two readers of one lake asked once; every count equal to the union on every node), `NetScatterTest` (the trade two
  nodes hold now counts once in `matched` and `scanned`), `FileTableIdentityTest`. NET.md sections 7 and 12 (both limitations removed),
  DRISHTI_NET.md sections 7, 8 and 10, CONFIGURATION, FILE_CONNECTOR, TROUBLESHOOTING.

## 2.4.0 — BI Studio, report schema v2 and boards as a report layout, Python data prep, the browser Python console, query by form at scale (2026-10-19)

- **Drill:** the failed console tests are re-run from the run's own summary (the pytest cache is off, so `--last-failed` re-ran the
  whole suite), one file at a time under a 20-minute limit; the Java 25 run re-runs a failed test once and reports it as a flake.
  [DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md).

- **Report schema v2 (R6).** A visual's `format:` is typed per visual family: one JSON Schema per family, written in YAML in the server
  (`bi/report/format/*.yaml`: chart, other-chart, card, table, matrix, slicer, static, code, and the shared common), checked on every report (a bad
  value of a key new in 2.4.0 is `DRS-9032`; a bad value of a 2.3 key and an unknown key are warnings, so a stored report never stops opening) and served
  at `GET /api/v1/bi/reports/format-schemas`. New keys: a subtitle, the title's size, a border, a shadow, a background and padding on every visual; label
  density and position, 100% stacking, the value axis (min, max, log, gridlines), the category axis (rotation), colours per series; a card's number size and
  label, a KPI's *better when*; a table's banding, wrapping and text size; a slicer's tile look and **sync group**; a text's alignment.
  **Conditional formatting rules** (`format.rules`): a rule on the measure or on another one (text and background), 2- and 3-stop colour scales, icons
  (arrows, traffic lights, signs, flags) and a colour per value of a field, on charts, cards, tables and matrices. **Theme items** (kind `theme`,
  `DRS-9036`; `GET /api/v1/bi/themes`, `/themes/{name}`, `POST /themes/validate`; shipped *Corporate* and *Print* in `config/bi/themes`): a palette,
  page and text colours, good and bad colours, a font and the format defaults of each kind of visual; a report names one with `theme: corporate`.
  The **interactions matrix** per page (`interactions: { source: { target: filter | highlight | none } }`, slicers too), highlight for lines, areas and
  combos; **sync groups** for slicers across pages; **tooltip pages** (`tooltip: true` on a page, `tooltip: <page>` on a visual); **small multiples**
  (the `split` well on bar, column, line and area); **matrix depth** (subtotals at every level of the row fields, true totals per level, stepped or tabular,
  opening at a level, values on rows). [REPORTS.md](docs/guides/REPORTS.md#5-format) sections 5, 6, 8 and 20.
- **Boards are a report layout (R7).** A page with `layout: board` holds **tiles** (`type: tile`, `from: { report, visual }`, `alert:`) beside the report's
  own visuals, which take its slicers, cross-filter and drill; a report of tiles needs no model; `board: { rotate, notify }`. The `board` kind is gone:
  **stored boards become reports at start** (`drishti.bi.migrate.on-start`, step `boards`: the id kept, the text before a saved state, the published text
  the next version, audited `bi-boards-migrated`, what cannot be converted listed in Admin, BI settings); board files are read as reports until converted
  (`drishti.py bi migrate --boards FILE... --write`, `POST /api/v1/bi/boards/convert`, `POST /api/v1/bi/migrate/boards`); the samples are
  `config/bi/reports/desk-wall.report.yaml` and `python-wall.report.yaml`; `/bi/b/<id>` and `/bi/design/board/<id>` open the report viewer and designer; an
  item created with kind `board` is stored as a report with board pages; tile alerts and their notifications, publishing checks, bundles, consumers and
  impact work on board pages. [BOARDS.md](docs/guides/BOARDS.md).
- **BI Studio as one product (console).** The report, model and theme designers share the Studio shell: the **Explorer** of your items (reports, boards,
  models, datasets, themes, Python) with a search and New, the **open items** as tabs, the designer's properties, and the **dock** with **Problems** (the
  server's Check of the draft a second after each change, each linked to its visual) and **Query** (the selected visual's model query and SQL). The
  **Format pane is generated from the format schemas**; the **conditional formatting editor** (rule, colour scale, icons, colour per value, chosen from
  lists and colour pickers); the **theme picker** and the **theme designer** (`/bi/design/theme/<id>`, a live preview); **Edit interactions** (in Format,
  and Filter / Highlight / None over each visual on the canvas); **tooltip pages** (New tooltip page; a popover over the hovered mark in the viewer, filtered
  to it); **small multiples** (one chart per value, one canvas); the **matrix** at every level (Expand all, Collapse all, stepped or tabular, values on
  rows, banding, data bars, rules); board pages in the report viewer (tiles, alert colours, kiosk and rotation) and designer (the tile editor, a live tile
  preview); the 2.3 board viewer and designer are removed. Embedded `<drishti-report>` draws the new matrix, rules and themes, and a 2.1 Perspective grid as
  Drishti's grid of the server's rows, as the console does (Drishti Elements 1.4.2).
  [BI_GUIDE.md section 26](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md#26-bi-studio-authoring-as-one-product-wave-c-240).
- **Tests.** `ReportSchemaV2Test`, `ThemeParserTest`, `BoardsAsReportsTest` (the 2.3 boards convert to reports that read back to the same boards; the shipped
  samples are the conversions), `BoardMigrationApiTest` (the start-up conversion); the Wave C acceptance in Chromium, `test_bi_studio_browser.py`: a Power BI
  author builds a desk pack (a matrix with a colour scale and icons, small multiples, a tooltip page, Edit interactions, slicers in a sync group, a theme) and a
  theme without the YAML tab, and the viewer draws it. **2.3.1 housekeeping:** the console's BI test stacks serve the market-risk valuations under the
  2.3.0 UPPER_SNAKE names (a `risk-store` file connector beside the test's own `trading-store`), the BI browser suites use `trade.DESK`, `trade.MTM` ... and the
  daily-snapshot semantics of 2.3.0 (a report over one business day).
- **Query by form at scale (2.4.0).** A kind's form is kept per data version and per whether the caller sees masks, instead of
  being built from a thousand rows on every request (twice per console search). On 35.7 million trades with 50 console users:
  form search p50 5.8 s → 0.21 s, the console 56 → 182 pages/s, 0 failures. [PERFORMANCE.md](docs/admin/PERFORMANCE.md),
  [QUERY_BY_FORM.md](docs/guides/QUERY_BY_FORM.md).
- **A search on a date with no data says so (2.4.0).** When no source holds the kind on the business date, the search answers
  `noData {businessDate, latestDate}` (new `SourcePlugin.latestDate`, cheap: a Delta table's newest date from its cached layout),
  and the console's form and search pages say no data is loaded for that date, name the newest date that has data and offer
  **Search on** it. [API_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md), [QUERY_BY_FORM.md](docs/guides/QUERY_BY_FORM.md).
- **Toronto business calendar `CATO` (2.4.0)**, in the server and the Python generator (golden 2026 and 2027 dates in both
  tests). [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md).
- **A restated day while serving, measured (2.4.0).** 400,000 ENDUR trades with 10 % amended reloaded under 30 users: 2 min 54 s,
  202 requests/s, 0 failures, the new version served within a minute. [PERFORMANCE.md](docs/admin/PERFORMANCE.md).
- **Designs for the next releases:** Drishti*net, several servers acting as one, for 2.5.0
  ([DRISHTI_NET.md](docs/architecture/DRISHTI_NET.md)); GenUI ([GENUI.md](docs/architecture/GENUI.md)) and AI management
  ([AI_MANAGEMENT.md](docs/architecture/AI_MANAGEMENT.md)) for 2.7.0 (AI), sharing and integration with field parameters for 2.6.0; the release order in
  [BI_DESIGN.md §12.2](docs/architecture/BI_DESIGN.md#122-the-three-waves).
- **Python data prep, step by step (2.4.0, wave C).** A Python Transform's input may be a **pack kind read straight through a connector**, as the
  reader (`{kind, pack?, connector?, fields?, where?, limit?}`: the kind's entitlements, row rules and masks apply; checked on validate, in the lineage),
  and `POST /api/v1/bi/python/input` answers one input's rows as the caller. **BI → Transforms** (`/bi/transform`) builds a Transform without typing YAML:
  inputs, then steps from a **snippets library** of 21 (append, unpivot, pivot, fill down, split, replace, dedupe, type fixes, joins, **as-of joins of
  prices and FX**, business-day alignment, tenor buckets; each value written as a literal, each snippet with an example run in pandas and in the sandbox)
  or by hand; after every change the steps run once in the console's sandbox and each step shows its rows, first rows and a **profile** (nulls,
  distinct, key hint, min and max, top values, histogram, text that looks like numbers or dates with *Fix the type*), the inputs' key hints and a
  failing step's line; the Python item and the Python dataset are written, read back and saved as drafts. Analyse has a **Profile** tab
  (`POST /bi/explore/profile`). **Warm workers**: the fork-server starts with the console (`bi.python.warm`). CLI: `drishti.py bi transform input |
  snippets | snippet`. [PYTHON_BI.md section 17](docs/guides/PYTHON_BI.md#17-data-prep-the-transform-editor-240),
  [tutorial 14, step 12](docs/tutorials/14-python-and-analyse.md#step-12--prepare-raw-trades-step-by-step-the-transform-editor-240).
  Tests: `TransformInputsApiTest`, `test_pyrunner_steps.py`, `test_transform_editor.py`, `test_cli_bitransform.py`, and Chromium
  (`test_data_prep_browser.py`: a Transform reading a connector built step by step with a preview at each step and no YAML typed, equal to a
  computation from the same trades).
- **The browser Python console (2.4.0, wave C).** **BI → Python console** (`/pyconsole`, not a BI page: Analyse and every `/bi` page still compute
  nothing in the browser) runs your own Python in Calc's Pyodide worker on a **masked extract** of a model (`POST /pyconsole/extract`, the reader's rows,
  `bi.pyconsole.max_rows`); **Promote to the server** runs the same code on the console backend over the extract rebuilt for you, compares the answers
  and keeps it as a notebook draft (`POST /pyconsole/promote`, bi-author). [ANALYSE.md section 16](docs/guides/ANALYSE.md#16-the-browser-python-console),
  [tutorial 14, step 13](docs/tutorials/14-python-and-analyse.md#step-13--your-own-python-in-the-browser-the-python-console-240). Chromium: an analyst's
  extract arrives masked, an author's result is promoted and gives the same answer.
- **Offline quote and news pages checked** (`DRISHTI_OFFLINE=true`, the committed snapshot): the topic page's note now says that offline mode shows the
  captured headlines (it said the tables stay empty); the browser test also checks that the company page draws the whole year of closes, ending at the
  quote's own price, with no policy violation or script error.
- **A board tile's *Alert on* is kept when it is set first (2.4.0).** In the tile editor, *Alert on*, the hysteresis and *Notify* set before a
  threshold were dropped (an alert is written only once it has a threshold); they now wait for it ([BOARDS.md](docs/guides/BOARDS.md)).
- **Model designer (2.4.0):** a dataset's own id column (a trade's `TRADE_ID`, which references `trade`) no longer makes the dataset a dimension, so a
  model of trades and desks starts with the trades as its fact; on a phone the Measures tab's *Quick calculation* row wraps instead of widening the page.
  A pack's heading link on Forms and the packs in a pack's detail list are 44 px tap targets.
- **Analyse: a filter's picker lists its values again (2.4.0).** A field put in *Filters* showed only *(all)*: the values the console sends with the
  pivot redrew the field chips but not the filter zone. The zone is drawn again when they arrive ([ANALYSE.md](docs/guides/ANALYSE.md)).
- **A trade id in a report table opens the trade again (2.4.0).** A column that references a kind links to it in the terminal, as a key column does: the
  model's description names the kind from the dataset's `references:` or, for a table over a pack kind, from the packs' link graph, so the sample
  valuations (`trading-risk-trades`, keyed by `VALUATION_ID` since 2.3.0) open their `TRADE_ID` as the trade again ([REPORTS.md](docs/guides/REPORTS.md)).
- **A console with live pages open stops promptly (2.4.0).** With several worker processes (`server.workers`, 4 by default) a stop waited for every
  open live stream, which never ends by itself: the worker stayed alive serving a silent stream, and the open reports and boards still said *Live*. A stop
  now closes the streams after `server.shutdown_seconds` (`DRISHTI_CONSOLE_SHUTDOWN_SECONDS`, 5 s); the pages say *Disconnected* and reconnect when the
  console is back ([OPERATIONS.md section 3.4](docs/admin/OPERATIONS.md#34-first-start-by-hand)).
- **A live channel stays one connection with several console workers (2.4.0).** A page's later calls on its open live channel (a view added or
  removed, `POST /api/channel/{cid}`; an embedded element's `POST /embed/v1/channel/{cid}` and its fresh token) reached whichever worker the
  connection landed on, and three in four found no such channel: the page reopened its channel, and an embedded `<drishti-view>` said
  *reconnecting* and repainted. A channel's id now names the worker that holds it, and the others pass the call on to it over a loopback port of
  its own, with a key the launcher makes for its workers (`core/channel_relay.py`; a single-process console has neither)
  ([OPERATIONS.md section 3.4](docs/admin/OPERATIONS.md#34-first-start-by-hand)).
- **A BI table that names its connector reads that connector only (2.4.0).** When the connector could not scan the table's columns (a column not in
  its layout) the documents were listed and read through every connector of the kind, so a table over one lake could answer another source's
  entities (the finance sample trades instead of the lake's own, for the latest date). The slow path now lists and reads the named connector only, for
  the newest date it holds when the query names none ([DATASETS.md](docs/guides/DATASETS.md)). `ModelAcceptanceTest` promotes every column its model
  reads, so its standing query sees the reload of its own lake again.
- **Personal API tokens and the 2.4.0 BI endpoints.** `POST /api/v1/bi/boards/convert`, `/bi/themes/validate` and `/bi/python/input` store nothing
  and are open to a token as reads (so `drishti.py bi migrate --boards FILE` converts files with a token); `POST /api/v1/bi/migrate/boards` is never a
  token's, as the other stored-item migrations.
- **The BI editors' examples use the 2.3.0 names (2.4.0).** The formula hint of the model designer (`sum(trade.MTM, where=trade.TRADE_STATUS ==
  'VALIDATED')`) and the dataset wizard's row filter and worked-out column examples (`MTM / NOTIONAL * 10000`, `related(desk.NAME)`) were in the
  lowercase 2.2 names; the pictures of EXPRESSIONS, DATASETS, BI_GUIDE, REPORTS and ANALYSE are taken again on the UPPER_SNAKE samples and their
  text follows (*Sum of MTM*, `trade.DESK`).
- **`sutra lint` and `sutra test` of a pack ignore the site's added packs (2.4.0).** A pack checked from the command line was loaded beside the packs
  an administrator had added in Admin → Packs (`<data dir>/packs/added.yaml`), so a pack that defines `counterparty` failed beside a site's `core`
  (`kind counterparty is defined by both pack ...`); `drishti.py pack make` reported its pack check failed for that reason ([CLI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/CLI_GUIDE.md)).
- **A new board from the workspace says where it opens (2.4.0).** The New item form still said a board *is laid out in the board designer* and
  offered *Create and open in the board designer*; a board is a report with board pages, so the hint and the button now say it opens in the report
  designer, no model needed ([BI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md)).
- **The workbench completes UPPER_SNAKE paths typed in lower case (2.4.0).** The inspector's expression fields matched a `$` or `@` prefix
  case by case, so `$.sch` offered nothing on a document whose fields are UPPER_SNAKE (the convention since 2.3.0); a prefix now matches in any
  case and the path is written as the document has it (`$.SCHEDULE`), as function names already were ([PANELS.md](docs/guides/PANELS.md)).
- **The elements demo host shows live valuations (2.4.0).** Since 2.3.0 a trade is static and the market-risk valuations tick, so
  `tools/elements-demo` shows `TVAL-1000008` (and `TVAL-1000002` in its side column) from the trading, counterparty-risk and market-risk packs; its
  search box knows `TVAL`. The `<drishti-view>` suites (`test_embed_elements_browser.py`, `test_embed_controls_browser.py`) run against it: masks
  on the trade the valuation values, tables, pivot and a live subscription built with control values on a netting set, the controls of a trade on
  its *Events* panel ([tools/elements-demo/README.md](tools/elements-demo/README.md), [tutorial 16](docs/tutorials/16-embed-drishti.md)).

## 2.3.0 — BI with filter context, time and live push; production-shaped trades from three source systems; a demo from a fresh clone (2026-10-17)

- **Ids are text; numeric ids in files are read as text.** The generators write `TRADE_ID` (ENDUR digits included), `LEG_ID`, `FLOW_ID`, `RESET_ID` and `EVENT_ID` as strings (data/testdata and the trading and market-risk samples regenerated, same digits). `lake build` reads a numeric id as its exact digits (`1000008`, never `1000008.0`) in the id column, the document and an id/key column it promotes (always STRING now; before, an all-number `TRADE_ID` became a DOUBLE column); `lake demo --with-real` turns a real extract's numeric ids into text; the Delta connector reads an older table's numeric id column (DOUBLE, LONG, INT, DECIMAL) as id text. No id prefix or shape is assumed.
- **The news-and-markets and world-economy packs work with no internet.** A real market snapshot is committed:
  `data/testdata/news-and-markets/quote/` (the twenty companies' quotes, a year of daily bars) and `news/` (the companies' and the
  fifteen topics' headlines), captured through the pack's own `yfinance` and `news` connectors by the new
  `drishti.py testdata capture-market` (`tools/testdata/capture_market.py`: a scratch server, two seconds between calls, backoff on
  429; dated and labelled `SNAPSHOT` in every document; the data belongs to Yahoo Finance, Google News and the publishers, for
  demonstration only). `lake demo` builds it into `news/quote` and `news/news`, which `news-store` now serves beside the topics and
  companies; the quote and headline pages add a **Snapshot** note and *As of* / *Fetched* end in *(snapshot captured <date>)*.
  **Offline mode**, `DRISHTI_OFFLINE=true` (`drishti.offline.enabled`, `drishti.offline.plugins`: `news,yfinance,feed`): connectors of
  internet plugins never start, whatever their files say, and show **off (offline mode)** in Admin → Connectors (state `OFFLINE`, no
  problem, no retry); the run configuration **Drishti server (demo lakes)** sets it. world-economy was already offline (its thirteen
  kinds are committed samples in `macro-store` and `civic-store`). [data/testdata/README.md](data/testdata/README.md#the-market-snapshot-news-and-markets-offline),
  [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#news-and-markets-and-world-economy-offline), [CONFIGURATION](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md#drishtioffline--offline-mode-no-internet).
  Tests: `OfflineModeTest` (the internet plugin is never started or called; the store serves the kind), `test_capture_market.py`
  (a stub server), the snapshot guards in `test_testdata.py`, and a Chromium test of a company page, a quote, a topic and a news page
  offline with the fakes receiving no request.

- **Shared blocks in the Build designer (step 1).** A Sutra written with shared blocks (`{ use: legs@1, with: {...} }`, the 130 trading Sutras) opens drawn: the preview is the server's own expansion, the canvas no longer says "Add your first panel", and the YAML check knows `use:`/`with:` (the schema's `$defs.use`) and every panel's `when:` (no false problems; a real `DRS-2048` says how to fix it). A Design of a pack Sutra with no sample gets the first record of the pack's `samples/<kind>.jsonl` its match holds for (`POST /builder/designs/{id}/samples {pack: true}`; Samples says "Sample picked from the pack: MX-…; add your own"). The "How this view was built" link carries the page's record (`&kind=&id=`) like About's. Design operations count a use as one written item (adding, moving, removing a written panel lands at its own place; a block's panel is refused with a pointer to the block), new `setBlock` operation sets a use's `with:` or removes it; `GET /builder/designs/{id}/blocks` lists the uses with the panels they bring, `GET /rachana/blocks[/{name}/{version}]` reads blocks. Canvas block groups, Open block and the inspector follow in step 2.


- **Lake maintenance in Admin → Lakes.** Tick tables, **Dry run** (files and size before → after, dates retention drops, files vacuum
  removes) or **Maintain now** in the background with progress and cancel; settings per table and schedules (day-and-time picker or cron
  text, or after every build; a run missed while down runs once at start) saved in `<data>/lakes/lake-maintenance.yaml`, the file
  `lake maintain` now reads by default; history on the page and in `lake maintain --history`. Only tables `lake build` made are
  maintained (others are their publisher's; `--force-foreign` overrides, with a warning); changed tables send the data-load signal.
  [LAKE_MAINTENANCE.md](docs/admin/LAKE_MAINTENANCE.md)
- **About this page opens the Sutra.** In *Why the page looks like this*, the Sutra that drew the page is a link that opens it in the
  designer with the record on screen as its sample; the Sutras not chosen open there too.
- **How this view was built links on.** The panel's Sutra opens in the designer and its Source opens the connector's page.
- **A Sutra link opens the Sutra at one click.** A link on Drishti's own pages to a Sutra (How this view was built, About this page,
  Admin → Packs) goes straight into the designer on that Sutra, reusing your own design of the same Sutra and version (the record
  you came from added as a sample); the same address from another site still asks first, now saying *Open the Sutra … in the designer*.
- **A rebuilt lake is never served from old BI figures.** `drishti.py lake demo` sends the data-load signal for every table and business
  date it wrote (`--server`, default `DRISHTI_SERVER` then `http://localhost:18480`; `--token-file` / `DRISHTI_TOKEN`); when no server is
  reachable, or one refuses, it leaves `<bi-store>/lake-rebuilt.json` and the server clears its stored columns and rollups at its next
  start (`lake build` leaves the same stamp when run without `--signal`, unless `--no-stamp`). A running server also notices by itself:
  each stored partition records the Delta files it was read from, and a date whose files changed is read again (other dates are kept),
  cached answers included. The Delta connector keys its own caches by version *and* commit time, so a table removed and written again
  (its versions start at 0 again) is never answered from the old table's ids, documents or columns.
- **Wave B QA fixes.** A visual is never cut silently: *first 500 of N* with **Show all**, and a time series is asked by week, month,
  quarter or year so its whole range fits (Trades overview's *Notional by trade date* covers every date; `trade.TRADE_DATE.month_start`
  and the other period parts are dates). The Cashflow ladder's model is fixed: trades a dimension, legs, cashflows and the new payments
  table many-to-one to it (so *by source system* works), *Net payments* and *Largest payment* in USD equivalent and signed (paid −,
  received +), scheduled notional apart; the absurd 66.9 trillion was every period's principal in seven currencies added up, checked
  against an independent sum of the test data. A cross-currency swap's exchange no longer books in the deal currency. A daily snapshot is
  a state: over a window it takes the last date, never a sum of days; the trades carry `NOTIONAL` (first leg, its currency). A
  calculation item keeps its own format (*Share* reads as a percent). Live chips say **Listening** until changes flow and **Too many live
  queries: close a tab** when refused; a closed tab ends its standing queries (keepalive `DELETE`) and an unread one is dropped after 60 s
  (`drishti.bi.mq.idle-close`). Counterparty, portfolio, legal-entity and index pages list every trade (paged, *Trades n of N*, a status
  filter); a netting set holds only live trades (its MTM, EE and CVA too) and lists every member with its status; Compare wraps instead of
  clipping and shows the changes inside cash flows, resets and events, each with where it sits; legs say Pay / receive from the booking
  or from the side (a buyer pays fixed); a floating flow's rate is its fixings' average; a fixed leg has no empty fixings chart; product
  types read in words with the code; trade search shows status and trade date; deal and counterparty headers wrap, never cut; Endur deals
  always carry legal entity, UEN and trader, and cash deals their payments; KYC fields formatted, collateral signs labelled, a CSA links
  its netting sets and collateral; the trail shows the last five views. Lakes screen and `drishti.py lake status`: every table with rows,
  files, size, partitions, business dates, latest date and last build. Designer: a second measure added with a card selected starts a new
  card, Escape deselects, Add forms empty after Add; the Quick calculation's balances default to the dataset's business date; the top-bar
  date stops at today; the BI rail wraps on a phone; BI home lists an item once per section; no WARN for the optional `sutras` folder;
  the macro store promotes the columns the economics models read. Tutorial data files use UPPER_SNAKE names; the demo ticker for tutorial
  12 is documented (`DRISHTI_DEMO_ENABLED=true`).
- **Committed test data and one command for the demo lakes.** `data/testdata/` (the one committed folder under `data/`, 21.5 MB
  of `.jsonl.gz`) holds seven business days of every dataset the seven packs read: 5,000 trades per source system (ENDUR with six
  look-alike counterparties, CALYPSO and MUREX samples and clones; unique ids; amendments and new bookings across the days) and
  every kind of core, market-data, counterparty-risk and market-risk, reconciled on the last day; the first day a snapshot, each
  later day its changes (`tools/testdata/make_testdata.py`, `--check`). `drishti.py lake demo` (run configuration **Build demo
  lakes**) rebuilds each day and writes the packs' Delta tables (trades by business date and `PRODUCT_TYPE`), the days moved to end
  on today's business date (`--as-of`), checks the connector files and says what to start next (about 50 s, 800 MB);
  `--with-real FILE` adds real extracts' trades, counterparties, agreements and netting sets to the local lake only. Run
  configuration **Drishti server (demo lakes)** (samples off, `-Xmx4g`); news-and-markets gains a `news-store` Delta connector;
  trade valuations get promoted columns in the risk lake. Guide: [data/testdata/README.md](data/testdata/README.md).
- **Trading Calc snippets on the production-shaped trade**: swap reprice and key rates on the leg's `DISCOUNT_INDEX` curve, the
  swaption from its underlying fixed leg, bond, CDS, a generic option grid, rate shift and peers on the risk engine's `TVAL`
  valuations, and a new payment ladder; the underlying-specific option pricers (the common schema names no underlying) and the
  P&L history were retired; stress-from-sensitivities rebuilds the rate shocks from `TVAL` DV01.

- **UPPER_SNAKE attribute names in every kind of the seven packs.** Counterparties, legal entities, books, desks, traders,
  calendars, agreements, CSAs, netting sets, collateral, margin calls, limits, exposure, CVA, SA-CCR, SIMM, curves, surfaces, FX,
  fixings, equities, bonds, VaR, stress, FRTB, P&L explain, economies, indicators, jurisdictions, elections, news topics and listed
  companies now carry `COUNTERPARTY_ID`, `NETTING_SET_ID`, `NET_MTM`, `EE_PEAK`, `ZERO_RATE`, `TENOR`, `GDP_USD_BN`, `ISO3` …
  (ids keep their values). The generators write them through one boundary (`tools/packgen/common/upper.py`), the Sutras, forms,
  key columns, link fields, about text, Calc snippets and pack tests follow, the BI samples (models, datasets, reports, the
  notebook) read the new columns, and the news, yfinance and public-feed connectors write UPPER_SNAKE documents
  (`com.ash.drishti.common.UpperSnake`). A graph panel reads `ID`/`FROM`/`TO` as well as `id`/`from`/`to`, a panel's default
  field (`open`, `close`, `label`, `value`, `date` …) reads the UPPER_SNAKE attribute when the row has that, and a control's
  options from a list of records read `ID` too. The retired example packs keep their names.
- **Pack samples are one JSON Lines file per kind**: `samples/<kind>.jsonl`, one document a line in the catalogue's order, split
  into `<kind>.part-001.jsonl` … only above 50 MB (`--sample-part-mb`); the seven packs went from 12,195 sample files to 69. The
  demo connector, BI pack models, pack export and import, `drishti.py design`, the lake and stream tools and the tests read both
  layouts (`com.ash.drishti.common.PackSamples`, `tools/packgen/common/packsamples.py`); the generators, the pack wizard and
  `pack_from_jsonl.py` write JSONL; packs in the older `samples/<kind>/<id>.json` layout keep working.
- Help: a dead CHANGELOG anchor to the Perspective chart section and three unused Perspective pictures removed.

- **Production-shaped trades from three source systems.** The trading pack's `trade` is the common schema a production extract has:
  47 upper-snake attributes (`SOURCE_SYSTEM`, `TRADE_ID`, `TRADE_STATUS`, `PRODUCT_TYPE`, `COUNTERPARTY`, `LEGAL_ENTITY`, `PORTFOLIO`,
  `TRADER_ID`, the dates, `CURRENCY` …) with `LEGS[]` (`INDEX`, `CASH_FLOWS[]` -> `RESETS[]`) and `EVENTS[]`, the same for **ENDUR**
  (the commodity desk's deals: `ComSwap`, `ComFut`, `Cash`, `CallNot`, `Commodity`, ids `1000001`…), **CALYPSO** (`CLY-…`) and
  **MUREX** (`MX-…`, the 125 products of the taxonomy between them). No camelCase remains on a trade; the old fields (`tradeId`,
  `productType`, `mtm`, `legs`, `schedule` …) are gone, and a trade's MTM, P&L and DV01 are now market-risk's **trade valuation**
  (`TVAL TVAL-<trade id>`, live). Generators in `tools/packgen/trades` (`make_trades.py --trades N --days D [--lake DIR]`: a week of
  business days with the day's amendments, so Compare has changes). Ids of the samples changed with their systems (`IMG-`, `BBG-`,
  `WSS-`, `END-` are gone: equity and structured are Murex, FX, money markets, fixed income and securities financing Calypso).
  [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#trading).
- **Union connectors.** Several connectors may serve one kind, one source system each (`union: true`): a read finds the entity in
  whichever holds it, a search and the type-ahead list every system in turn, columns, pivots, the search form's drop-downs and BI tables
  over the kind read them all (a store behind the union is still not asked). The Delta connector reads a kind from a named folder
  (`table.trade: trade_endur`). The trading pack ships `trade-endur`, `trade-calypso` and `trade-murex` (replacing `trading-store`);
  `drishti.py lake build --connector trade-endur` (or `--table`) writes the table a connector reads.
  [CONNECTORS.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTORS.md#several-connectors-for-one-kind-a-union).
- **Deal screens and pages for traders and risk analysts.** One deal Sutra per product type: F2 the DES ticket, F3 the legs side by
  side (each with its notional profile, fixings and cash flows opening to their resets), F4 every flow, F5 events by leg with leg and
  date controls, F6 the netted payment ladder and payments by month. New derived pages `CTRD`, `PTRD`, `LTRD`, `ITRD` (a counterparty's,
  portfolio's, legal entity's and index's trades from every system: live trades, payments in 7/30/90 days, notional by product type as a
  donut and by index, maturity ladder, payments by month); `DPNL` desk P&L is gone. Rachana-EL `group()`, `days()`, `today()`; `line`
  `step: true`; `hbar` `shape: donut`; `line`, `area` and `timeline` may sit inside a tab.
- **One Sutra per source system and product type, built from shared blocks.** The trading pack's Sutras match
  `$.SOURCE_SYSTEM == '<SYSTEM>' && $.PRODUCT_TYPE == '<type>'` and sit in `sutras/common/`, `endur/`, `calypso/`, `murex/` and `pages/`
  (the old product-group folders are gone). Folders are for people only: Drishti reads `sutras/` recursively and infers nothing from a
  folder name. New in Rachana: **shared panel blocks** (`<name>.v<N>.block.yaml` with `params:`, used as `{ use: legs@1, with: {...} }`,
  `DRS-2048` for a block that cannot be used) and a panel's **`when:`** (shown only where it holds), which gives a product's sub types
  their own panels inside its one Sutra (Endur `ENGY-SWAP` and `ENGY-B-SWAP`, `NGX-COMM-PHYS` and `COMM-WARRANTS-INV` …). Admin → Packs →
  a pack → **Sutra coverage** (`GET /api/v1/packs/{name}/coverage`) lists every combination of the matched fields in the data with the
  Sutra that lays it out, flagging *none* and *several*. [RACHANA_REFERENCE.md](docs/guides/RACHANA_REFERENCE.md#shared-panel-blocks-use).
- **Compare on deep documents.** Arrays are matched by their identifier in any case and convention (`LEGS` by `LEG_ID`, `CASH_FLOWS` by
  `FLOW_ID`, `RESETS` by `RESET_ID`, `EVENTS` by `EVENT_ID`), and a side-by-side panel shows the ticket and each leg's terms on the two
  dates with the fields that differ marked.
- **Search form for trades** on the new fields: source system, product type and a product sub type that follows it, status, side,
  currency, legal entity, portfolio, counterparty (type to find), trader, trade and maturity dates; drop-downs list the values of every
  system. A form field may `depend` on another enum. Upper-snake names read as words in inferred labels (`MATURITY_DATE`: *Maturity date*).
- **BI samples** follow: `trades-basic`, `trading-risk` and `live-trading` read the trade valuations (columns `DESK`, `PORTFOLIO`,
  `PRODUCT_TYPE`, `MTM`, `PNL_1D` …), `trading-cashflows` reads the bookings of the three systems (`LEGS`, `LEGS[].CASH_FLOWS`). The
  default field masks add `TRADER_ID`, `TRADER_FIRST_NAME` and `TRADER_LAST_NAME`.

- **Seven packs.** `config/packs` now holds exactly seven packs, all on by default (`drishti.packs.enabled` /
  `DRISHTI_PACKS` defaults to `core,market-data,trading,counterparty-risk,market-risk,world-economy,news-and-markets`; it was `finance`):
  **core** (`CORE`, from `banking-core`: counterparties, now with `COUNTRY_OF_RISK` and `UEN` and links to their economy and headlines,
  groups, issuers, CCPs, legal entities, books, desks, traders, calendars, clearing accounts); **market-data** (`MKT`); **trading** (`TRDS`,
  `TRD` trades, extends core and market-data); **counterparty-risk** (`CCR`, now also `AGR` master agreements and `CSA`s, whose Delta tables
  moved from the `reference` domain to a new `legal` domain: rebuild the lake or move `<lake>/reference/{agreement,csa}` to `<lake>/legal/`);
  **market-risk** (`MRSK`); **world-economy** (`WRLD`, `economics` and `politics-society` in one pack, one generator
  `tools/packgen/world/make.py`); **news-and-markets** (`NWMK`, `news-watch` and `market-watch` in one sample pack; topics and companies link
  to their economy). Kinds, mnemonics and ids are unchanged; the codes `BNK`, `ECO`, `POLS`, `NEWSW`, `MKTW` are gone.
  `finance`, `genomics`, `logistics`, `retail-banking`, `climate-risk`, `operational-risk` and `liquidity-risk` are **retired to
  `examples/packs/`**: not shipped in the images, not tested by default, still loadable with the new `drishti.packs.extra-dirs`
  (`DRISHTI_PACKS_EXTRA_DIRS=examples/packs`, server and console; a pack there may extend the shipped ones). Every example a shipped pack
  advertises opens a full view (`DomainPacksTest`), and every `config/bi` item reads only what the seven serve (`ShippedExamplesPacksTest`).
  [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#the-packs-that-ship), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md).
- **No hidden files or folders, and everything Drishti writes lives under the data folder.** Connector edit history and generated-file markers: `config/connectors/.history/` and `.generated/` are now `<data>/connectors/history/` and `generated/`; the pack baseline of `pack make` and `pack new`: `<pack>/generated/`; the lake builder state: `_drishti-lake/`; the installed-packs folders `previous-versions/` and `uploads-awaiting-deploy/`; `data/packs/settings/migrated/`; the temp-folder markers `drishti-temp-folder.txt` and `drishti-console-temp-folder.txt`; the Calc and DuckDB-Wasm stamps `drishti-pyodide.txt` and `drishti-duckdb-wasm.txt`; temp files lose their leading dot. Every old name is read once and renamed with a log line (nothing is lost). The IDE QA profile moves from `qa-data/` to `data/qa/`; `pack make` and the other tools write `build/` under the data folder; `tools/drill.sh` logs to `<data>/drill/`; pytest no longer creates `.pytest_cache`. Only names an external tool requires keep their dot (see the developer guide). A guard test (`tools/test_no_hidden_names.py`) fails on a new hidden name.
- **Run configurations for IntelliJ IDEA and PyCharm; QA with no terminal.** `.run/` now holds Drishti server (default, QA profile on ports 18580/17580 with its own git-ignored
  `data/qa/` folder, and a debug-friendly one), Drishti console (default and QA), Drishti build server jar (Maven), a compound Drishti QA (server + console) and Python
  configurations for QA pack make, QA lake build (big JSON to Delta, editable parameters), QA server smoke and QA doctor. `docs/guides/IDE_GUIDE.md` is rewritten around them,
  with the QA walk (build the jar, QA server, QA console, lake build, Build -> New pack, deploy, load data, open a deal) and more common problems. `deltalake` joins
  `drishti-console/requirements.txt` so the lake build runs from the console's `.venv`.
- **Console: reconnects to the server by itself.** Start order no longer matters and a restarted server is noticed. A background watch checks
  each server every `backend.check_seconds` (default 5, `DRISHTI_CONSOLE_BACKEND_CHECK_SECONDS`), logs each change once, shows it on Admin >
  Health and answers `GET /healthz/backend`. Every page carries `server-watch.js`: when a page was rendered while the server was unreachable, or a
  call fails with `DRS-5003`, a banner ("Server not reachable - retrying...", Retry now) polls the console with a 2 s to 30 s backoff and, when
  the server is back, says "Connected again" and reloads the page (never one with unsaved edits: it offers Reload). The sign-in page says so too.
- **One large file of deep trading documents, from Build -> New pack to a deployed pack with its data, all in the browser**
  ([Schema to pack, A deep trading JSON](docs/guides/SCHEMA_TO_PACK.md#appendix-a-deep-trading-json)). *Reading:* a JSON array of any size
  is streamed like JSON Lines (one document in memory at a time); **every row is counted** for the columns that could tell record types apart
  (rare types are found, with their true row count), up to `builder.pack_profile_rows_per_type` (10,000) rows of each type are **profiled**
  (how often each field has a value, digits written as text, every array's size at every depth) and `builder.pack_design_samples_per_type` (50)
  of each type are kept as **design samples**, chosen for variety; a 2 GB / 500,000-row file reads in about 20 s with memory flat (about
  110 MB in the page). *Planning:* ids of any case and type (`TRADE_ID`, an integer, preferred over `TRANSACTION_ID`; integer ids are labels,
  never amounts); **blank text is no value** (a field blank everywhere is hidden, never an enum of `""`); `UPPER_SNAKE` names read as words
  (*Trade ID*, *Buy sell code*, *Counterparty UEN*); a document with no business-date field is a **snapshot** stored under the day it is
  loaded (`ingest: {key, snapshot: true}`; choose a date field on the card instead); a type column (`PRODUCT_TYPE`) is found by itself and
  gives **exactly one Sutra per type**, named after it (`comswap`, `cash`), each designed from its own type's rows, with the catch-all only
  when some rows have no type (the card lists "5 types -> 5 Sutras" with each type's rows); **pages per value** (counterparty, portfolio,
  index) become derived kinds with links both ways; **Anonymise samples** (on by default for data files: names, ids and amounts replaced
  the same way everywhere, dates and short vocabulary kept) keeps every real value out of the pack's samples, tests and catalogue.
  *The deep design* (any depth, nothing about trades in it): a deal-ticket strip, the document's filled fields, an array of a few items
  with arrays of its own (legs) **side by side**, each item's schedule a row wide drilling into the levels below (cash flows -> resets),
  plain arrays (events) as tables that say *No events* when absent, a payment timeline across all items, the other documents of the same
  counterparty on the right; and a **BI model** with a dataset per level (deal, legs, cash flows, resets, events), each row carrying the keys
  above it. *Loading:* **Admin -> Packs -> a pack -> Load data** streams the whole file to the server (JSON array or JSON Lines, up to
  `drishti.loads.upload-max-mb`), which writes it into the pack's File connector by business date, checks every row against the pack's Sutras
  (types with no Sutra, fields no Sutra shows), and announces the load (refresh, verify; the pages per value are rebuilt).
- **Rachana: a table or ladder drills down level by level** (`levels:`, with `crumb`, `root`; `empty:` for the words of an empty table) to any
  depth: one level at a time with its own columns, count and total, a breadcrumb, the parent row as a ticket, ↑ ↓ Enter → to open, Esc,
  Backspace or ← to go up, and the open level in the address (`?dr.<panel>=1.6`) so it can be shared and Back goes up
  ([levels](docs/guides/RACHANA_REFERENCE.md#levels-drill-down-level-by-level)). New functions `flatten(list)` and `nonblank(a, b, …)`. A panel
  whose `source` names an entity no source holds says it has no data rather than failing.
- **Command line:** `pack make FILE.json --name N` reads a JSON array (or JSON Lines) with no schema through the console's engine (the same
  plan as the page), with `--anonymise/--no-anonymise`, `--related none|PATH,...` and `--as-of DATE` for a snapshot; `data ingest --as-of`.
- **Server:** `GET|POST /api/v1/admin/packs/{pack}/data` (Load data; token scope `packs:admin`); the File connector's
  `layout.<kind>.title` (promoted columns after the id in the search title, so type-ahead finds a deal by its counterparty); a BI table may be
  over an array at any depth (`each: LEGS[].CASH_FLOWS[].RESETS`, columns `$.TRADE_ID`, `^.FLOW_ID`, `^^.LEG_ID`); packs may carry
  `config/bi/models/`; derived pages and BI tables read documents 500 at a time (5,000 deals no longer exhaust the heap);
  `SourcePlugin.dependsOn(kind)` lets a load refresh what is built from the loaded kind.
- **Synthetic data:** `tools/packgen/comswap_synth.py` (a commodity book: ComSwap, ComFut, Cash, Commodity and a rare CallNot, the shape of a
  trading-system export, every value invented; samples in `docs/guides/examples/comswap/`) and `tools/packgen/note_synth.py` (a structured
  note, four levels deep, another shape).

### 2.3.0 (in progress) — Calculation, live and scale (wave B, server lane)

- **Server: the filter context (wave B task 1).** DEL's `calc(agg, where=, ignore=[…] | ALL, keep=[…], via='r_5')`, `share_of(agg, over=[…] | ALL)`,
  `parent_of(agg, hierarchy=)` and `avg_by(agg, by=[…])` are built (catalogue `status: built`, EXPRESSIONS.md regenerated): `where=` alone restricts
  the aggregates; the others are **context sub-queries** (the same compiler, as the same reader, at the coarser grain, left-joined on the fields both
  keep), so security is never in scope and `ignore=ALL` is "everything you may see"; nesting at most three deep (`DRS-9127`, `DRS-9166`); a measure's
  `via:` key is `calc(…, via=)`. The plan has `Context` nodes (hash, explain); no rollup answers a context. **Model `calc-groups`** (items over
  `SELECTED`, precedence) and **`parameters`** (what-if `number`/`integer`/`text`/`date`/`boolean` as `param.x`, and `field` parameters) are read,
  checked and described (`describe`: `calcGroups`, `parameters`, `calendar`, `fx`). **MQ v2** (`mq: 2`): `params`, `calc: {Group: Item}`,
  `perspective`, `options.budget`; the values, items and local measures are part of the plan hash (a local measure no longer shares a cache entry
  with another of the same name). New codes `DRS-9164` (parameter), `DRS-9165` (calculation group), `DRS-9166` (context), and reserved for the scale
  task `DRS-9028`, `DRS-9029`, `DRS-9184`. Property test: % of total, ignore-a-slicer, keep, where and nested contexts equal an independent
  computation for random fields, filters and readers; a YTD calculation group over ten measures and a what-if shock equal theirs.
- **Server: time and balances (wave B task 2).** `last`, `first`, `opening`, `closing`, `avg_over`, `as_of`, `shift_date`, `asof()`,
  `fiscal_ytd`, `fx` and `convert(…, at=)` are built. `closing` takes each period's last business day on the model's calendar up to the as-of date,
  per group (May 2027 closes on Friday the 28th, Memorial Day being the 31st); `opening` is the close of the row before along the answer's date field,
  else of the business day before the as-of period (read at that date for a snapshot's `businessDate`); `last`/`first` are per group (a context
  sub-query finds the group's date); `as_of` binds every scan of its sub-query to another business date. A model's **`calendars: { holidays,
  fiscal-year-start }`** drives the business days (also of `business_days_between`, which now subtracts holidays, and of relative windows) and
  `fiscal_ytd`; its **`fx: { rates, from, to, rate, date, base }`** feeds `fx` (latest rate on or before the date, stored pair, inverse or through the
  base) and `convert` (each amount by its own column's currency, at the as-of date, the trade date or another date column), which lifts the currency
  guard. A measure written with a balance function is not semi-additive any more; one that is, added across dates with other measures, is refused
  (`DRS-9159`) with the balance functions as the fix. Calculation groups no longer hide a measure's currency from the guard.
- **Server: time modes and standing queries (wave B task 3).** A **generation bus** (`engine/GenerationBus`) publishes every change of a model's data
  once, in order: a live batch with its rows (written, before, removed), a data load or refresh with its business date (`backdated` before the current
  one), a history sample; standing queries are **pushed** (the stream waits on its groups, no polling of generations). Subscriptions are grouped by
  **(plan hash, security signature)** (`StandingQueryRegistry`): one evaluation per group, a second subscriber gets the group's answer without one
  (`group`, `mode`, `subscribers` in the subscribe answer). A group's **`Maintainer`** keeps it current by the rules of BI_DESIGN §4.6: `sum`, `count`,
  `avg` (and arithmetic of them) of the live table's own columns **by difference** (the reader's row rules and the query's filters worked out per
  changed row), `min`/`max`/`distinct`/`percentile` by asking the **touched groups** again, contexts and fields of other tables by asking again, windows,
  time intelligence and Python **on the tick**. A per-model **maintenance budget** (`drishti.bi.mq.maintenance-budget`, `tick`) degrades a busy model's
  groups to tick mode (`state: tick`); an overrun doubles the throttle; a security change re-plans the group. Each plan scan is bound to its own time
  (**mixed live/snapshot**: `live` for a live table, `current` for a snapshot one asked live). **Back-dated refresh:** a load for an earlier date
  moves only that date's generation (`Layers.generation(key, date)`): an as-of answer of another date keeps its L1 entry, a standing query that reads
  later dates is not evaluated again. Property test: a board (rows), a report (diff) and an alert (value) for three readers over one live model equal
  the same query asked now after random update streams.
- **Server: the tier planner and Python nodes (wave B task 4).** `plan/TierPlanner` places every node of the plan (stage 7): a direct table's scan and
  an aggregate wholly in one SQL store that aggregates at the **source**, Python nodes on the **console**, the rest on the **Java** tier; each scan has its
  layer (`columns`, `live`, `history`, `direct`, `rollup <name>`). **Explain** (`options.explain`) answers the tree with tiers and layers, `tiers`,
  `timings` (`engineMillis`, `pythonMillis`, `layer`) and `explainText`. Each connector has a **capability descriptor** (`ConnectorCapabilities`: SQL
  dialect, aggregates, filter types, streams, dated, max fragments) that replaces the default-dialect fallback of direct queries, and a **fragment pool
  and circuit breaker** (`FragmentPools`, `drishti.bi.exec.max-fragments`, `breaker-failures`, `breaker-open`): layer builds and direct queries run in
  the pool; a failing connector is refused at once (`DRS-9026`, named) until one fragment succeeds again; `GET /bi/engine` lists them. **Python
  measures in any grouped query** (R8, plan node `PyAggregate`): the SQL measures run first, the inputs' rows per group are read as the reader, and the
  Python runner gives one value per group (a masked input leaves the column `None`; sorting or filtering on it is `DRS-9162`). The runner is the
  console's **Py-node endpoint** (`ConsolePythonRunner`: `POST {drishti.bi.transform.console-url}/internal/py/transform` and `/measure`, token
  `console-token`) when set, else the process runner as before.
- **Server: scale (wave B task 5).** `engine/Admission` serves waiting queries by **work class** (interactive before standing-query maintenance before
  background), within a person's share (`drishti.bi.query.max-concurrent-per-user`: standing work does not count against it) and a **model's share**
  (`drishti.bi.exec.per-model-share`); a query waits `admission`, then `DRS-9025`. **Cost estimates and budgets:** before a query runs the engine adds
  up the rows its layers and live tables hold for the dates it reads and refuses it over `options.budget.rows` or `drishti.bi.exec.max-estimated-rows`
  (`DRS-9029`, with the estimate and the fix); `options.budget.millis` and the class's `interactive`/`background` limit cut its time. A query that runs
  the engine out of memory, even spilling, is `DRS-9028`. **Micrometer metrics** of BI_DESIGN §6.8 (`drishti_bi_query_seconds{model,layer}`,
  `_query_rows`, `_cache_requests_total{result}`, `_admission_wait_seconds`, `_refusals_total{code}`, `_fragment_seconds{tier,connector}`,
  `_sq_active`, `_sq_lag_seconds`, `_sq_mode{mode}`, `_python_seconds{item}`, `_layer_rebuild_seconds`). **Several Java nodes:** a
  `ShardDirectory` (rendezvous hashing of a model over the nodes that are up) names each model's shard owner; a subscription asked of another node is
  held on the owner under the same id and held again where the shard moved when the owner is lost (`DRS-9184` while it cannot be reached); bridged
  generation buses carry loads and refreshes between nodes. Proven in process with two nodes (`TwoNodesTest`); the HTTP transport between Java nodes
  is not built yet (run one Java node). No static state holds generations or subscriptions. **The 500-viewer load test** (`ViewersLoadTest`) is
  written for a real server (`-Ddrishti.load.url=…`) and skipped otherwise. OPERATIONS.md: BI metrics and BI scale.
- **Console: no browser compute (wave B, console task 1).** DuckDB-Wasm, Perspective (with its Arrow module) and BI's Pyodide driver are gone from every
  BI page, and no BI page's policy has `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (a test fails if one loads a compute engine). Analyse's Slice is worked out by the console
  (`POST /bi/explore/pivot`), SQL cells are Drishti SQL on the server (a cell over `extract` on the console), Python cells and visuals run on the console
  only (a manifest's `target: browser` or `both` runs there; the samples say `server`); a 2.1 Perspective visual is Drishti's grid of the server's rows,
