Parser and schema (`DRS-2029`, `DRS-2101`), `MapSupport` reads the kind once and applies the caller's masks and rights, console macro, CSV, workbench palette and drop roles, `drishti.panels.map-*` settings; the jurisdiction (politics-society) and economy (economics) Sutras carry one, through the pack generators. Shapes are vendored Natural Earth: 1:110m countries, plus **admin-1 regions, one simplified file per country** (`static/vendor/echarts/maps/regions/<ISO3>.json`, 251 files, about 7 MB, ISO 3166-2 codes, `tools/maps/build_regions.py`) and `world-points.json` for countries too small for the world shapes; no tiles, no map service, nothing leaves the network. The BI `map` visual **drills world, country, region** on a click (the cross-filter at each level, a breadcrumb World › Australia, `location` accepting ISO 3166-2 codes; Format: `level` and `drill`), and the new sample report **World economics** (dataset `world-economics`, real World Bank data, no IMF) has a drillable map, cards and a table. `<drishti-report>` and `<drishti-panel>` serve the same shapes (Elements library 1.2.0). Tests: `MapPanelTest`, `test_maps.py`, `test_maps_browser.py`. Docs: RACHANA_REFERENCE, PANEL_KINDS, REPORTS, BI_GUIDE, VENDOR.md, CONFIGURATION, the politics-society guide.
- **Changed:** **Forms** in the top bar opens the forms home (`/forms`) instead of a menu listing every pack (it ran off the screen); it is lit on any form page. A test checks that a top-bar menu stays open until the person closes it.
- **Added: hands-on tutorials 1 to 9 in `docs/tutorials/`** (index of all 17, the rest marked coming): your first 15 minutes; look up anything; query by form; connect your data (a file connector, a PostgreSQL one with TLS and a pool, test, retry, the detail page); build a pack from your data (Build, New pack, deploy, roll back, `pack make`); design a screen (tabs holding panels, a slider, column filters, About this page); load data daily (the data-load signal, expectations, late data, a measure alert on a missing load); share and collaborate (a filtered view, a discussion, mentions, the inbox); reports (a saved search on a schedule, a subscription for a colleague, how both sit under the Reports menu). Each step has a worked example, the real command output where a command exists, and a real 1280 px screenshot (phone shots where it matters) made by `tools/docs/screenshots.py --guide t01` to `t09` from a scratch stack; the sample data is in `docs/tutorials/data/`. `drishti-console/tests/test_tutorials.py` checks every image exists and every link and anchor resolves. **Fixed:** the workbench's YAML editor called `panels` and `body` on a `tabs` panel unknown options ("`panels` is not an option of tabs panels") although the grammar and the server accept them; the schema the editor reads now lists both (test in `NestedTabsTest`).
- **Added: sixteen more LLM provider types, the way sajhamcpserver has them, in Java and Python.** `groq`, `together`, `fireworks`, `deepseek`, `xai`, `openrouter`, `perplexity`, `huggingface`, `vllm`, `lmstudio`, `mistral` and `openai_compatible` (OpenAI Chat Completions with presets: a base URL, a key variable and a model catalogue), `azure_openai` (api key, bearer or Microsoft Entra ID by client secret or workload identity; managed identity is not built), `gemini` (AI Studio; Vertex is not built), `cohere`, `ollama` (local, no key) and `bedrock` (Converse, InvokeModel, ListFoundationModels; the AWS SDK v2 in Java, boto3 in Python, so the whole AWS credential chain works). On the existing `LLMProvider` / `LLMModel` abstractions: chat, tools, JSON mode, embeddings, model listing (the catalogue `llm_catalog.json`, mirrored from sajha's `catalog.py`, plus the live list), `health()` and `probe()`, `describe_config()` with every secret masked, keys only as `${ENV}` (a preset also reads the vendor's own variable), the shared proxy block, the AI policy, and **off by default** (`config/ai/<type>.yaml.example`, all `enabled: false`). Every provider and the console's MCP client take the connectors' shared `tls:` block (private CA, PKCS12/JKS stores, mutual TLS, hostname check, expiry shown in Admin → AI, through a CONNECT proxy; the console reads PEM and PKCS12, not JKS). Tests: a fake server per API in both languages (request, auth header, reply, 401/429/500, timeout, SigV4 header), the shared contract `llm_contract_http.json` against five providers, TLS with a test CA. Docs: [AI_PROVIDERS.md](docs/architecture/AI_PROVIDERS.md) section 11, CONFIGURATION, TLS.md matrix, TROUBLESHOOTING. `boto3` joins the console's `requirements.txt`; `drishti-ai` gains the AWS SDK modules `bedrockruntime` and `bedrock`.
- **Changed:** Admin pages link names to their detail pages: on Health every connector and pack name (tables, a pack's connector list, failed-to-start rows); on Data loads the pack (overview and each pack's page); in Packs' deploy history the pack.
- **Added: database connection pools, default 20, with no leaks and no long holds.** Every database connection now comes from a pool: the JDBC/PostgreSQL connector and its loader (`pool-size` default 4 becomes **20**; the loader borrows one connection per batch and one per published day and takes `--pool-size`), the identity database on PostgreSQL (Hikari, 20, leak detection on), MongoDB (`maxPoolSize`), Redis (up to `pool-size` multiplexed connections, opened as the open ones get busy) and Aerospike (`maxConnsPerNode`). One set of connector settings, `pool-size`, `pool-min-idle`, `pool-idle-timeout`, `pool-max-lifetime`, `pool-connection-timeout`, `pool-leak-threshold` and `statement-timeout`, and a site default `drishti.db.pool.*` that connectors inherit; the shipped example connector files and the `postgres` profile set none. A connection is borrowed per unit of work in a try-with-resources and returned at once, a returned connection with an open transaction is rolled back, PostgreSQL gets `statement_timeout`, a borrow held past the leak threshold is logged once with the borrower's stack. **SQLite and DuckDB are exempt** (embedded files; documented why) and still close what they open. New `ConnectionDisciplineTest` fails the build on a `getConnection`, `borrow`, `createStatement` or `prepareStatement` outside a try-with-resources and on any field holding a `Connection`, `Statement` or `ResultSet`. **Visible:** the connector's page and Admin → Health show size, in use, idle, waiting and the longest current borrow; `GET /api/v1/admin/health` has `sources[].pool`, `identityPool` and `poolsAttention`; Prometheus has `drishti_db_pool_max`, `_open`, `_in_use`, `_idle`, `_waiting`, `_longest_borrow_seconds`, `_leaks`, `_timeouts`. Plugin SPI: `SourcePlugin.poolStats()` and the `com.ash.drishti.api.pool` package (`ResourcePool`, `PoolSettings`, `SitePool`, `PoolStats`). Docs: CONNECTOR_FILES section 14, POSTGRES/MONGODB/REDIS/AEROSPIKE/DUCKDB_CONNECTOR, CONFIGURATION, OPERATIONS (sizing against `max_connections`), TROUBLESHOOTING. Tests: pool size default and override, the site default, a stress run with injected errors ending with nothing in use, leak detection on a deliberately held connection, the connection timeout, Redis pool growth and shrink, Mongo pool settings, the identity pool, Health and the connector page.
- **Changed: the About page (`/about`) is a product page.** Hero with the mark, tagline, version and *Sign in* / *Open the terminal* and *Documentation*; what Drishti is and the problem it solves; key features as cards; measured performance with the source document named; capabilities by area; competitive advantage with a prominent link to the competitive analysis; technology, use cases, author and licence. The Sutra, source and pack listings are gone from it (they live in *Admin → Health* and *Admin → Packs*, linked as *System details for admins*). Tests: route test and a phone-width check.
- **Added: AI-driven BI, stage 2 (Rūpaka 20.1): report from words, ask the data, explain this number** ([BI_AI.md](docs/guides/BI_AI.md)). Off by default (`drishti.ai.enabled`), on the AI layer, under the features `bi-draft`, `bi-ask`, `bi-explain` of its policy (masks, roles, limits, audit; the BI power each hook needs still applies). **Describe a report** in the designer: the model drafts report YAML from the dataset's model (names, types, descriptions; never a row), the report checker passes it before it is shown (one repair round, else `DRS-4020`), *Use this draft* puts it in the YAML tab, nothing is saved. **Ask the data** on a report page: the model maps the question to a dataset query, the **engine runs it as the reader** (row rules, masks), shown as a table, the visual that suits it and the query used. **Explain** on a visual: lineage (dataset, measure definition, columns, pack kinds and connectors) and the engine's breakdown by the main dimensions under the page's state; with `drishti.bi.ai.values: aggregates` a short wording of it. New: `/api/v1/bi/ai/{status,draft,ask,explain}` (token scope `ai:use`), `drishti.bi.ai.*`, **`DRS-4020`**, the console script `bi-ai.js`; tests `BiAiTest`, `test_bi_ai.py`, `test_bi_ai_browser.py` (mock provider only); docs BI_AI.md, BI_GUIDE 22, AI_PROVIDERS (BI uses), USER_GUIDE, API_GUIDE, CONFIGURATION, TROUBLESHOOTING, RUPAKA 20.1.
- **Changed: the AI layer follows the abstractions of sajhamcpserver, in Python and Java.** `LLMProvider` and `LLMModel` (`from_settings`, `models`, `model`, `chat_model`, `embedding_model`, `health`, `describe_config`, `close`; `chat_completions_create` / `chatCompletionsCreate`, the stream and async twins, `embeddings_create`), the canonical OpenAI-shaped request and response types (extension field `drishti`), `ProviderBase`, the provider registry, `LLMFactory` and `GovernedModel` (the AI policy applied to every call), the error classes with kinds shared with Java, and the tool base `BaseMCPTool` / `BaseMcpTool`. The wire providers (OpenAI through Spring AI, Anthropic, `server`, mock) and `config/ai/*.yaml` are unchanged; streaming is emulated from one completion. One shared contract file runs against the mock in Java (`LLMContractTest`) and Python (`test_llm_contract.py`). [AI_PROVIDERS.md](docs/architecture/AI_PROVIDERS.md) section 10 maps each name.
- **Added: Analyse (Rūpaka phase 9, lean).** The rail's *Explore* placeholder is now **Analyse** at `/bi/explore`: open a dataset as a **masked extract** (the server's dataset query as the reader, cut to `bi.explore.max_rows`, rebuilt on every call), then **Slice** (a drag-and-drop pivot in the page), **SQL** cells (one read-only `SELECT` over the table `extract` on the console, with limits on text, time and rows) and **Python** cells (pandas over `df`, charts in `result`, `quant.price(...)`; the phase 6 sandbox on the server, or Pyodide in the browser; needs `bi-author`). **Notebooks** are BI items of kind `notebook` (versions and review like any item; *Open in Analyse* on the item page); **Promote** turns a Python cell into a Python Measure or Visual draft and a slice into a report draft. **`QuantEngine`** (`drishti-console/core/quant/`) with a stand-in (Black-Scholes, bond, NPV) and a documented slot for QLRisk (not imported). New console routes `/bi/explore/*` (extract, sql, python, notebook/check, promote, status), settings `bi.explore.*`, the Python items' codes `DRS-9070` to `9074`. Decision: DuckDB-Wasm is not vendored, so Slice is JavaScript and SQL runs on the console in SQLite (the page keeps the strict policy, no WebAssembly). Not built: DuckDB-Wasm in the page, million-row extracts, Data and Notes tabs, saved outputs, scheduled notebooks, a real quant engine. A test now fails when the server's `drishti.bi.report.python-packages` and the console's `bi.python.packages` differ. New: [ANALYSE.md](docs/guides/ANALYSE.md), QUANT_SERVICE.md section 16, BI_GUIDE 21, BI_TUTORIAL chapter 25, RUPAKA 20.9. Tests: console unit and route tests (two readers get different extracts, SQL limits and read-only, notebook check and promote, the quant stand-in, a Python cell) and one Chromium file (slice, SQL, Python, save, phone 390).
- **Added: Python in BI (Rūpaka phase 6).** **Python items:** a Measure (`compute(table, params)`, a number per group over a pandas table) or a Visual (`render(table, params)`, an ECharts option, SVG or PNG: data, never HTML) is a BI item of kind `python` (or a file in `config/bi/python/<id>.python.yaml`) with a manifest (`interface`, `inputs`, `packages`, `target: server | browser | both`, `timeout`, `memory`, `params`, `source`), versions, review and publishing like any item; publishing checks the manifest (`DRS-9070`), the item page's **Check the Python** also parses the source. **In reports:** a dataset measure `{ python, inputs, by }` (a query that names it is still `DRS-9013`) and a new `python` visual (designer group *Python*; `format: { python, params, run: auto | server | browser }`) that hands the rows the person may see, masked as the server masked them, to the item. **Server runner:** one short-lived worker process of the console per call (`python -I`, empty environment, a scratch folder, CPU, memory, file and wall-clock limits, an audit hook against sockets, processes and files outside the scratch folder, an import guard and package allow-list, a network namespace with no interfaces where the machine allows it, a pool of `bi.python.workers`), failures as `DRS-9071` to `9074` with the traceback for authors only. **Browser target:** the same item in Pyodide in Calc's worker, loaded on first use, on the rows the page already has; the report page keeps the strict content-security policy. Samples: *Desk P&L volatility* (measure), *P&L histogram* (visual) and the report *Desk P&L risk (Python)*. Server calls take about 220 to 320 ms (a worker is started per call; the 50 ms design target is not met). Not built: Transform and Source, Python inside a SQL query, tests shipped with an item, Vega-Lite, a warm pool. New: [PYTHON_BI.md](docs/guides/PYTHON_BI.md), BI_TUTORIAL chapter 24, DATASETS 4.2, REPORTS 3.3, RUPAKA 20.8; settings `drishti.bi.report.python-*` and the console's `bi.python.*`; `pandas`, `numpy` and `pyarrow` join the console's `requirements.txt`.
- **Added: BI essentials (wave 3).** **Scheduled refresh of datasets:** `refresh:` on a dataset (and `refresh.schedule` on a table) takes `daily 06:00`, `business-days 07:30` (the pack's holiday calendar), `weekly`, `monthly ... business`, `hourly`, a cron, or `after-load`; a scheduler rebuilds the columns and rollup layers, a failure is retried with the shared policy (`drishti.retry.bi.*`, then `max-attempts`), and the dataset page's new **Refresh** section and `GET /api/v1/bi/datasets/{id}/refresh` show status, last and next run, rows, time and failures (`drishti.bi.refresh.*`). **Time intelligence:** `ytd`, `mtd`, `qtd`, `prior_period`, `same_period_last_year` (business-day aware, windows bound as parameters, as-of the end of the query's dates) and `running` in the measure language, with a badge in the designer's Fields pane; `lag` and `rank` stay `DRS-9013`. **Five visuals:** `map` (countries by ISO code, or points by latitude and longitude, from a vendored Natural Earth country file: no tiles, no CDN, public domain), `funnel`, `histogram`, `box` plot and `bubble`, in the catalogue, the designer, exports (PDF, PNG, Excel, CSV), the viewer and `<drishti-report>`; masks apply. **True totals:** a matrix with subtotals and a table with `totals: true` show the engine's totals for ratios, averages, distinct counts, percentiles, minimums and maximums instead of `–` (`totals` in the query answer; `drishti.bi.report.totals-max-rows`, `distribution-rows`). The sample *Counterparty risk* gains a map of the exposure by country, a `MTM ytd` card and a histogram. Docs: DATASETS 4.1 and 7.2, REPORTS 3.1 and 3.2, BI_GUIDE, BI_TUTORIAL chapter 23 (pictures), API_GUIDE, CONFIGURATION, TROUBLESHOOTING, RUPAKA 20.7 (the five items moved from not built to built). Tests: JUnit (schedule, retry and `after-load` with a test clock; time-intelligence SQL against a brute-force sum, with USNY holidays; totals and distributions against brute force; the API), console unit tests, one Chromium file.

## 1.22.0 — Distribution: export, subscriptions, alerts and embedded reports (2026-10-11)
- **Added: embedding and phone layouts (Rūpaka phase 7, part 3: phase 7 complete).** **`<drishti-report>`**, a Drishti Element beside `<drishti-view>`: a *published* BI report, one page or one visual of it, in another web application's page (Shadow DOM, no iframe, strict-CSP friendly, nothing from the server parsed as markup), signed in through the existing token exchange and **always masked**, with the viewer's slicers, page tabs, business date, bookmarks, filter or highlight, **cross-filtering** and "show as a table", **live redraws** over the page's one embed connection (`bilive:<report>` on the embed channel), attributes `report`, `page`, `visual`, `filters`, `chrome`, `theme`, `live`, a `filters` property, `selectPage()`, `clearFilters()`, and the events `drishti:loaded`, `filter-changed`, `page-changed`, `masked`, `tick`, `error`. **Admin → Embedding governs which hosts may embed which reports**: the application's `kinds` takes `report:<id>` and `report:*`, the new scope `embed:report` opens the three read paths a report needs (`GET /api/v1/bi/reports/*`, `POST .../query`, `GET .../live`) and never the authoring ones, an unpublished report is refused `DRS-8005`. The console's embed API: `GET /embed/v1/reports/{id}`, `POST .../query`, `GET .../catalogue`. **Per-report phone layouts**: `layouts: {phone: {<page>: {order, hide, span}}}` (640 px and narrower; hidden visuals are not queried; the viewer uses the screen's width, the element its own), parsed and checked by the server, and the designer's new **Phone tab** (drag to order, hide, height, a 390 px preview). The sample *Live trading desk* has one. Docs: DISTRIBUTION.md section 5, REPORTS.md section 14, BI_GUIDE, BI_TUTORIAL chapter 22, API_GUIDE, ELEMENTS.md section 18, RUPAKA 20.6 part 3.
- **Added: Drishti Elements steps 11 to 15.** The library is **versioned** (`1.1.0`): `/embed/v1/elements/<version>/drishti-elements.js` and `.../m/<name>.js` immutable with `integrity.json` (SHA-384), `1` and `1.1` answer `302`, another version `410 DRS-8006`, a test fails when a module changed under the same version; **`embed.lifecycle`** announces an embed API major with `Deprecation` and `Sunset` headers and then `410`. The npm package folder **`@drishti/elements`** (`drishti-console/web/elements/package/`, `tools/elements_package.py`: the modules, TypeScript declarations and React JSX types generated from one source with the reference tables). **`<drishti-panel>`** (one panel of a view). The developer guide `docs/guides/ELEMENTS.md` (five-minute start, token exchange in Node, Java and Python, SRI and npm, CSP, theming, a cookbook, troubleshooting by code), ADR-021, a help-centre entry, and a small load test (20 elements, 10 entities, one connection). Not built: `<drishti-about>`, `<drishti-search>` (needs a search scope), the React/Angular/Vue smoke pages, workspaces on `<drishti-view>`, the SharedWorker.
- **Fixed: drills failed at random on "Too many open files" / inotify.** Every server context opened six operating-system watch services (inotify instances, limit often 128). All file watchers (Sutras, packs, connectors, BI, AI, MCP) now share one per JVM through `SharedWatchService` (drishti-common); behaviour is unchanged. The server tests also cap the Spring context cache at 8. OPERATIONS.md 16, ARCHITECTURE.md 10.
- **Added: alerts on numbers and board notifications (Rūpaka phase 7, part 2).** *Alert me* on a visual of a BI report, on a dataset measure or on a field of a Sutra view (click a number): an alert fires when the number crosses a limit (`above`, `below`, `between`, `outside`), moves too far (a percentage or an amount, against the previous look or the previous business day) or does not arrive by a time. It looks on a schedule, after a data load (the load signal) or, for a live table, on change after a quiet time (debounce). **Each person is judged on their own number**: the engine applies their row rules and masks, and a number masked for them is never judged, said or sent. A small state machine (armed, active, hysteresis, cooldown) keeps it from flapping. It tells you in the inbox (a new *Alerts* tab, the bell and a toast), by email to your own address, and in the chat bridges (a new route event `alert`; a post with no figure); with a history, an audit trail, pause and resume, *Look now* and a preview, and an administrators' overview. A board tile whose colour turns red can tell everyone who reads the board (`notify:` in a tile's `alert`), each on their own number; `DRS-9053` now refuses only `notifications` and `subscriptions`. The existing `/alerts` page is extended, not duplicated (the older per-entity rules are unchanged); linked from **Reports** and the inbox. New: `/api/v1/me/measure-alerts/**`, `/api/v1/admin/measure-alerts/**`, `DRS-9062`, `drishti.alerts.measure.*` (CONFIGURATION.md). Docs: DISTRIBUTION.md section 4, BOARDS.md, BI_GUIDE.md, USER_GUIDE.md, API_GUIDE.md, TROUBLESHOOTING.md, RUPAKA.md 20.6, BI_TUTORIAL.md chapter 21. Tests: JUnit (decision logic, per-person masking, triggers, debounce, boards), console routes and one Chromium test (create, fire by a load, inbox, masked, phone).
- **Changed:** subscriptions refuse **outside email addresses** altogether (`DRS-9061`): a person who is not a user has no rights to render with. The administrator setting `drishti.distribution.subscriptions.external-recipients` and `external-domains` are removed; a subscription saved with an address keeps it, and the address is skipped at every send with the reason in the history.
- **Fixed:** API_GUIDE showed the literal text of a template in the captured export output; it now shows the real output.
- **Added: distribution, part 1 (Rūpaka phase 7).** Export a BI report, page or visual, or a Sutra view, as PDF (paged, with a running header), PNG, Excel (a typed sheet per visual) or CSV, made for the requester with their masks and row rules; and **subscriptions**: a report, page, visual or view by email on a schedule (`business-days`, `weekly`, `monthly`, business-day aware with the pack calendar) or after a data load, as the email itself, a PDF or an Excel file, to users and roles, each recipient getting their own render. Pause, resume, run now, a history, retries with the shared retry policy, audit. *Export* and *Subscribe* on the report bar and the view header; subscriptions in one list with the scheduled reports under **Reports**; settings `drishti.distribution.*`; `DRS-9060` and `DRS-9061`; [DISTRIBUTION.md](docs/guides/DISTRIBUTION.md), BI_TUTORIAL chapter 20.
- **Fixed:** email and bridge posts name a panel by the title the sender's view showed (the filtered title), not the unfiltered one.


## 1.21.0 — BI governance and streaming, connectors behind a proxy, Forms and Reports in the top bar, docs checked against the code (2026-10-11)
- **Fixed:** the top bar no longer overlaps at 1280 to 1500 px (the Views menu sat over the business-date box once there were five menus): menu labels collapse to icons below 1500 px and the command line may shrink to 160 px; `test_topbar_widths_browser.py` checks 1024 to 1920 px on `/t`, `/bi` and a view for overlapping controls and sideways scroll. BI copy no longer says boards are "not run yet" or "phase 1"; a **board in a BI bundle** now brings its reports (and their datasets) on export and has its body validated (DRS-905x) in the import dry run, with a warning for a tile report that is neither in the bundle nor here; the help centre summary of the BI guide covers phases 1 to 5.
- **Fixed:** **the connector settings catalogue now matches the plugin code, and four docs/tooling gaps from the docs review.** `plugin-settings.yaml` no longer marks as `required` what the plugin defaults (aerospike `hosts` `localhost:3000` and `namespace` `test`, delta `root`, file `root`, kafka `bootstrap-servers` `localhost:9092`, rabbitmq `uri`, activemq `broker-url`; the code's default is shown instead), and declares the keys the plugins read that drew an "unknown setting" warning: `layout.*` (aerospike, duckdb, file, jdbc, mongodb, redis), jdbc `ids.*`, `columns.*`, `reverse.*`, `part-shape.*`, delta and iceberg `hadoop.*`, iceberg `catalog.*`, kafka `kind.*` and `id-field.*`, and for rabbitmq and activemq `kind`, `id-field`, `kind.*`, `id-field.*`, `cache-mb` and `state.*`. Test: `PluginSettingsCatalogueSyncTest` scans the plugin sources and fails when a plugin reads a key the catalogue lacks or a `required` setting has a default in the code. `POST /builder/shape` is open to every signed-in user like the other Build workbench steps (design, suggest, edit, check; saving and shipping need the author right); its Javadoc said author-only. `tools/drill.sh` now runs `test_cli_bi.py`, `test_cli_connectors.py`, `test_cli_loads.py`, `test_pack_form.py`, `test_schemapack.py` and the pytest tests of `tools/realdata`. [RACHANA_REFERENCE.md](docs/guides/RACHANA_REFERENCE.md) gains the pack `form:` section; [OPERATIONALISING.md](docs/guides/OPERATIONALISING.md) had two sections numbered 17.
- **Changed:** query by form: a field with more than three values is a checkbox dropdown with a filter box (was more than six); three or fewer stay a row of checkboxes.
- **Changed:** the top bar's **Live** is one round toggle on every screen size: green with a filled dot while live (a click pauses on a static snapshot of the current business date), red with two pause bars while paused or on a picked date (a click resumes live). A real button with `aria-pressed`, Enter and Space, a tooltip, kept across pages. USER_GUIDE "Business dates". `test_live_toggle_browser.py` (1280 and 390 px, light and dark).
- **Added:** **Forms and Reports in the top bar, and a forms home.** Two new top-bar entries beside BI, built the same way (icon, tooltip, active state, mega menu, keyboard, finger-sized rows on a phone): **Forms** (*Forms — query by form*; *All forms* and one entry per pack with a `form:` section) and **Reports** (*Reports — saved and scheduled reports, and BI reports*; moved out of Views; with the `bi` power also the BI catalogue filtered to reports and the BI reports you opened last). New page `/forms`: the packs you may use that declare a form, their kinds as links with field counts, a filter box, your recent forms (browser storage) and your saved searches; `FORM <GO>` alone and the old `/form` open it. Roles and the sample-content policy are the server's, as for the forms themselves. [USER_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md) (top bar, the forms home). Tests: `test_form_routes.py`, `test_form_browser.py`, the phone menu and page-width sweeps.
- **Added:** **Rūpaka phase 5: streaming BI.** A dataset table with `mode: live` is now **kept in memory by the engine and updated by key** as the source pushes: the engine reads the kind once, subscribes to each entity through the same TopicHub the terminal's live views use (or the connector the table names; the demo source, Kafka and Redis push), applies batches of changes in one transaction with the retention (`live: { rows, age }`, `drishti.bi.live.max-rows`), skips rows that did not change, resyncs every 30 s (new entities are followed; a connector that cannot push is read again), reports `live`, `stale`, `failed`, and closes a table idle for ten minutes. Every live table has the columns `updateSeq` and `updatedAt`. **Row rules and masks are applied by the query compiler on every query, as the reader**; the table knows no reader. **Live reports:** a report over such a dataset has a stream (`GET /api/v1/bi/reports/{id}/live`: `hello`, `tick`, `status`; no data in it; the latest state, never a queue; `DRS-9051` past `max-streams` and `max-streams-per-user`) that reaches the browser over the console's one channel (`bilive:<report id>`); the viewer redraws its visuals in place (a chart keeps its instance, a table its scroll, a card flashes), coalesced (one round at a time, at most every 250 ms, three queries at a time), with a live indicator (last update, **Pause** and **Resume**, **Stale**, **Feed failing**, **Disconnected**, **Paused (tab in background)**) and the old picture kept and marked when an update fails; a **rolling chart** (`live-chart`). **Perspective:** `perspective-grid` and `perspective-pivot` over the rows of one table: the first read as **masked Arrow** (`POST /bi/reports/{id}/query?format=arrow`), then only the rows whose `updateSeq` moved, by key; a plain-table fallback when WebAssembly cannot start; the content security policy resolved properly: a page that holds a Perspective visual gets, in its own response only, `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` and the SHA-256 of the four styles Perspective 3.8.0 writes (never `unsafe-eval`, `unsafe-inline` or a blob worker), every other BI page stays strict; `tools/perspective-style-hashes.py` measures the list on the real pages and a Chromium test fails naming a missing hash after a library update. **Boards:** `config/bi/boards/<id>.board.yaml` and `board` items: pages of tiles (each a visual of a report, on the report grid), per-tile **alert colouring** by threshold (in words as well as colour), **kiosk** mode with **auto-rotating pages**, Perspective and rolling-chart tiles; `GET /api/v1/bi/boards/{id}`, `POST /bi/boards/validate`; the viewer at `/bi/b/<id>`. `DRS-9050` (a live table cannot be kept), `9051` (too many streams), `9052` (a board is not valid), `9053` (reserved: notifications). Settings: server `drishti.bi.live.*` and `drishti.bi.report.perspective-rows`, console `bi.live.min_interval_ms`, `bi.live.stale_seconds`, `bi.board.rotate_seconds`. Samples: the live datasets `live-trading` and `live-exposure`, the reports **Live trading desk** and **Live exposure**, the board **Desk wall**; BI_TUTORIAL chapters 18 and 19 with real screenshots. The Perspective retest (RUPAKA_POC.md section 12): the second-tab trap of phase 0 does not reproduce on the report page in Chromium 153, Chrome 155, Firefox 155 and WebKit 26.6; a 10,000-row grid takes 1,000 updates a second at 60 frames a second and 21 MB of JS heap. **Not built** (marked everywhere): incrementally maintained aggregates by group (the table is incremental, measures are recomputed over it), a history of a live table, notifications from boards (phase 7), a designer for boards, Perspective charts and settings panel, live tables over arrays or in `direct` queries, the LOAD_AND_MEMORY capacity run. [DATASETS.md](docs/guides/DATASETS.md#71-live-tables-phase-5), [REPORTS.md](docs/guides/REPORTS.md#13-live-visuals-and-perspective-phase-5), [BOARDS.md](docs/guides/BOARDS.md), [BI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md) section 18, [BI_TUTORIAL.md](docs/tutorials/10-first-bi-report.md) chapters 18 and 19, [LIVE.md](docs/architecture/LIVE.md), [API_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), [TROUBLESHOOTING.md](docs/guides/TROUBLESHOOTING.md), [RUPAKA.md](docs/architecture/RUPAKA.md) section 20.5. Tests: incremental table maintenance, retention by rows and by age, deletions, merged bursts, row rules and masks per reader after every update, polling sources, refusals (`LiveTablesTest`); the whole stack with a ticking demo source (`BiLiveApiTest`: stream, masks, Arrow and `since`, per-person limit, boards); report and board parsing; console routes and policy; and Chromium tests (live redraw in place, coalescing, pause and resume, a dropped console, a background tab, Perspective under the policy with streaming rows, masks, the plain fallback, two tabs, the style-hash check, the Desk wall with alerts, kiosk and rotation, the phone at 390 px).
- **Added:** **an outbound HTTP proxy for connectors, AI providers and MCP servers.** One shared mechanism (`com.ash.drishti.api.proxy`, next to the TLS module; the plugin SPI stays Spring-free): a `proxy:` block in a connector file's `settings:` (`url: http://host:port`, `username`, `password` as `${ENV}` only, `no-proxy` names, suffixes, addresses and CIDR blocks, `enabled`), or `proxy: none` to go direct; a **site default** `drishti.http.proxy.*` in `application.yaml` (`url`, `username`, `password`, `no-proxy`, `enabled`, `from-environment`; off by default, read at start) that every connector inherits unless it says otherwise; and, with `drishti.http.proxy.from-environment: true`, the standard `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY` and `NO_PROXY` variables. `http://` targets are forwarded, `https://` targets are tunnelled with `CONNECT` and keep their `tls.*` end to end. **Covered:** REST, feeds, news, yfinance, S3 (the AWS SDK's proxy configuration; the Apache client is used when a proxy applies), Delta and Iceberg on S3 (SDK proxy configuration, Hadoop `fs.s3a.proxy.*`, the Iceberg REST catalog's `rest.client.proxy.*`), the Kafka **schema registry** (the brokers are not HTTP), the AI providers (OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic) and the Streamable HTTP MCP client, in the server (JDK HTTP client) and in the console (`httpx`, same rules, the `DRISHTI_HTTP_PROXY_*` environment variables as site default). **Not covered:** a proxy reached over TLS (refused with a message), NTLM, Kerberos or SOCKS proxies, PAC files, and the server's own non-connector clients (pack registry, report e-mail, OIDC, the collaboration bridge). Basic credentials never leak: the password is `***` in Admin, health, the API and logs; a literal `proxy.password` or a password inside the URL is refused on save and in AI and MCP files (`drishti.http.proxy.password` literal in a configuration file stops the server at start). **Admin → Connectors:** the connector page has a **Proxy** row (effective proxy, user, `password ***`, no-proxy, where it came from: connector, site default, environment or disabled), the editor has a **Proxy** section with the same line, and **Test connection** goes through the proxy and says so (with plain-words hints for 407, CONNECT refused and an unreachable proxy). Health ends `via proxy host:port (source)`, or says the host is on the no-proxy list. **Changed:** the console's AI and MCP `httpx` clients no longer pick up `HTTP_PROXY` / `HTTPS_PROXY` by themselves (`trust_env` is off): set `DRISHTI_HTTP_PROXY_FROM_ENVIRONMENT=true` for that. The JDK refuses Basic credentials for a `CONNECT` tunnel by default; Drishti allows them (`jdk.http.auth.tunneling.disabledSchemes` unset) when a proxy with a login is configured. New settings: `drishti.http.proxy.*`. [PROXY.md](docs/connectors/TLS.md#outbound-http-proxy-for-connectors-ai-providers-and-mcp-servers), [CONNECTOR_GUIDE.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTORS.md), [CONNECTOR_FILES.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTOR_FILES.md), each connector's "Outbound proxy" section, [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), [AI_PROVIDERS.md](docs/architecture/AI_PROVIDERS.md), [MCP_CLIENT.md](docs/architecture/MCP_CLIENT.md), [TROUBLESHOOTING.md](docs/guides/TROUBLESHOOTING.md). Tests (no real network; `FakeProxy` in `drishti-testkit` is a small forward proxy with HTTP forwarding, CONNECT and Basic `Proxy-Authorization` checks): `ProxySettingsTest`, `RestProxyTest`, `FeedProxyTest`, `NewsProxyTest`, `YFinanceProxyTest`, `S3ProxyTest`, `S3StorageProxyTest`, `HadoopLakeProxyTest`, `IcebergProxyTest`, `SchemaRegistryProxyTest`, `AiProxyTest`, `ConnectorProxyTest`, `SiteProxyEnvironmentPostProcessorTest`, `tests/test_ai_proxy.py`, `test_admin_detail_pages.py` and `test_admin_connectors_proxy_browser.py` (Chromium).
- **Added:** **Rūpaka phase 4: publishing and governance.** Every save of a dataset or report is a *saved state* (author, time, message), with a history list, a **side-by-side YAML diff** of any two states or versions and *restore as a new draft*; the published version stays pinned. **Review** extends publish-with-review: the reviewers (any `bi-steward`, and workspace members with `approve`) are told in the inbox, the review page shows the diff, Check, the impact and a **data preview** answered with the reviewer's own rows and masks, approve and reject keep their comments in the item's review record, four-eyes holds. **Certification** only by a steward, badges in the catalogue and search, and it **falls automatically** (with a warning and an inbox notice) when a dataset is published with a removed or renamed field, measure or hierarchy. **Lineage** (dataset: tables, pack kinds, connectors, reports; report: dataset and the names it uses) and **impact analysis** ("what breaks if I remove X"), used on review, publish and import: publishing a dataset change that breaks published reports is refused (`DRS-9040`) unless a `bi-admin` forces it. **Environments:** BI bundles (versions, sample flags, owners, audience, checksums) exported and imported with a dry run, workspace and owner mapping (`DRS-9041`, `9042`), at `/bi/environments`. Everything is audited. **API:** `/api/v1/bi/items/{id}/history|diff|review|lineage|impact`, `POST /bi/items/{id}/history/{n}/restore`, `publish` and `approve` with `force`, `GET /bi/bundle`, `POST /bi/bundle/import`. **Settings:** `drishti.bi.governance.*`. **Not built** (marked everywhere): BI content in a pack archive and rollback of an import, rename detection, impact on a changed meaning or type, lineage past the connector, threaded diff comments. [BI_GOVERNANCE.md](docs/guides/BI_GOVERNANCE.md), [BI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md) section 17, [BI_TUTORIAL.md](docs/tutorials/10-first-bi-report.md) chapter 17, [API_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), [RUPAKA.md](docs/architecture/RUPAKA.md) section 20.4. Tests: `BiGovernanceApiTest` (versions, review four-eyes, certification revoke, impact, bundle round trip, audit), `LineDiffTest`, console route tests and `test_bi_gov_browser.py` (Chromium, ports 18992/17992, phone).
- **Added:** **Connectors that are code, not a store.** Two new source plugins whose documents are computed when asked for. `news` (`plugins/drishti-plugin-news`) takes a name from the id (`NEWS-Federal_Reserve`, `@de-DE` for another edition, `~all` for no relevance filter), asks Google News RSS (no key), parses it with XXE off and a size limit, keeps only headlines whose title names the entity or an alias, applies publisher allow and deny lists, sorts newest first, removes duplicates, caches per name for a TTL in a bounded cache, retries with the shared backoff, and reports the steps that chose the headlines (`pipeline`) and a count by publisher. `yfinance` (`plugins/drishti-plugin-yfinance`) returns a quote (price, change, change %, currency, market state, day and 52-week ranges, volume, market cap when given) and price history for a ticker from Yahoo Finance's public chart endpoint (`range` and `interval` settings), with a bounded cache and the same failure handling. **Both are off as shipped**: the names and tickers leave the network, and Yahoo's endpoint is unofficial and restricted to samples and internal demonstrations. Two **sample packs** use them: `news-watch` (fifteen topics, each with headlines, a count by source, the selection steps and the unfiltered list) and `market-watch` (twenty listed companies, each with a quote, a closing-price chart and headlines; extends `news-watch`); their connector templates write `config/connectors/news.yaml` and `yfinance.yaml` disabled at first start, enable with `DRISHTI_NEWS_ENABLED` and `DRISHTI_YFINANCE_ENABLED` or live in Admin, Connectors (a new plugin jar needs a restart). A panel cell whose text is a web address is now a link that opens in a new tab. Pages that read these through `source:` need `drishti.graph.link-budget` above 40 ms or show *pending* on the first view. [NEWS.md](docs/connectors/NEWS.md), [YFINANCE.md](docs/connectors/YFINANCE.md), [CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md#connectors-that-compute-data-on-demand), [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md). Tests never call Google or Yahoo: local fake servers serve canned RSS and JSON (an XXE attempt, bad JSON, oversized bodies, a slow response, 429 and 5xx then success, an unknown symbol); contract tests for both plugins; a Chromium test over the real server and console (ports 18993 and 17993) shows a company page and a topic page, and the same pages with the connectors off.

## 1.20.0 — BI you can use: datasets, reports and the designer; deep trades; real public data (2026-10-10)
- **Added:** **Rūpaka phase 3: reports, the designer and the Model view.** A *report* (YAML: pages on a 12-column grid, visuals bound to a dataset's fields and measures through wells, filters at report, page and visual level with basic, advanced and top-N, bookmarks,
  drill-through pages, a theme) is parsed, validated against its dataset (`DRS-9030` to `9035`) and written back the way a person writes it; publishing a report checks it. The viewer at `/bi/r/<id>[/<page>]` (`BI <report> <GO>` opens it) draws 21 visuals (bar, column, line, area,
  combo, scatter, pie, donut, waterfall, treemap, heatmap with ECharts; card, multi-row card, KPI, gauge; table, matrix with subtotals; list, dropdown and range slicers; text, image, button), each from one dataset query built on the server and run as the reader (row rules and masks),
  with cross-filter and highlight, drill-down along a hierarchy, drill-through to a page, show the rows, show as a table, open in the terminal, bookmarks, a business-date picker, the keyboard and a phone layout. The designer at `/bi/design/<draft>` has the Power BI panes (Fields with date
  parts and search, Visualizations with Build wells and Format, Filters, page tabs), a canvas to drag and resize on the grid and operate from the keyboard, a YAML tab that works both ways with suggestions, real data and the query time on every visual, Check (validation, accessibility,
  layout, performance), undo and redo; a field dropped on the canvas makes the best visual. The Model view at `/bi/m/<dataset>` draws tables as boxes and relationships as lines, relates by dragging a column onto another, accepts relationships proposed from the data, keeps positions under
  `layout:` and rewrites only the `relationships:` and `layout:` blocks. **API:** `/api/v1/bi/reports` (`GET /{id}`, `POST /{id}/query`, `/query`, `/validate`, `/format`, `GET /catalogue`, `/dataset`); `POST /datasets/validate` returns the parsed model. **Settings:** `drishti.bi.report.*`.
  Three sample reports in `config/bi/reports` (`trades-overview`, `counterparty-risk`, `cashflow-ladder`). **Not built** (marked everywhere): Perspective and live visuals, the map, funnel, histogram, box, bubble, Python and entity visuals (`DRS-9033`), the relative-date slicer, true totals
  for measures that do not add up, a report over several datasets, per-device layouts, a Check that runs expected values. [REPORTS.md](docs/guides/REPORTS.md), [BI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md) sections 14 to 16, [BI_TUTORIAL.md](docs/tutorials/10-first-bi-report.md) chapters 10 to 15,
  [API_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), [RUPAKA.md](docs/architecture/RUPAKA.md) section 20.3. Tests: report parser, validator and writer, query building per visual (slicers, cross-filter, highlight, drill, top-N, rows), `BiReportApiTest` over
  a generated lake (three roles), console route tests, and Chromium tests of the viewer, the designer, the cross-pack samples (drill-through, row rules, masks) and the Model view, with the phone and wide-screen sweeps.
- **Added:** **control values and column filters travel with a sent share.** A share now keeps the sender's *view state*: the `ctl.*` control values and the `f.<panel>.<column>` column filters, as query text in a new nullable `view_state` column on `drishti_share` (added by the schema script and, on SQLite, by `IdentityDatabase`; the file store reads old rows as having none; a share's hash is unchanged without one). The share dialog sends it (`view` in `POST /shares`, at most `drishti.collab.share.max-view-state`, default 4000, else `DRS-5001`), `GET /shares/{id}` returns it, the console opens the recipient on the same controls and filters, and the share's watermarked picture (and so the email's attachment) is built with the control values and shows only the rows the column filters leave (a server port of the console's filter grammar, applied after the recipients' masks; a masked cell never matches). There is no scheduled subscription yet, so none stores them. Tests: `ColumnFiltersTest`, `SnapshotApiTest`, `ShareApiContract` (file and database stores), a Chromium test of a filtered view shared and opened by the recipient. RUPAKA.md 8.1, COLLABORATION.md, API_GUIDE.md, CONFIGURATION.md, USER_GUIDE.md.
- **Added:** **the `slider` control.** `kind: slider` with `min`, `max` and `step` (numbers or expressions over the record) and `range: true` for two handles (value `from..to`; one handle is a number); the value is held to the bounds, snapped to the step and ordered. Arrow keys, `PageUp` / `PageDown`, `Home` / `End`, drag or press on the track; `role="slider"` with `aria-valuemin` / `-max` / `-now` / `-valuetext`; 44 px handles on touch; in the workbench Controls tab (Min, Max, Step, *Two handles*) and `RachanaSchema`. The counterparty-risk `netting-set` Sutra gains a *Notional at least* slider on its member trades (generated by `make_sutras.py`). Tests: `ControlsTest`, `SliderValueTest`, `ControlsApiTest`, `test_slider_browser.py` (Chromium, keyboard, drag, address round trip, phone). RACHANA_REFERENCE.md, USER_GUIDE.md, SCREEN_DESIGNER.md, RUPAKA.md.
- **Fixed:** `RedisSourcePluginTest.aLaterLoadOfPartOfADayMergesIntoItsColumnsAndIsPushedToOpenViews` was flaky under load: a load's day announcement refreshes the catalogue on its own thread and `refresh()` returns at once when one is running, so the test could read the columns before the load was visible. It now waits (bounded, 20 s) for the ids it expects; 20 of 20 passes on JDK 21 and on JDK 25.
- **Added:** **a page for every connector and pack.** On `/admin/connectors` and `/admin/packs` the names are links (keyboard reachable) to `/admin/connectors/<name>` and `/admin/packs/<name>`. The connector page: plugin, origin, on/off, status and health, last update, retry state with *Retry now*, **TLS with each certificate's expiry**, kinds served, packs and kinds that use it, settings in effect (credentials masked), the file (read only) and its history, with Edit, Test connection, Compare or restore, Enable/Disable, Reset and Delete as on the list (the editor is shared). The pack page: status (on, off, not loaded, refused with the reason and retry state), extends and needed-by, kinds with mnemonics, a record to open and `FORM <pack>` per kind, Sutras with Open in Build, the connectors it names (missing ones flagged with Create), data-load expectations and the last load, About and glossary counts, deploy history with Roll back. Server: `GET /api/v1/admin/packs/{name}/detail` (new) and a `tls` summary and `retry` on `GET /api/v1/admin/connectors/{name}`. The editor opens by address with `?name=<n>&tab=history|test`. Tests: `test_admin_detail_pages.py` (routes, administrators only), `test_admin_detail_browser.py` (list to detail to the linked pack and connector, keyboard, phone), both pages in the phone sweep of `test_page_widths_browser.py`. Docs: USER_GUIDE, CONNECTOR_FILES, PACKS (new screenshots).
- **Changed:** `tools/realdata` is now the whole pipeline for the `economics` and `politics-society` packs: `realdata.py about | tests | docs` join `fetch | normalise | write`, and `all` from the cached raw data regenerates everything committed byte for byte (dataset, `tools/packgen/*/about.yaml` from `sources.yaml` plus `about/*.kinds.yaml`, packs, Sutra tests from `pack_tests.yaml`, the generated tables of REAL_DATA.md); `check` fails on drift. New `--no-imf` builds World Bank, UNDP and Factbook packs with no IMF forecast, series or mention (the committed packs are untouched). REAL_DATA.md gains a licence note: IMF data is for internal demos only; commercial or client use needs the IMF's permission or a `--no-imf` build. Tests: `tools/realdata/tests/test_pipeline.py`.
- **Added:** **Rūpaka phase 2: datasets and the query engine.** A *dataset* (YAML: tables from pack kinds by connector logical name across packs, columns with paths and types, calculated columns, relationships, measures in Rachana-EL extended with aggregation
  (`sum avg min max count distinct ratio percentile`, conditions, `[Measure]` references), hierarchies, row rules per role, masked columns, rollups) is parsed, validated (`DRS-9010` to `9013`) and queried: `POST /api/v1/bi/datasets/{id}/query` compiles a visual query to one **parameterised** SQL
  statement (names checked and quoted, every value bound, joins many-to-one from the measures' table, row rules the reader cannot remove, masks applied before aggregation) and runs it on an embedded DuckDB behind an `AnalyticEngine` interface. **Layers:** a typed Parquet **columns layer** per table per
  business date in the BI store (`drishti.bi.query.store-dir`), rebuilt for exactly the date a data-load signal names, and **rollups** with aggregate-aware routing (rollup, else columns, else the source); `direct` tables pushed into a SQL store; a minimal `live` snapshot. Answers as masked **Arrow IPC** or JSON, a
  result cache keyed by dataset version, query, the reader's rules and masks and the data generation, limits for time, rows, bytes and concurrency (`DRS-9020` to `9027`). **Connector SPI:** optional `scan(ScanRequest)` (column batches) and `sqlTarget()`; Delta, DuckDB, PostgreSQL (table mode) and file scan
  over their promoted columns, DuckDB and PostgreSQL expose a SQL target (`ColumnScans`). **Model from packs:** kinds become tables, the packs' link graph becomes relationships across packs, relationships are proposed by value matching with the match rate (`/bi/model/start|infer`). **Pages:** Datasets list, Start from packs,
  a dataset's model page with a query preview grid. **Three sample datasets** in `config/bi/datasets` (`trades-basic`, `trading-risk`, `trading-cashflows`; `sample: true`, hidden from those who may not see sample content, with a new `PackAccess.samplesVisible`) and a tutorial. The phase 0 query endpoint runs on the engine; the console serves static files
  compressed (gzip, Brotli when installed). **Not built** (marked everywhere): cron refresh schedule, time intelligence and window functions, Python measures, several unrelated fact tables in one query, incrementally updated live tables, the BI store as a connector. Numbers: [RUPAKA_POC.md](docs/architecture/RUPAKA_POC.md) section 11. Docs:
  [DATASETS.md](docs/guides/DATASETS.md), [BI_TUTORIAL.md](docs/tutorials/10-first-bi-report.md), [BI_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md) section 13, [API_GUIDE.md](docs/guides/API_GUIDE.md), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), [CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTOR_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md), [RUPAKA.md](docs/architecture/RUPAKA.md) 20.2.
  Tests: dataset parser and validator, expression compiler and engine (injection, three readers, routing, caching, limits, direct and live), pack models, the scan helper, `DatasetApiTest` over a generated lake, console routes, one browser test, static compression.
- **Changed:** the `economics` and `politics-society` packs carry **real public data**, not invented samples, and are no longer `sample: true`. A new repeatable tool, `tools/realdata/` (`realdata.py fetch | normalise | write | all | check`, stdlib only, config in `sources.yaml`), downloads the World Bank WDI and Worldwide Governance Indicators (CC BY 4.0), the IMF World Economic Outlook (DataMapper, reuse with attribution), UNDP's Human Development Index (CC BY 3.0 IGO) and the CIA World Factbook (public domain, via the CC0 factbook.json mirror) into a cache outside the repository, normalises them to a 2.4 MB committed dataset and regenerates both packs through their generators (`--check` clean). Economics: 213 economies, 3,119 country indicators with history and rank, 192 IMF forecasts to 2031, 185 labour markets, 192 fiscal positions, 12 World Bank country groups; politics-society: 216 jurisdictions with seats by party, 430 office holders, 378 elections, 282 parties, 7 world regions, 4,297 social and governance indicators. Every document shows its source, dataset, retrieval date and licence in a *Data source and licence* panel (and in `_meta`). The kinds with no open global data (central-bank decisions, trade flows, price baskets, polls, bills, candidates) are removed; the FORM, About glossary (definitions and units for every indicator) and pack tests are regenerated for the new kinds. `packbuild` gains `compact_json` (unindented sample documents) and `form_overrides` (FORM field types and labels). The packs and the dataset are 21 MB of text. Guide: [REAL_DATA.md](docs/guides/REAL_DATA.md); attribution in [THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md](THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md). Tests: `tools/realdata/tests` (14, offline, recorded fixtures), `pack check` on both packs, `DomainPacksTest`, `AboutPacksTest`.
- **Added:** **tabs hold panels** (Rachana). A `tabs` panel takes `panels:`, a list of panels (`kv`, `status`, `table`, `ladder`, `hbar`, `markdown`, `metric`) shown inside each tab and read
  with `@` the tab's element, so a tab per leg, whatever the count, can hold the leg's terms and a table whose rows are `@.cashflows` and whose tree children are `@.fixings`: a
  trade, its legs, their cashflows and their fixings on one screen. Checks: `DRS-2010` (a panel in a tab needs an `id`), `DRS-2023` (a kind a tab cannot hold, tabs in tabs, a Pivot tab,
  `panels` on another kind), `DRS-2024`, `DRS-2030`. Parser, writer, JSON schema for the workbench, server pipeline, masking, snapshot, CSV export (Tab, Panel, Row, Field, Value),
  console macros and the element stylesheet. Live patches repaint the whole tabs panel: the selected tab and the opened tree rows stay. Docs: RACHANA_REFERENCE ("Panels inside a tab"),
  PANEL_KINDS, USER_GUIDE ("A deep trade"), PACK_DEVELOPER_GUIDE ("Modelling deep documents"), INFERENCE.
- **Added:** inference lays out a short list of records that hold lists of their own (legs with cashflows, cashflows with fixings) as tabs, one per record, each with a kv of the
  record's fields and a table per list, a tree when those rows hold lists again (a second `LegsRule`, 0.92). The reference swap IRS-48213's inferred layout now has its cashflows in the
  leg tabs instead of a separate table of the first leg's.
- **Changed:** the trading pack's samples are deep: interest-rate swaps (all the swap family) have `fixings[]` on their floating cashflows and named their calculation-period fields
  `periodStart`, `periodEnd`, `accrualFactor`, `discountFactor` (were `accrualStart`, `accrualEnd`, `yearFraction`, `df`) and the leg's `businessDayConvention` (was `businessDay`); the basis
  swap has two floating legs, the cross-currency swap two floating legs in two currencies with initial and final exchange of notional as cashflows, the cap and floor one leg of
  caplets or floorlets each with its fixing, the European swaption its option terms (`exercise`) and the underlying swap's legs, and the commodity swap three or four legs (fixed price, index
  average with daily fixings, fee, sometimes a location differential). MTM of those products is now the sum of their leg PVs, so the netting-set, book and exposure samples that add trade MTM
  changed with them. `tools/packgen/banking/data_deep.py`; `make_data.py`, `make_sutras.py`, `make_packs.py`, `make_docs.py` `--check` clean; the trading pack's Sutras show a tab per leg.
- **Added:** the demo source's `_meta.walk` takes a path into the document (`legs.1.cashflows.0.fixings.3.rate`), so a nested cell ticks; the deep samples walk one projected fixing.
- **Fixed:** after a live update the tabs of a panel did not switch any more (`root.getElementById is not a function`), and a tree table lost the rows the reader had opened.
- **Tests:** `NestedTabsTest`, `DeepTradeViewTest`, `DemoTickerPathTest`, the inference test for deep documents, `test_export.py`, and `test_deep_trade_browser.py` (Chromium: three and four tabs,
  a cashflow opening to its fixings, a live tick of a nested cell, opened rows surviving a tick, a phone at 390 px).

## 1.19.0 — Connectors as files, TLS everywhere, query by form, Elements, no restarts, BI begins (2026-10-10)
- **Added:** the `BI` command keyword, working like `FORM`: `BI <GO>` the BI home, `BI WORKSPACE`, `BI CATALOGUE`, `BI <report-or-board>` by id or title (several matches open the catalogue searched), type-ahead after `BI ` offering the items the user may open; `RUPAKA` stays as an alias. `BI` is a reserved word beside `RUPAKA` and `FORM`: the pack loader and `drishti pack check` refuse a pack mnemonic named `BI` (no shipped pack uses it). Docs: BI_GUIDE, USER_GUIDE, PACK_DEVELOPER_GUIDE, RUPAKA.md section 4.
- **Changed:** query by form: an enum field with more than 6 values (asset class, product type, book, desk, currency) is one **multi-select dropdown** the size of a select
  instead of a wall of tick boxes: a summary ("All", "REPO", "3 selected: ABS, REPO, +1"), a search box, *Select all* / *Clear*, keyboard, 44 px rows on touch; up to 6 values stay
  a checkbox row; the address encoding is unchanged. One shared component, `static/js/multiselect.js` ([DEVELOPER_GUIDE 5.8a](docs/guides/DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md)), also draws the
  `multi-select` controls of Sutras and works inside `<drishti-view>`. Tests: `test_form_browser.py`.
- **Added:** **controls on a screen** (Rūpaka phase 0a, [RUPAKA.md 8.1](docs/architecture/RUPAKA.md#81-interactive-sutras-and-slicers-controls-view-state-and-filters)
  "as built"). A Sutra declares `controls:` (dropdown, multi-select, cascading, toggle, segmented, date, date-range, number-range, top-n, search; options from a list,
  an expression over the record such as `$.legs[*].id`, or an entity kind; `default`, `remember`, `required`, `placement` strip / header / side) and panels read the
  values as `ctl.<id>` in `rows`, `where`, `limit` and `title`. Rachana-EL gained `ctl.<id>`, the projection `[*]` and the functions `within` and `oneOf`; panels gained
  `where`, a `limit` that reads a control, and `on-select: { by, field, filter }` (a click or Enter on a row or bar sets a control: cross-filtering); problem codes
  `DRS-2034` to `DRS-2039`. The server evaluates with the request's `ctl.*` values (view and stream endpoints), builds only `&panels=a,b` when asked, reads options from the
  record as the caller may see it (a masked value is never listed and never matches) and builds live frames with the values the stream was opened with. Console: one script,
  `controls.js`, draws every kind (keyboard, 44 px on touch, both themes), asks `GET /v/{kind}/{id}/panels` for only the panels that read the control (whole view 17.0 ms and
  12.8 KB, one panel 10.1 ms and 5.5 KB on a warm local server), keeps the values in the address (`?ctl.legs=2&ctl.when=2026-10-01..2026-12-31`) and the Share link, moves the live
  subscription, and works inside `<drishti-view>` (a `controls` attribute and property, a composed `drishti:controls` event, `GET /embed/v1/views/{kind}/{id}/panels`; Chromium,
  Firefox, WebKit). The workbench has a **Controls** tab (cards, YAML two ways, one `setControls` operation, the real controls over the previewed sample). Shipped on
  `irs-fixfloat` (cashflows by leg and date range) and `netting-set` (member trades by asset class, product and how many, the asset-class bars selecting). `remember: user` is
  per browser (the server has no per-user view-preference store); not built: a `slider` kind, options from a dataset column, a view-level `title:` reading a control, the Pivot tab
  and CSV following the controls, control values stored with a *sent* share, picture or subscription (a copied link carries them). Docs:
  [RACHANA_REFERENCE](docs/guides/RACHANA_REFERENCE.md#controls), [USER_GUIDE](docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md#controls-on-a-screen-choose-what-a-panel-shows),
  [SCREEN_DESIGNER](docs/guides/SCREEN_DESIGNER.md#the-controls-tab), [ELEMENTS](docs/architecture/ELEMENTS.md). Tests: `ControlsTest`, `ControlsApiTest`, `ControlsLiveTest`,
  `test_controls_routes.py`, `test_controls_browser.py`, `test_workbench_controls_browser.py`, `test_embed_controls_browser.py`.
- **Added:** **Rūpaka phase 1: the BI shell and catalogue** ([BI guide](docs/guides/BI_GUIDE.md)). A **BI** top-bar menu for people with the new `bi` power and the reserved `RUPAKA` keyword (`RUPAKA <GO>`, `RUPAKA WORKSPACE`, `RUPAKA CATALOGUE`, `RUPAKA <report>`; any case; type-ahead); packs and `pack check` refuse a mnemonic named `RUPAKA` or `FORM`. A `/bi` page with its own layout (rail, Home, Catalogue, Workspaces, items; keys `/`, `g h`, `g c`, `g w`, `?`; phone and every theme). Personal and shared workspaces (viewer, contributor, member, admin), typed items (dataset, report, board, notebook) with a stored, versioned, **not executed** YAML body, draft, proposed, published with restore, review reusing the `approve` power and four-eyes, promoted and certified endorsement, audiences of roles, users and packs enforced on the server, favourites, recents and views. Storage in the identity database; shipped read-only files in `drishti.bi.dir` (`config/bi`), **watched live with no restart** (`drishti.bi.watch`: auto, poll, off; a bad file keeps its last good version and shows in Admin, BI settings and on Health). Powers `bi`, `bi-author`, `bi-steward`, `bi-admin` on roles (`roles.<role>.bi`, `bi` on the role API); Admin, BI settings (powers per role, workspace creation policy); API `/api/v1/bi/**` with token scopes `bi:write`, `bi:approve`, `bi:admin`; every change audited; DRS-9001 to DRS-9006; `drishti.py bi workspace|catalogue list|get|publish`. Datasets, reports and the designer are not built yet (phases 2 and 3); the Phase 0 proof of concept stays for administrators under the BI menu (`bi.poc_enabled`). Settings: `drishti.bi.*`, console `bi.enabled` (`DRISHTI_BI_ENABLED`).
- **Added:** **retry with backoff, and nothing needs a restart.** Anything that failed to load or lost its connection is tried again by itself, so it works as soon as the cause is fixed. One mechanism (`RetryPolicy`, `RetryTracker`, `RetryBoard` in `drishti-common`; `core/retry.py` in the console): exponential backoff with jitter (`drishti.retry.*`: `initial` 5s, `factor` 2, `max` 5m, `jitter` 0.2, `permanent-after` 3, `enabled`, with per-area overrides `drishti.retry.packs|connectors|ai|mcp.*`; console `retry.*`). **Packs** refused (a clash, a missing `extends`, a bad file) are re-evaluated when the packs folder, the connector files, the sample-pack switch or the overlay change, and on the schedule: a pack loads after its conflict is removed with no change in its own folder. **Connectors** that fail to start, or whose plugin reports `DOWN`, are retried (started afresh after a few looks) and serve again as soon as the source is up. **AI providers** that cannot be built and **MCP servers** that cannot be set up or lost their connection are rebuilt / reconnected; the console's own AI and MCP layer now also reloads its files without a restart (`ai.poll_seconds`) and retries the same way. Errors retrying cannot fix are classed permanent after `permanent-after` repeats and slowed to `max`. State is visible: Admin -> Packs, Connectors, AI and Health show "failed - next retry in Ns (attempt k)" and the last error; `GET /api/v1/admin/retry`. **Retry now** (`POST /api/v1/admin/retry/{packs|connectors|ai|mcp|all}`, admin, audited, token scope `packs:admin`). Failures are logged and audited on attempts 1, 2, 4, 8, ... and every recovery always (`pack|connector|ai|mcp-retry-failed`, `-recovered`, `-retry-now`). See [OPERATIONS.md](docs/admin/OPERATIONS.md#retries-what-failed-is-tried-again-by-itself), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md#retry-settings).
- **Changed:** **no setting a pack contributes needs a restart any more.** Roles, the link graph (reference patterns, fields, badges, impact), search columns and pivots, formats, semantic hints, About pages and guides, kind routes and the demo connector's sample folders are now swapped in live by the pack reloader (`LiveSettings`: each is built from the new packs first, a refusal changes nothing, then all are swapped in one reload at a time and the cached view layouts are dropped); `restartFor` in the answer is always empty. The console's help library is rebuilt when a pack's version or `help.yaml` changes. OPERATIONS.md has a table of every artifact type, how a change is noticed and what a bad edit does ("What changes live (no restart)"). The AI registry no longer logs a line at every look at the folder.
- **Changed:** the holiday calendars (CAL-USNY, GBLO, EUTA, JPTO) name every holiday (New Year's Day, Martin Luther King Jr. Day, Good Friday, Thanksgiving Day, ... and "(observed)" for a weekend substitute) instead of "Public holiday". Each holiday line is `{date, name, weekday, year, observed}` for 2026 and 2027; the `calendar` Sutra shows Holidays 2026 and 2027, the next holiday and its date, and a table of Date, Day, Holiday and Observed with every line on one page. `BusinessCalendar` (Java) has `holidayNames(year)`; a test pins the Java and Python calendars to the same dates (Python Boxing Day now follows the Java substitute rule).
- **Added:** **query by form** (`FORM <pack> <GO>`; [User guide](docs/guides/USER_GUIDE.md#search-with-a-form-form-pack)). A page of typed inputs for a kind (text with equals/contains/starts with/regex and a case toggle; number and amount with `=`, between, `>`, `<` and `10m`/`1bn`; date on/between/before/after or a business-day range such as `last 5 business days`; enum multi-select of the values in the data; one-or-many ids pasted as a list; yes/no) that the server compiles to the existing search grammar and runs as any search (masks, kinds per role, packs per user and sample visibility apply). The generated command is shown and copyable, the results are the console table with column filters, the key cell opens the view and Alt+Left returns to the form with the criteria (they are in the URL), and the search can be saved by name (`savedSearches` setting), watched as a monitor, scheduled as a report or shared as a link. `FORM` is a reserved command word with type-ahead for the packs and kinds you may open. API: `GET /api/v1/forms/{pack}`, `POST /api/v1/forms/{pack}/{kind}/compile`.
- **Added:** **hide the form** on `FORM <pack>`: a Hide form / Show form button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) and Alt+F fold the form to one line of its criteria (click it to reopen); after Search the form folds when results arrive (`form.collapse_on_submit` in `config/application.yaml`, default true), criteria kept; the state is in the address (`&form=hidden|shown`) and a hand-opened form stays open for the tab. [User guide](docs/guides/QUERY_BY_FORM.md#give-the-results-the-room-hide-the-form).
- **Added:** the pack.yaml `form:` section ([Pack developer guide](docs/guides/PACK_DEVELOPER_GUIDE.md#form-the-search-form-of-a-kind)): per kind, fields with `path`, `label`, `type` (text, number, amount, date, enum, id, bool), `operators`, `default`, `order`, `group`, `help`, `required`, `hidden`, `values`. A kind with none gets a form inferred from its search columns, Sutra match and strip fields and sample values, labelled from `about.yaml`. Every shipped pack declares forms (the generators write them, `--check` clean; `finance` and `logistics` by hand), `pack check` validates them (`DRS-2060` in the workbench), and `pack make` and New pack write one from samples and schemas.
- **Added:** **regular expressions in search, on a linear-time engine** (`SafeRegex`, written in-tree because the build is offline; RE2/J would be a drop-in). `matches(field, 'pattern')`, `matchesCase(field, 'pattern')` and `field matches 'pattern'` in any search and in Rachana-EL; back-references, look-around, atomic groups, possessive quantifiers and `\b` are refused with `DRS-4010`, as are patterns over 200 characters or 4,000 expanded steps; a value is cut to 8,192 characters and a match over 50 ms is `DRS-4011` (`drishti.search.regex.*`). A backslash in a quoted search string is now kept.
- **Added:** the workbench **Form tab** ([Screen designer](docs/guides/SCREEN_DESIGNER.md#the-form-tab)): the field palette of the samples, a card for each form field (type, operators, label, default, group, help, required, hidden), reordering by drag and by Alt+Up/Down, the YAML in both directions, a live preview of the form, the form kept as a step of the design's log (undo, redo, revisions), and a form change that travels with the proposal (reviewers see before and after) and is in force once approved.
- **Added:** **pluggable LLM providers and an MCP client** ([AI_PROVIDERS.md](docs/architecture/AI_PROVIDERS.md), [MCP_CLIENT.md](docs/architecture/MCP_CLIENT.md)), off by default (`drishti.ai.enabled`, `drishti.mcp.enabled`). New module `drishti-ai` (no controllers; depends only on `drishti-api` and `drishti-common`; ArchUnit rule): Drishti's own interfaces `LlmProvider`, `LlmModel`, `EmbeddingModel`, `LlmRegistry`, `LlmPolicy` / `LlmGateway`, `ProviderFactory` (ServiceLoader or Spring bean: a new provider is one class and one registration). Providers are files, one YAML per provider in `config/ai/<name>.yaml` (`drishti.ai.dir`, `DRISHTI_AI_DIR`; type, `base-url`, `api-key: ${ENV}` only (a literal key is refused), models with logical names, timeouts, `tls.*`; live reload like connectors). Implementations: `openai` through Spring AI 1.0.3 (`spring-ai-starter-model-openai`, BOM in dependencyManagement, Java 17+ bytecode so the enforcer rule `maxJdkVersion 21` passes; any OpenAI-compatible endpoint incl. Azure, vLLM, Ollama `/v1`), `anthropic` (the existing Ask provider ported; Spring AI's Anthropic starter is not used, to keep one HTTP stack and the shared TLS), and `mock` (deterministic, no network: fixed, echo, rules, tool calls, latency, errors, usage). Policy: masking with `drishti.security.redact` and extra patterns, audit row per call (action `ai`, never the text), per-user/per-feature rate limits, daily cost limit, features per role. MCP client: `McpClient`, `McpRegistry`, `McpGateway` (roles/features per server and tool, masked arguments and results, audit), Streamable HTTP and mock transports, one YAML per server in `config/mcp/<name>.yaml` (`drishti.mcp.dir`, headers as `${ENV}`, `allowed-tools`); tested live against the SAJHA MCP server (`config/mcp/sajha.yaml.example`). REST: `GET /api/v1/admin/ai`, `GET /api/v1/admin/mcp/{server}/tools`, `POST /api/v1/admin/ai/test` (scope `ai:admin`, audited), `GET /api/v1/ai/models`, `POST /api/v1/ai/chat`, `POST /api/v1/ai/mcp/{server}/tools/{tool}` (scope `ai:use`); DRS-4015..4014. Console: `core/ai/` mirrors it with `typing.Protocol`s (providers `openai`, `anthropic`, `server` (recommended: keys stay on the server), `mock`; MCP `streamable-http`, `server`, `mock`), Admin -> AI (read-only), `POST /api/ai/chat`. **Ask now runs on the AI layer**; its `endpoint`/`api`/`api-key` settings still work as provider `ask` with a deprecation warning.
- **Changed:** **the product owner's layout.** The packs moved from `./packs` to `./config/packs` (`drishti.packs.dir`, `DRISHTI_PACKS_DIR`; the console's default is `../config/packs`), beside `config/connectors/` and the new `config/bi/` (`drishti.bi.dir`, `DRISHTI_BI_DIR`: `datasets/`, `reports/`, `python/` and a README; Rupaka content lives only there, never inside a pack). A new umbrella `drishti.data.dir` (`DRISHTI_DATA_DIR`, default `./data`) is the base of every runtime path (identity database, caches, loads, collab, designs, reports, governance, the pack overlay, installed packs, settings and samples-mode, the lake roots the packs suggest, and the plugins' and command-line tools' defaults); each path's own setting still overrides. For one release a missing packs folder falls back to `./packs` (the console to `../packs`) with a warning. Docs, tests, tools, Dockerfiles and CI follow ([CONFIGURATION.md](docs/admin/CONFIGURATION.md), "The data folder and the config folder").
- **Changed:** **generated packs name their connectors.** `tools/schemapack.py`, Build → New pack (`core/schemakit`) and `pack new --lake` write `connectors: [<pack>-store]`, the `routes`, and the connection settings as `connector-templates`, as [CONNECTOR_FILES.md](docs/connectors/CONNECTOR_FILES.md) describes, instead of an inline definition; `pack bundle`, `pack diff`, `data ingest` and the lake tools read both forms.
- **Added:** **live pack reload** (no restart). The packs folder is watched (`drishti.packs.watch`: auto | poll | off, with `poll` and `reload-debounce`): a new pack folder is checked with the loaded packs and loaded (kinds, mnemonics, Sutras, connector templates become connector files by the usual rule); an edited `pack.yaml`, `about.yaml`, Sutra or sample is re-checked and swapped in; a deleted pack is unloaded, the connectors only it used stop and open views of its kinds say "pack removed". A bad edit keeps the last good version and the problem shows in Admin -> Packs and Health; a pack another loaded pack extends is never unloaded; bursts are debounced, one reload runs at a time, every change is audited. Admin -> Packs Load and Unload, archive deploy, registry install and rollback now use this path instead of restarting the server (`restarting` is always `false`; settings bound at start, such as roles, graph, search columns, formats and About pages, are reported in `restartFor`). See [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#loading-a-pack-while-the-server-runs).
- **Added:** **sample packs.** `sample: true` in `pack.yaml` (set on genomics, politics-society, logistics and economics) and `drishti.packs.samples` = `visible` (default) | `developers` (only authors and administrators see them; others get them filtered on the server as a pack switched off) | `hidden` (not loaded, their connectors do not start). Admin -> Packs shows a Sample badge and "Hide samples from business users", a saved setting that overrides the property, applied at once and audited (`pack-samples`); `GET`/`PUT /api/v1/admin/packs/samples`. See [PACKS.md](docs/guides/PACKS.md#sample-packs).
- **Fixed:** `POST /api/v1/admin/packs/deploy/{id}/connectors` is declared in the `packs:admin` token scope; the connector-files guide states that a connector's `kinds:` adds the kinds it serves to the routed ones, and that a shared connector folder lets a file left by another run merge into a connector defined in the server configuration; `RecentStoreApiTest` uses its own folder.
- **Added:** **Excel-style column filters on every table, matrix and pivot** (RUPAKA.md 8.1, delivery 1; no grammar change). A funnel in each column heading (hover or focus on a desktop, always shown and 44 px on touch) opens a menu: sort (A→Z, smallest→largest, oldest→newest), a search box over the column's distinct values with counts, *(Select all)* and *(Blanks)*, and conditions by the detected type: numbers (equals, does not equal, greater, less, between, top / bottom N, above / below average), dates (today, yesterday, this / last week and month, last 7 / 30 days, between, before, after) and text (equals, does not equal, contains, does not contain, begins / ends with); OK, Cancel, Clear. Filters combine across columns and with the *Filter rows* box; the funnel fills and its tooltip says what it does; the pager reads `12 of 840 rows (filtered)`, a *Clear filters* button and `Alt+Shift+X` clear them, and the counts are announced politely. They survive live patches (rows leave or enter), work with paging and zoom fit, and are part of the address (`?f.<panel>.<column>=in:a,b`, `gt:1m`, `bt:1~9`, `top:10`, `today` ...) so reload, share and snapshot reproduce them; remembering per user is off. Masked values are one *(masked)* entry and never match a condition; in a matrix a parent stays, open, when a row below it matches. Keyboard: Enter or Space opens the menu, arrows, Space and type-ahead in the list, Esc closes only the menu. Also inside `<drishti-view>` (`colfilter` joins the enhancers; the element sheet gains `colfilter.css`). New `static/js/colfilter.js`, `static/css/colfilter.css`; every table event `drishti:column-filter` is the hook for a server-paged table (none exists yet). USER_GUIDE "Filter a table like Excel" with pictures (`tools/docs/screenshots.py --guide colfilter`); tests `test_colfilter_browser.py` (Chromium, phone) and an element test in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- **Changed:** a table's footer with a filter on reads `n of N rows (filtered)` (it read `a–b of n (filtered from N)`); sorting always puts blank cells last, ascending or descending; in a matrix a parent stays open when any row below it matches the filter box (it checked only its first child).
- **Added:** **TLS for the rest of the connectors** with the same shared `tls.*` settings ([TLS.md](docs/connectors/TLS.md#connector-matrix)). Redis (`rediss://`, a private CA, mutual TLS; the Lettuce `verifyPeer` URI option still works, deprecated, with a warning), REST and the feeds' mirrors (HTTPS with a private CA or mutual TLS, the new `TlsHttp` helper in the plugin API), S3 (a private CA or client certificate for MinIO, Ceph or a gateway), MongoDB (TLS and `auth-mechanism: x509` certificate log-in), PostgreSQL through the JDBC connector (the module's context handed to the driver, `sslmode=verify-full`, certificate log-in with `pg_hba` `cert`; the other drivers keep their own URL options, documented), Aerospike (TLS policy, `tls-name`, `auth-mode: pki`), Delta (the native engine's S3 client) and Iceberg (S3FileIO for REST catalogs). Each checks at start (a wrong file or password, or `tls.*` beside an address that is not TLS, stops the connector naming the setting and the file), adds a certificate's expiry to the health text, and has a Security section with complete connector files, verification commands and real error messages. Integration tests run against real servers in Docker (Redis 8, MongoDB 7 with x.509, PostgreSQL 18) and in-process HTTPS servers; Aerospike's live check is a documented manual procedure (its TLS is Enterprise Edition). Not covered because the libraries have no hook: a private CA on Hadoop's S3A (`engine: hadoop`, `catalog: hadoop`) and the Iceberg REST catalog's own connection; both are refused or warned about at start and point to the JVM truststore.
- **Changed:** **the connector form's `tls.*` section is generated from the code that reads the settings** (`TlsSettings.settingSpecs`), with the real names, types, secret flags and help text (`tls.ca-file`, `tls.truststore`, `tls.cert-file`, ... instead of the earlier guessed `tls.truststore.path`); a test fails if the two ever differ. The Kafka settings (`flavour`, `security.protocol`, `sasl.*`, `schema-registry.*`, `schema-registry.tls.*`) are in the catalogue, so Admin -> Connectors validates and offers them. A `-file` twin (`tls.keystore-password-file`) holds a path and is no longer taken for a secret; a `${KEY}:${SECRET}` pair of references is accepted as a secret value. New secure examples in `config/connectors.examples/other/` (Kafka mutual TLS with PEM, Confluent Cloud, RabbitMQ `amqps://` with certificate log-in, ActiveMQ `ssl://`, PostgreSQL, Redis, MongoDB x.509, REST mutual TLS, S3, Delta, Iceberg, Aerospike).
